## Additional finding: matching subagent-specific literals in the installed CLI

The embedded arm64 Mach-O string table contains exact subagent-related error literals also present in the peeled `rust-v0.162.0-alpha.17.2` source: `Agent depth limit reached. Solve the task yourself.`, `spawn_agent could not resolve the child model for service tier validation`, and `spawn_agent could not resolve the child model for reasoning effort validation`. The first occurs in the V1 spawn and resume handlers; the latter two are in child configuration validation. This provides another content-level cross-check that the public tag is relevant to the installed binary. A string match does not prove that the whole binary was built from this commit, nor that any matched branch ran in the desktop session. The binary identity remains the SHA-256 and Mach-O UUID above; no runtime action was taken for this check.

## Additional finding: composer Stop callback reaches the conversation manager

Static tracing of the installed app bundle resolves the regular composer Stop path across three hashed resources:

| ASAR resource | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- |
| `webview/assets/app-primary-6f60e9ac4103.js` | `7fe9c00b03cb1de3ebc3a4bf298fc44b13a7fe67ca9cc335a9e2ecda969a1321` |
| `webview/assets/app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` | `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f` |
| `webview/assets/app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` | `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245` |

In `app-primary`, the submit-button component renders a Stop button when `submitButtonMode` is `stop`; its click callback invokes the supplied `onStop`. The Codex composer adapter wraps and passes that callback, and the session component supplies its stop handler. For a local composer, the handler uses the current resolved host when no explicit conversation ID is supplied; when an ID is supplied, it looks up that conversation's host in the app store. It passes both `conversationId` and `hostId` to `yda`. `yda` uses an injected client when present, otherwise resolves the conversation manager from the explicit host ID or the store's host mapping, then calls `interruptConversation(conversationId, "user-stop", expectedTurnId)`. The static callback chain is therefore composer Stop -> `onStop` -> Codex composer adapter -> session stop handler -> `yda` -> host manager `interruptConversation`.

This is not the `beforeArchiveConversation` callback and it does not call `stopThread`. The archive path may converge internally only when `stopThread` falls back from durable `thread/stop` to `interruptConversation`. The selected manager's `user-stop` path can schedule subagent-descendant interruption in the background; this is best-effort cleanup, and the interrupt result or expected-turn guard can affect whether the cascade starts. This is a bundle-level static call graph: no button was clicked and no runtime routing was observed. The Stop control is rendered only when the composer state selects the `stop` submission mode. Bundle byte offsets and excerpts are retained in the analysis notes; offsets are approximate byte offsets within the extracted JS files.

The same `app-primary` component computes that mode as `Yo != null || (!Hs && $t && (Oe || !qo)) ? "stop" : "submit"`. Nearby props identify `Hs` as the `thread-goal-continuation` block reason, `$t` as `isResponseInProgress`, and `qo` as the content flag passed to submit-block evaluation. `Yo` is also passed as `workingConversationId` (falling back to the current conversation ID under a separate visibility condition). Import mapping resolves `Sw` to the conversation-scoped `eYt` state cell exported by `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js`; it defaults to null. A selector in that module checks the stored message ID against the conversation message tree, while adjacent persistence code uses the explicit key `chatgpt-image-generation-stops-v1` and reads/writes stop records scoped by conversation and account. This identifies the state as an image-generation stop marker, not subagent-specific state. Other bundle consumers use it to block user-message submission, fail page generation with `unavailable`, and return early from image-edit actions; composer async results also use it as an invalidation condition. The code shows a shared cancellation/stop gate, but the write callsite relevant to this Stop UI remains unresolved, so static evidence does not establish when it is set. Thus a non-null working conversation ID selects Stop directly; otherwise the response-in-progress, goal-continuation, this image-generation stop marker, and content conditions govern it. This establishes UI gating, not a runtime observation. The expression is around byte offset 1,382,610; the resulting mode is passed into the composer around 1,402,642. In `app-initial`, the state declaration is around byte offset 1,506,066, persistence key around 1,509,362, and the message-tree selector around 1,535,696.

The inspected callsites, as byte offsets in the extracted resources, were: `app-primary` Stop-button branch around 861,747 and click callback around 869,779; adapter `onStop` input around 1,365,164, wrapper around 1,379,045, composer prop around 1,402,642, and session handler wiring around 1,425,623; handler path around 1,420,466–1,420,619; `app-initial` `yda` implementation around 5,453,540; `app-shared` facade around 2,540,264 and manager implementation around 3,239,876. They are navigation aids for these exact hashed files, not stable source line numbers.

## Additional finding: distinct standalone and desktop CLI identities

On 2026-10-13, `type -a codex` resolved the user's PATH-first command to `/Users/joey/.local/bin/codex`, a symlink into the standalone release package. That binary reports `codex-cli 0.162.1`, is arm64, and has SHA-256 `74c6a6d263f40d65f99c27a815275f5b4ed0bcc2baecc396b4cf3255498bdfc0`. The separately inspected ChatGPT.app embedded binary reports `codex-cli 0.162.0-alpha.17.2`, has SHA-256 `0d5ac7aae20c4eda6242428d5e218c55ba96d8763fda2a271088ff37104b9d28`, and was the executable for the observed desktop process. That process was running `exec-server --remote` against the Codex cloud-environments endpoint; this establishes the process mode, not what code ran on the remote host. Do not treat standalone CLI behavior as a desktop-host probe.

The standalone version's public annotated tag `rust-v0.162.1` peels to commit [`092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d). This is a separate source comparison target, not build provenance for the standalone binary. Comparing this commit with the earlier embedded-version source snapshot shows that agent-tree shutdown exposes a bounded structured report: failures carry an operation, optional thread identity, and either a stable error category plus phase or `GuardAbandoned`; at most 64 failures are retained, with further failures counted. The controller records failures in child-spawn cleanup, including stopping a cancelled child, discarding its persistence, and closing its spawn edge; residency shutdown also records stop failures. The report becomes observable after tracked tree operations drain. This is static source evidence. No shutdown failure was induced, and no live spawn was run against standalone CLI 0.162.1. The [public 0.162.1 release notes](https://github.com/openai/codex/releases/tag/rust-v0.162.1) identify TUI asynchronous-question and startup feature-setting fixes, so the subagent lifecycle comparison here is a source-tree delta, not a claim that these changes were called out as release-note features.

The source-tree contrast also shows a fork-history filtering difference in `core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs`: the earlier snapshot excludes `AdditionalTools` unconditionally, while 0.162.1 retains those declarations only when the child also preserves the parent's context baselines, including when filtering compacted replacement histories. The 0.162.1 source comment states that tool declarations and their comparison baseline must survive or be rebuilt together. This is paired context-preservation logic, not evidence that arbitrary parent tools are copied into every child. The V2 spawn path also captures the child's startup config and resolves its default reasoning effort before later child metadata can replace it, then uses that snapshot for spawn telemetry; this is measurement timing, not a second config override applied after startup. Sources: [`fork item filter`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L88-L130), [`compacted-history filtering`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L1137-L1178), and [startup snapshot used for spawn telemetry](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L904-L998).

The same tag's `spawn_agent_fork_strips_parent_usage_hints_from_compacted_history` source test asserts that a full-history child retains the `AdditionalTools` declaration embedded in a compaction replacement history, keeps the compacted non-hint summary, strips parent-local approval and stale parent guidance, and installs the child subagent hint. A separate full-history fork test checks that the tool catalog declaration and its `WorldState` comparison baseline are both present in the child. This is direct evidence of test intent and assertions in the source, not a test run or proof of the installed desktop behavior. Sources: [compacted-history test](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L2908-L2938), [child history and retention assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L3098-L3138), and [tool declarations with catalog baseline](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L2794-L2822).

Interpret version diffs here as source-snapshot contrasts. The local `rust-v0.162.1` checkout was shallow when inspected, and the parent commits needed to establish ancestry were not available; this comparison does not establish a linear release transition or the commit that introduced these changes. Both public tags remain useful for independently inspecting their source trees.

### Shutdown request API and ownership boundary (0.162.1 source)

The new public `ThreadManager::request_agent_tree_shutdown(thread_id)` first resolves a **loaded** thread, then closes the exact local runtime's `TaskTracker` admission fence, cancels its shared tree token, closes its mailboxes, and returns an `AgentTreeShutdown` handle. `wait()` and `wait_detailed()` wait until all members admitted before the fence have drained. `admit_start()` checks the closed fence both before and after acquiring a tracker token, so a concurrent start is either tracked by the wait or rejected with `RuntimeShutdown`.

The membership sites inspected cover session startup/session loop, fresh child spawn, agent resume, V2 child loading, legacy completion watcher, resident eviction, and some inter-agent delivery operations. Startup cancellation retains membership until it has stopped session I/O or shut down a partially built session and discarded uncommitted persistence. Teardown guards record abandoned cleanup; detailed reports retain at most 64 failures and count the rest. The ordinary `wait()` surface collapses the report into a generic fatal error; `wait_detailed()` exposes the bounded structured report.

The API explicitly leaves registry removal and caller-owned persistence handoff to its caller. It only fences starts that share the current local runtime; top-level loads/resumes of the same thread ID outside that runtime must be serialized separately. Subagent creation passes the parent's same `LocalAgentRuntime` into the child, so targeting a loaded child ID with this API would resolve the shared runtime and fence the root and its same-runtime siblings too; it is not a child-subtree-only handle. This runtime-wide fence is distinct from `close_agent`: the latter marks the target spawn edge closed and walks the target plus live descendants, shutting them down individually. The inspected app-server idle-thread unload is a third path: it calls `CodexThread::shutdown_and_wait()` for one thread, then removes that same runtime with `remove_thread_if_matches`. A read-only search of the 0.162.1 source found no internal production call site for `request_agent_tree_shutdown` beyond its definition. Thus this tag proves a host/SDK-facing shutdown mechanism exists, not that the ChatGPT desktop invokes it for subagents. This is source inspection only; the API and race behavior were not exercised live. Sources: [`thread_manager/shutdown.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager/shutdown.rs#L105-L147), [`agent/control/runtime.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/runtime.rs#L274-L302), [`codex_delegate.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/codex_delegate.rs#L81-L130), [`session/startup.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/session/startup.rs#L32-L105), [`close_agent`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/legacy.rs#L48-L123), and [app-server idle unload](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_lifecycle.rs#L419-L489).

## Additional finding: spawn tool result identity

The V2 `spawn_agent` success result serializes `task_name` and optionally `nickname`; it does not serialize the child `ThreadId`. The handler and started-activity path retain the actual child thread ID separately, and `SubAgentActivityEvent` has both `agent_thread_id` and canonical `agent_path`. The source default hides spawn metadata, which removes the optional nickname from the tool result. This distinguishes the model-facing result from internal activity identity; it does not establish what a particular UI renders. Sources: [`multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs), [`config/mod.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs), and [`protocol.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs).

The controller waits for spawn-edge persistence and initial input admission, commits reservations, and returns the live agent before the V2 handler emits the `Started` activity. Thus `Started` is evidence of successful control-plane creation/input admission in this source path, not evidence that the child has begun model inference. Actual execution permit acquisition belongs to later scheduling. Sources: [`control/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs) and [`multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs). This is static source ordering, not a live timing measurement.

## Additional finding: V1 and V2 expose different spawn identities

In the same source tag, the V1 spawn handler returns `agent_id` as the child thread ID string, plus an optional nickname. It also emits a `CollabAgentToolCall` turn item before and after spawn with sender/receiver thread IDs, prompt, model, reasoning effort, and agent status. The V1 `wait_agent.targets` contract can therefore consume the ID returned by V1 spawn. V2 instead returns a normalized `task_name`; its child thread ID stays in internal handler/activity structures. Do not interchange the V1 ID and V2 task name. This is a source-contract comparison and does not establish which fields a particular desktop UI displays. Source: [`V1 spawn handler`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents/spawn.rs), [`V1 wait handler`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents/wait.rs), and [`V2 spawn handler`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs).

## Additional finding: V2 spawn handler exists in the installed machine code

The installed arm64 Mach-O retains the Rust symbol for `multi_agents_v2::spawn::Handler::handle` at `0x105083d2c`. Targeted `otool -arch arm64 -tvV -p <mangled-symbol>` disassembly shows call instructions at `0x1050846d0`, `0x1050848c4`, `0x105085184`, and `0x105085204`, whose resolved symbol names are respectively `multi_agents_common::thread_spawn_source`, `session::multi_agents::resolve_usage_hints`, `record_collab_spawn_failure`, and `collab_spawn_error`. This is direct local machine-code evidence that the installed binary contains a V2 spawn handler wired to these named helpers. Symbol names and call sites do not by themselves prove every helper's behavior or that this exact binary was built from the matching public tag. The disassembly was inspected locally and is not a captured live spawn.

Reproduce the symbol lookup and focused disassembly on the same binary with:

```sh
BIN='/Applications/ChatGPT.app/Contents/Resources/codex-cli/CodexCLI.app/Contents/MacOS/codex'
SYM=$(nm -an "$BIN" | awk '/multi_agents_v2.*spawn.*Handler.*handle/ {print $NF; exit}')
otool -arch arm64 -tvV -p "$SYM" "$BIN"
```

## Live CLI probe

On 2026-10-13, one controlled CLI run used the embedded binary with `exec`, `--enable multi_agent_v2`, `--ignore-user-config`, `--sandbox read-only`, `--skip-git-repo-check`, and `/tmp` as cwd. The parent was asked to spawn one child with a fixed response, wait for it, and avoid other tools or file access. The persisted rollout records `multi_agent_version: v2`; `spawn_agent` returned only `{"task_name":"/root/probe_child"}`. The parent then emitted `SubAgentActivity` items of kind `started` and `completed` for that path, and called `wait_agent`, which returned `Wait completed.` with `timed_out: false`. A separate child rollout records `thread_source: subagent`, a `thread_spawn` source with parent linkage and depth 1, and final text `CHILD_PROBE_OK`. The parent received a typed `AgentMessage` from `/root/probe_child` with that result. This is one successful live CLI run, not a desktop conversation trace or evidence that the default desktop configuration selects V2.

An otherwise equivalent `--ephemeral` attempt did not create a child: the parent turn reported `spawn_agent failed because no rollout was found for the thread`, and no child result was available. This indicates that the tested spawn path needs a persisted rollout for the parent. It does not establish whether every ephemeral mode or host has the same constraint. Neither run read or modified project files; the successful run created normal parent and child session records under the user's Codex session store.

A follow-up fresh `exec` probe passed `--ignore-user-config --enable multi_agent --disable multi_agent_v2` and requested one fixed-response child. The same embedded CLI's `features list --disable multi_agent_v2` reported V2 `false`, and `debug models` reported the `gpt-6.1-sol` entry as `v1`. Yet this new run's rollout `turn_context` recorded `multi_agent_version=v2`; its `spawn_agent` call returned the V2-shaped `task_name`, and the child completed. In the matching source, `multi_agent_version_for_model` first honors the V2/Disabled config override, then uses the current turn's `ModelInfo.multi_agent_version`, then falls back to feature selection. Disabling `multi_agent_v2` cancels the config's V2 override; it does not force Disabled or V1 while agents remain enabled.

The source also narrows the measurement mismatch. `codex debug models` constructs its own config and model manager, then prints that manager's `raw_model_catalog`. A session instead asks its model manager for the active catalog, matches candidates against the turn's model slug, constructs `ModelInfo`, and applies config overrides. The source `with_config_overrides` function does not modify `multi_agent_version`. These are separate command/session contexts, so the debug output is not proof of the exact metadata snapshot consumed by the turn. A metadata-only read of the local cache found `gpt-6.1-sol` marked `v1`, with `fetched_at=2026-10-13T10:35:56Z`; the probe rollout began at `10:28:48Z`, so this cache snapshot post-dates the probe. The matching source declares a 300-second default cache TTL. This later snapshot does not reveal the catalog held in the probe session. The source supports independent catalog/context as a possible explanation, but the actual candidate metadata for this probe was not captured; the specific cause remains unknown. Sources: [`debug models` implementation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/cli/src/main.rs#L2093-L2121), [`get_model_info`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/models-manager/src/manager.rs#L212-L224), [`ModelInfo` config overrides](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/models-manager/src/model_info.rs#L19-L58), [`resolve_multi_agent_version_for_model`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L4019-L4031), and [`multi_agent_version_for_model`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs#L1487-L1512). This was one fresh CLI exec and does not generalize across models or hosts.

## Desktop research-session runtime metadata

The local Codex Desktop rollout used for this reverse-engineering work was structurally inspected on 2026-10-13 without exporting message text. Its session metadata identifies `originator=Codex Desktop`, `cli_version=0.162.0-alpha.17.2`, and `thread_source=user`. All 48 recorded `turn_context` entries contain `multi_agent_version=v1`. The corresponding thread row in the live app-server's local `state_5.sqlite` has no `agent_path`, and a read-only query of `thread_spawn_edges` found no parent or child edge for that thread. The thread-history projection also contains no `collabAgentToolCall` or `subAgentActivity` item for it. Together these observations support that this conversation was recorded as V1 and no subagent activity was observed in the inspected persisted surfaces. They do not establish a global desktop default, the version used by other conversations, or rule out another path not represented in those records. The rollout and database remain local and are not included in this repository; this is a scoped observation from the inspected local records.

A corrected read-only recount on 2026-10-14 parsed the current 181 local rollout JSONL files and emitted only aggregate counts. It found 19 `thread_source=subagent` records for desktop CLI `0.162.0-alpha.17.2`: 16 with `multi_agent_version=v1` and 3 with `v2`. All 19 child metadata parent IDs matched a parent rollout in the local store. These are rollout records, not necessarily 19 independent initial spawn calls; 12 carry a `forked_from_id`, including all 3 V2 records. Each of the 3 V2 records has one `task_complete` event with a non-empty `last_agent_message`; all three parent-child pairs also have rows in the local `thread_spawn_edges` table and corresponding parent/child thread rows. The edge rows currently report `open`, which is a database snapshot value and does not establish that a completed child is still active. After grouping parent rollout files by thread ID (some threads have multiple files), all three parents contain `spawn_agent` and `wait_agent` calls, matching `SubAgentActivity` items of kind `Started` and `Completed`, and a later `agent_message` whose author matches the child's recorded agent path. One parent also records `SubAgentActivity(Interacted)`. In all three pairs the parent `spawn_agent` call precedes the child `task_started` timestamp, but that child event precedes the parent's recorded `SubAgentActivity(Started)`; later, child `task_complete` precedes parent `SubAgentActivity(Completed)`, which precedes the matching parent `agent_message`. This shows the Started activity record can lag the child's recorded start. The ordering matches the source structure: the controller admits the initial input before returning from `spawn`, while the handler emits the Started activity only after that await returns. These are locally recorded event sequences, not proof that the visible ChatGPT desktop UI rendered every event or that the sample represents a default. The earlier 178-file count and child-version split were superseded by this recount. No prompt/answer contents, thread IDs, paths, agent names, or account identifiers were emitted or added to this repository.

A subsequent read-only recount on 2026-10-14 found 182 local rollout JSONL files and 20 matching desktop `0.162.0-alpha.17.2` subagent records: 17 V1 and 3 V2. All 20 child metadata parent IDs matched a parent rollout. This corrects the immediately prior 181-file snapshot by one V1 rollout. This recount emitted aggregate metadata only; it did not repeat the earlier event-body, wait-call, or database-edge correlation, so the detailed V2 findings above still refer to the three previously inspected V2 records. The counts remain rollout records, not independent initial spawn calls. No prompt, answer, ID, path, or agent identity was exported.

A follow-up matched each parent's `wait_agent` function call to its `function_call_output` by call ID, then compared the recorded timestamps with linked child and parent events. All three sampled parents had exactly one matched wait call; two outputs recorded `timed_out=false` and one recorded `timed_out=true`. The two non-timeout outputs mapped to `MailboxActivity` under the version-matched [`WaitAgentResult` outcome mapping](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/wait.rs#L135-L187); none mapped to `Steered`. In all three pairs, the parent's matching `agent_message` was recorded after the wait output. In the two `MailboxActivity` pairs, the linked child's `task_complete` and the parent's `SubAgentActivity(Completed)` were recorded between the wait call and its output. The timed-out child rollout contains multiple turns, so the available aggregation does not establish the completion timing of the specific child turn relative to that wait; no timeout-before-completion conclusion is drawn. The matched source emits V2 `Completed` activity before constructing and submitting a queue-only result communication (`trigger_turn=false`); when a loaded parent receives communication, its input queue enqueues the message and publishes `Mailbox` activity, while the later turn-input path records an `AgentMessage` in parent history. These source steps are consistent with the two local `MailboxActivity` returns occurring between completion activity and parent-history message, but the rollout has no mailbox-enqueue event and cannot identify which notification woke the handler. Sources: [`completion delivery`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs#L70-L111), [`parent mailbox enqueue`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/handlers.rs#L79-L94) and [`activity publication`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs#L115-L153). This remains a three-pair local sample and does not establish UI render timing or behavior across other sessions/configurations. No tool output text, message body, call ID, thread ID, path, or agent identity was exported.

The signature and notarization establish an OpenAI-signed local artifact, while the CLI version and Mach-O UUID identify the inspected build. They do not prove that it was built from the peeled source commit. The nested CLI bundle has only a generic bundle version, no dSYM/build manifest was found in the inspected bundle path, and selected embedded-string scans did not reveal a source revision. The matching tag remains a version-level source comparison, not a cryptographic source-to-binary match. The source tag itself is annotated but unsigned, and the peeled commit also has no Git signature; the remote GitHub tag ref was separately confirmed to resolve to the recorded tag object and commit. This establishes the fetched repository state, not a signed release attestation.

A separate read-only schema inspection of the local app-server `state_5.sqlite` found `threads` and `thread_spawn_edges` tables but no turn, event, transcript, or history table. The database therefore supports the parent-child identity correlation above, not a transcript/UI-history claim. Rollout JSONL files are the source for the recorded event sequence in this sample. This is limited to the inspected database schema; it does not establish that no other local or remote persistence surface exists.

A read-only process/socket snapshot on 2026-10-14 found the ChatGPT desktop main process and 166 descendants running, but no TCP listener in that process tree and no `--remote-debugging-*` or `--inspect` flag on the main process. The main process had the local `.codex/ipc/ipc.sock` Unix socket open; no data was sent to it. A static cross-check against the installed ChatGPT app bundle (version `26.1007.30912`, `app.asar` SHA-256 `75e397e2280dde93b70a85aa926f4282c728eacc84dfeb15ba7fb6ddd84628af`) found the matching path helper and IPC router/client implementation described below. `startup-requirements-C6B1x9gL.js` also uses the IPC path helpers to assemble Node `--allow-ipc=<path>` options and checks `process.permission.has("ipc", path)`, establishing a sandbox permission allowlist use. Separately, the exported startup configuration reader instantiates a stdio app-server connection: its writer appends a newline to JSON, and the reader sends `initialize`, `configRequirements/read`, then `config/read`. This does not establish that the configuration exchange uses `.codex/ipc/ipc.sock`. A read-only `lsof`/`netstat` snapshot found eight main-process descriptors for that path. A later peer-address cross-check mapped one endpoint pair: ChatGPT PID 62272 FD 252 (`0x10bc814dbe7031b5`, named `.codex/ipc/ipc.sock`) is paired with `SkyComputerUseService` PID 63010 FD 18 (`0x5d4fe0f51022012f`); `ps` identifies the latter as `/Users/joey/.codex/computer-use/Codex Computer Use.app/Contents/MacOS/SkyComputerUseService`, a child of PID 62272. This identifies one process peer, but not the socket message types or which side created/accepted the connection. The other path descriptors' peers and active router/fallback branch remain unresolved. REA's passive Electron workflow requires a loopback CDP endpoint, so this running instance did not provide a supported endpoint for UI notification capture. These are point-in-time host observations and do not establish that another launch configuration cannot expose CDP. No socket payload was read or written; no desktop notification stream or live React render was captured.

### Follow-up: desktop bundle refreshes child topology from typed app-server items

In the installed shared bundle `src-BJv5Mk7n.js` (SHA-256 `72e2b366d574206f76fc079c79b64c31ce378970a797475ded8709908d338f08`), notification reducer `Qx` updates conversation item state for `item/started` and `item/completed`. In the `item/completed` branch, after storing the normalized item, it calls `subagentTopology.discoverForSpawn(parentThreadId,item)` when the item is either `collabAgentToolCall` with tool `spawnAgent`, or `subAgentActivity` with kind `started` (callsite around source offsets 583,096–583,140). The latter means an activity item whose kind is `started`, delivered as a completed turn item; it is not the `item/started` notification for that activity item. For exactly one receiver whose stored thread metadata points to the expected parent, discovery refreshes that child and then scans its subtree. If receiver count is not one, the receiver ID is absent, or the stored parent identity does not match, it falls back to discovery from the parent. The topology reader queries `thread/list` using the subagent-spawn source kind when supported, otherwise supplements from stored item history, then reads candidate children with concurrency capped at two and checks both returned thread ID and parent identity. Failed or incomplete history reads remain marked incomplete. This is static bundle evidence of the client-side discovery policy, not proof the current desktop session executed the path or that a particular query succeeded.

### Follow-up: `ipc.sock` is a local desktop IPC router endpoint in the installed bundle

A whole-`/.vite/build/*.js` importer scan in the same `app.asar` found that `src-BJv5Mk7n.js` (SHA-256 `72e2b366d574206f76fc079c79b64c31ce378970a797475ded8709908d338f08`) imports the path and framing helpers from `application-network-startup-CfAQmSYg.js`. At byte offsets 1,518,660–1,524,407, the `IpcRouter` accepts stream connections, attaches the length-prefixed JSON reader, registers clients after an `initialize` request, and routes request/response/broadcast and client-discovery messages. At 1,524,408–1,525,501, `IpcRouterManager.getOrStartRouterEndpoint()` first checks `~/.codex/ipc/ipc.sock`; on non-Windows it can reuse an owned, connectable temp socket under `codex-ipc/ipc-<uid>.sock`; otherwise it removes only an owned stale primary socket, creates a Node `net` server on the primary path, and applies the socket permission helper after `listen`. The client class at 1,525,502 onward connects to the chosen endpoint, attaches the same framed reader, sends `initialize` with its client type, and retries after a connection closes. This statically identifies the primary path as an endpoint for the app's local IPC router and the framing helpers as its protocol implementation, rather than merely a path on a permission list.

The main bundle `main-CqEoQDOU.js` (SHA-256 `09d19386e1f23cf4ccbc950fc40d85a1e8a064a08be588b2a4be6a89f6ada9e6`) imports that client class as `i.y` and constructs `desktop` clients at byte offsets 724,424, 3,218,136, 3,318,575, and 3,354,777. The first is wired to host-manager coordination; the later callsites are guarded by `localClientCoordinationEnabled` and include query-cache invalidation and a per-WebContents host service. In the inspected whole main bundle, the identifiable `sendBroadcast` call sends `thread-read-state-changed`; the `addAnyBroadcastHandler` call handles `query-cache-invalidate`. The app-server connection separately registers `item/started` and `item/completed` callbacks at byte offset 721,963 and branches on `subAgentActivity` and `collabAgentToolCall` items. This establishes distinct visible call paths in this bundle, not that the generic IPC router could never carry additional messages. The inspected per-WebContents specialization adds host query-cache invalidation around the shared IPC service; the generic router supports local requests and broadcasts. A live `lsof` peer-address snapshot maps one `ipc.sock` endpoint to the child process `SkyComputerUseService` (PID 63010, PPID 62272); it does not reveal the payload or connection direction. These static/runtime observations establish some desktop client roles and one process peer, not which roles the other path descriptors serve. They do not establish that subagent lifecycle notifications use this socket. The typed app-server item notification and subagent topology paths remain separately identified in the webview/app-server bundles. The other peer mappings, current router election/fallback branch, and whether any subagent-specific message traverses this router remain unobserved; no socket payload was read or written during this investigation.

## Finding ledger

| Question | Result | Evidence and boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Is spawn a separate CLI process? | The inspected source path uses shared `AgentControl` to create a child thread. | The V2 handler calls `agent_control.spawn(SpawnRequest { ... })`; the controller calls `spawn_new_thread_with_source` or the forked-thread path. This source call path does not establish every platform/runtime mode. |
| What gives a child its identity? | A child thread ID plus `ThreadSpawn` source metadata and agent path. | Source: [`spawn.rs` handler](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs), [`control/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs). |
| Does V1 return the same spawn identifier as V2? | No. V1 returns the child thread ID as `agent_id`; V2 returns `task_name`. | V1 also emits a `CollabAgentToolCall` turn item with sender/receiver IDs and status, while V2 has a separate activity/task-name contract. See [V1/V2 spawn identity](#additional-finding-v1-and-v2-expose-different-spawn-identities). |
| When does the child receive its first input? | After the parent-child spawn edge has been persisted and awaited. | In `spawn_agent_internal`, edge persistence is scheduled and `pending_spawn.wait_for_edge()` precedes `send_input` / inter-agent communication. Source: [`control/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs). |
| How does V2 completion reach the parent? | Session terminal events become an `AgentTurnOutcome`; completion activity and result communication are routed separately. The result uses `trigger_turn = false`, may enter a receptive active turn or remain queued for a later turn, and does not itself start an idle parent turn. | Source: [`session/mod.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs), [`completion.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs), [`control.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs), and [`input_queue.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs). Queue acceptance is not proof the parent model consumed the result. |
| Which terminal states produce a V2 parent result? | Completed and errored outcomes can route a result; ordinary interruption and standalone shutdown completion do not use this hook. | The session hook only handles `TurnComplete` / `TurnAborted`, and `is_final` excludes `Interrupted`; Guardian `TooManyDenials` is specially converted to `Errored`. `ShutdownComplete` updates status but is not among the hook's accepted events. Child event persistence/delivery is a separate path. Sources: [`session/mod.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs), [`agent/status.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/status.rs), and [`session_prefix.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session_prefix.rs). Static source analysis only; other UI status reads and live behavior were not observed. |
| Does an `Interrupted` activity prove a child turn was canceled? | No; it marks the accepted tool path and carries a pre-dispatch status snapshot. | The V2 handler reads a target snapshot, calls controller interruption, and emits `SubAgentActivityKind::Interrupted` after success. The controller treats `ThreadNotFound` / `InternalAgentDied` during dispatch as tolerated outcomes, and does not reload an unloaded child. Thus this activity alone does not prove a running turn was actually canceled. Sources: [`interrupt_agent` handler](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/interrupt_agent.rs), [`interrupt controller`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/interrupt.rs), and [`control op`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs). Static source only; no live interrupt race was induced. |
| Are child completion results and error notices truncated alike? | No, not in the inspected formatter. | Error status text is truncated to 900 tokens, with 100 tokens reserved under a 1,000-token message target; successful `last_agent_message` is cloned directly and has no same-layer truncation call. This does not establish end-to-end context/transport limits. Sources: [`session_prefix.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session_prefix.rs) and [`inter_agent_completion_message.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/context/inter_agent_completion_message.rs). Static source only. |
| What protocol item does the parent receive for a V2 completion? | A typed `AgentMessage` with the child as author and parent as recipient, containing the rendered completion text as `InputText`. | `InterAgentCompletionMessage::role()` is `assistant`, but completion routing calls `render()` and passes the resulting string through `InterAgentCommunication`; protocol conversion yields `ResponseItem::AgentMessage`, not an ordinary role-tagged `ResponseItem::Message`. The parent session prepares and records the item in history/rollout before emitting its raw response item event. This establishes the source representation and recording order, not that a live model sampled or used it. Sources: [`inter_agent_completion_message.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/context/inter_agent_completion_message.rs), [`completion.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs), [`protocol.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs), and [`session/mod.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs). | Is the completion payload necessarily the model's `final_answer` phase? | The source does not enforce that phase in `last_agent_message` extraction. | Assistant `Message` items map to `AgentMessage`; finalization concatenates text and stores a nonblank body without phase filtering. Phase affects adjacent mailbox deferral logic. The turn carries the last sampling result into `TurnComplete`, which completion routing uses as payload. The desktop query separately prefers `phase=final_answer`, then falls back to the latest nonempty agent message. This identifies different selection rules; no live phase/output was captured. Sources: [`event_mapping.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/event_mapping.rs), [`stream_events_utils.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/stream_events_utils.rs), [`turn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs), and [`UI result query`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/webview/assets/chatgpt-subagent-final-response-query-b2b6816e81df.js). Static source only. |
| What does the completion watcher do? | It is the legacy notification route in this source snapshot. | Spawn starts `maybe_start_completion_watcher` only when the effective version is not V2. The watcher subscribes to child status, checks terminal status, then injects a legacy notification or sends a completion message for a V2 child encountered during legacy recovery. Source: [`control.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs), [`control/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs). |
| What does `wait_agent` wait for? | The contract differs by version: V1 waits for terminal status from explicit targets; V2 waits for current-turn input-queue activity. | V1 accepts target thread IDs and returns when any target reaches a final status (initially final targets return immediately); it can collect additional already-ready finals. V2 takes no target IDs, subscribes to the current sub-turn's queue activity, and completes on pending/new mailbox activity, steer, or deadline. In the pending turn state, user input/function-call output is steer and takes precedence over pending inter-agent communication (mailbox). Mailbox activity is published when communication is accepted/enqueued or mailbox is explicitly notified; new turn input publishes steer. A mailbox wake does not establish that the parent model consumed the result. V2 timeout below min is raised to min and above max is rejected; source defaults are 10s/30s/1h. V1 requires positive timeout and clamps to its range. Sources: [`V1 wait`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents/wait.rs), [`V2 wait`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/wait.rs), [`input queue`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs), and [`timeout defaults`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs). Static source analysis only; no wait call was observed live. |
| What does `SubAgentActivity(Completed)` contain? | It is a status/lifecycle item, separate from the child result communication. | The typed activity has an ID, kind, child thread ID, and agent path; it carries no result text. Completion routing emits it as an instantaneous `ItemStarted`/`ItemCompleted` item to the activity recipient. App-server v2 consumes canonical turn-item lifecycle; deprecated `EventMsg::SubAgentActivity` remains for raw-event/rollout compatibility. The completion message is separately routed as `InterAgentCommunication` to the structural parent. Sources: [`protocol item/event`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs), [`activity emission`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs), [`completion routing`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs), and [`app-server item handling`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/app-server/src/bespoke_event_handling.rs). Static source only; no live event was captured. |
| How does subagent activity reach the desktop UI store? | Core emits a canonical instantaneous item; app-server v2 forwards typed item notifications, which the bundle stores and uses to trigger discovery. | `emit_sub_agent_activity` emits `ItemStarted` then `ItemCompleted` for one `TurnItem::SubAgentActivity`; the v2 event handler maps those canonical events to `item/started`/`item/completed`. The app-shared notification handler normalizes the item, inserts/updates the turn, then calls `discoverForSpawn` for a spawn call or `subAgentActivity(started)`. Deprecated raw `EventMsg::SubAgentActivity` is explicitly skipped by the v2 app-server path. Static source and bundle evidence; no desktop notification stream was captured. |
| Are completion activity and result delivery coupled? | No; completion activity is best-effort to the initiating thread, while result delivery separately targets the structural parent. | If a distinct initiating path cannot be resolved, or its session cannot be looked up for activity emission, the error is logged and completion routing continues. Result delivery then has its own queued/failed outcome metric. Thus the activity can be absent while result communication still succeeds. Sources: [`completion routing`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs) and [`activity emission`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs). Static source only; no delivery failure was induced. |
| Can a mailbox wake be consumed in the same model request? | Not necessarily: queue activity and current-turn mailbox delivery are separate checks. | `subscribe_activity` reports mailbox pending via `has_pending_mailbox_items`, which checks retained mail/controller updates without consulting `MailboxDeliveryPhase`. Delivering to a current turn and draining mailbox into turn input do check that phase. A visible assistant final-answer item can defer queue-only mailbox delivery to `NextTurn` (unless explicit same-turn work is pending); tool/follow-up/steer paths can reopen `CurrentTurn`. Thus `wait_agent` can see mailbox activity while the communication remains queued for the next turn. Sources: [`input_queue.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs), [`stream_events_utils.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/stream_events_utils.rs), and [`V2 wait.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/wait.rs). Static source only; no online timing experiment. |

## Versioned source path

The public source tag exactly matches the CLI's reported version. In that snapshot:

1. The V2 tool handler builds a `ThreadSpawn` source and sends a `SpawnRequest` to `AgentControl`.
2. `spawn_agent_internal` selects the effective multi-agent version, admits the start, checks capacity, reserves registry/residency slots, and prepares inherited environment/execution policy and metadata.
3. The controller creates the child thread (or forked thread), records the spawn edge, waits for persistence, then submits the first input.
4. On `TurnComplete` or `TurnAborted`, a V2 session constructs an `AgentTurnOutcome` and calls `turn_finished`.
5. Completion routing may emit a `Completed` activity to the initiating thread and queues an inter-agent result communication with `trigger_turn = false`.
6. The parent queue receiving a message and the parent model processing it are separate events.

The older watcher accepts only a `ThreadSpawn` source with a structural parent and only starts while the shared agent-tree runtime admits new work. Its status stream emits an initial snapshot, then coalesced watch-channel updates; it subscribes before reading thread settings so it does not miss an intervening status change. If subscription setup fails or the stream ends/errors before a terminal status is observed, it calls `get_status` once. Final statuses are `Completed`, `Errored`, `Shutdown`, and `NotFound`; `PendingInit`, `Running`, and `Interrupted` are non-final. A tree-shutdown cancellation exits the select, and a `Shutdown` result is suppressed when that same tree shutdown is in progress. Other final results are routed to the structural parent: when an agent path exists and the child is V2, the watcher sends a result communication with `trigger_turn = false`; otherwise it injects `SubagentNotification` without starting a parent turn. Missing parent state or failed path/message construction ends delivery; the V2 send result is intentionally ignored. The watcher holds an agent-tree teardown guard, and an abnormal drop before `complete()` records a shutdown failure. Fresh and resumed spawn callsites both start it only when the effective version is not V2. Sources: [`control.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs), [`control/watch.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/watch.rs), [`status.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/status.rs), [`control/runtime.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/runtime.rs), and [`control/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs). This is source-derived behavior; no runtime watcher event was captured.

### Same-tag app-server and state database path

The same source tag also defines the local `thread/list` relation filter. `parentThreadId` and `ancestorThreadId` are mutually exclusive and become direct-child or descendant filters; the app-server passes them through the thread store. The state DB implements descendant lookup with a recursive SQLite CTE over `thread_spawn_edges`, then joins those child IDs to stored threads. `sourceKinds: [SubAgentThreadSpawn]` is mapped to a post-filter over the stored session source. The selected source path's child edge is upserted in the local agent graph store unless the child is ephemeral, and the spawn controller waits for the edge-write task before sending the child's first input. This links edge persistence to the source-level spawn sequence and explains the client bundle's efficient ancestor query. It remains evidence about the matching public source tag; it does not prove this local ChatGPT app's backend process is byte-for-byte from that commit.

Sources: [`thread_processor.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs), [`state/src/runtime/threads.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/state/src/runtime/threads.rs), [`thread-store/src/local/list_threads.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/thread-store/src/local/list_threads.rs), [`control.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs), [`control/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs), and [`0021_thread_spawn_edges.sql`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/state/migrations/0021_thread_spawn_edges.sql).

The persisted edge lifecycle has only `open` and `closed`: open means the child is still live or resumable in the spawn graph. Explicit `close_agent` marks the edge closed before shutdown; `shutdown_live_agent` intentionally does not. Thus edge state is not the same field as the UI's working/done/failed status, and a shutdown operation alone does not imply graph closure.

### Parent result delivery after child termination

In the same source tag, V2 terminal handling has two parent-facing effects with different conditions. A successful child emits `SubAgentActivityKind::Completed` only when its outcome has a `parent_turn_id`; the result communication is then built separately for the parent agent path. The result message has `trigger_turn = false`, so delivery does not by itself start an idle parent turn.

For a loaded parent, the controller submits `Op::InterAgentCommunication` to the session. The session enqueues it in its mailbox and lets the shared pending-work scheduler decide whether to start work; for `trigger_turn = false`, an ordinary idle session is not automatically started. Mailbox delivery can still be accepted into the current active turn while that turn accepts mailbox input. Otherwise the item remains pending and can be collected into later turn input. For an unloaded parent, the controller queues the communication in the local runtime mailbox; the next loaded session takes those messages into its own input queue. Accordingly, successful queue submission proves delivery was accepted or retained, not that the parent model has already consumed the result.

When a parent turn processes the queued `TurnInput::InterAgentCommunication`, the hook path calls `Session::record_inter_agent_communication`. `InterAgentCommunication::to_model_input_item` converts it to a typed `ResponseItem::AgentMessage` carrying author, recipient, and content; the session prepares and records that item in conversation history/rollout and emits the response item. This establishes the source-level handoff from mailbox to model-visible conversation context, while still not proving that a particular live model request sampled that item. Sources: [`hook_runtime.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/hook_runtime.rs), [`session/mod.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs), and [`protocol.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs).

The result content is formatted from terminal status: successful output, an error message with bounded error text and next-step guidance, or shutdown/not-found wording; pending, running, and interrupted statuses produce no generic completion message. Special Guardian denial outcomes use a separate instruction. Sources: [`completion.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs), [`control.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs), [`mailbox.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/mailbox.rs), [`handlers.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/handlers.rs), and [`input_queue.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs).

### `send_message` versus `followup_task`

Both V2 tools use one shared handler that rejects whitespace-only messages, resolves the target to a thread ID, builds a resume config from the caller's current turn, sends an `AgentInput::Message`, and emits an `Interacted` activity after the send succeeds. The difference is the delivery mode: `send_message` selects `QueueOnly`, while `followup_task` selects `TriggerTurn`. The target resolution accepts a thread ID directly or resolves an agent reference in the caller's session source. Sources: [`message_tool.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/message_tool.rs), [`send_message.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/send_message.rs), [`followup_task.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/followup_task.rs), and [`control/api.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs).

For a loaded target, `QueueOnly` submits ordinary inter-agent communication without the trigger flag. The session handler enqueues it and does not ask the pending-work scheduler to start a turn unless the target has an outstanding durable sleep. For an unloaded target, the controller retains queue-only mail in a runtime mailbox; later session loading drains it into the session queue. By contrast, `TriggerTurn` ensures the V2 target is loaded, marks plaintext communication as `NewTask` (or sets the trigger flag on encrypted communication), checks turn-start capacity, and asks the session scheduler to start a regular turn when pending mailbox work is present and the session is idle. Triggered delivery to the root agent is explicitly rejected. Sources: [`delivery.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/delivery.rs), [`control/api.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs), [`control.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs), [`handlers.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/handlers.rs), and [`tasks/mod.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tasks/mod.rs).

Trigger delivery to an unloaded child uses the existing thread identity and stored history/rollout to resume a V2 session; it does not call the spawn path to create a replacement child. The restore path rejects non-V2 history. If a parent is supplied as owner, it validates that parent is still running, belongs to the same runtime registry, and matches the child's recorded parent; the sender-driven message path passes no parent object and instead restores from the child source and stored configuration/history. Sources: [`control/api.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs) and [`control/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs).

These paths establish submission and wake policy, not immediate consumption: a queued message can wait for a later turn, and an `Interacted` activity is emitted after accepted delivery rather than after the target model has read the message. This is source-derived behavior for the matching tag; no live child was messaged in this investigation.

### V2 interruption is not closure

The V2 `interrupt_agent` handler snapshots the target first, asks the controller to interrupt it, returns the snapshot's prior status, and emits an `Interrupted` activity. The V2 controller rejects the root and self-targets. If the child runtime is unloaded or already dead, thread-not-found/internal-agent-died errors from the interrupt dispatch are treated as success; this path does not reload the target. It does not mark the persisted spawn edge closed or remove the agent from the registry. In contrast, `close_agent` explicitly marks the edge closed and shuts down the target plus currently live descendants; ordinary shutdown removes runtime/registry/mailbox state but intentionally does not itself close the persisted edge. A later `followup_task` takes the trigger-turn path and ensures the V2 target is loaded before delivery. Sources: [`interrupt_agent.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/interrupt_agent.rs), [`control/interrupt.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/interrupt.rs), [`control/api.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs), [`control/legacy.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/legacy.rs), and [`control/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs). This is a static source distinction; no interrupt/restart experiment was run.

### `list_agents` is a loaded-runtime view

The V2 handler returns agent path/name and status, with an optional prefix resolved relative to the caller's agent path. The controller gathers metadata from the in-memory agent registry, then includes an entry only if its thread is currently present in the thread manager; known but unloaded agents are skipped, and a root entry is likewise added only when its thread is loaded. The app UI's separate persisted `thread/list` and history-discovery paths therefore have broader historical coverage than this tool result. Sources: [`multi_agents_v2/list_agents.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/list_agents.rs), [`control/api.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs), [`control.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs), and [`registry.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/registry.rs).

### Spawn admission and rollback

The V2 spawn path applies more than one resource check. `ensure_execution_capacity` checks the V2 subagent turn-execution limiter; actual turn admission obtains a separate RAII execution guard, held for the admitted task's lifetime. The limiter is initialized from the session's effective V2 agent max-thread setting. Spawn also reserves a registry slot. For V2 resident child sources it first reserves a V2 residency slot and does not pass the ordinary registry max-thread limit to `reserve_spawn_slot`; other spawn paths pass their effective agent max-thread limit to the registry. These controls therefore describe different resources and should not be collapsed into one concurrency limit. Sources: [`execution.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/execution.rs), [`api.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs), [`tasks/mod.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tasks/mod.rs), [`session/session.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/session.rs), [`spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs), and [`registry.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/registry.rs).

After thread creation, `PendingSpawn` owns the child and tree membership until initial input admission succeeds. If that scope exits before `disarm`, its drop handler schedules cleanup: remove and stop the child, discard its live rollout when available, wait for any pending edge-write task, mark the spawn edge closed, then release membership. An uncommitted registry reservation also releases the count and any reserved path on drop. This is source-described failure cleanup; this investigation did not inject a failure or observe it at runtime. Source: [`spawn_guard.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn_guard.rs) and [`registry.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/registry.rs).

### Child input, configuration, and history modes

The V2 handler turns the supplied `message` into the child's first `AgentInput::Message` and requests `TriggerTurn`. A direct plaintext tool call becomes `AgentMessage::Plaintext`; other tool-call sources become `AgentMessage::Encrypted`. This describes the input type selected by the handler, not whether any downstream transport decrypts or exposes particular content. The handler also passes the current turn's environment snapshot, parent/turn/root IDs, and requested fork mode to the controller. Source: [`multi_agents_v2.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2.rs) and [`multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs).

`fork_turns` defaults to `all`; `none` selects a fresh thread, while `all` and any positive integer select `FullHistory`. The integer is accepted for compatibility but does not limit copied turns. V2 rejects legacy `fork_context`. In a fresh thread, the controller may preserve paginated history mode and, only when the V2 dynamic-tools feature is enabled, copies the parent's dynamic tools. In a full-history fork it loads parent model context, filters agent-message items and selected parent-local metadata/instructions, then creates the fork. These branches mean `none` still receives the explicit initial `message`; it means no forked parent history, not no input. Sources: [`spawn.rs` handler](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs) and [`control/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs).

Child config is assembled from the live parent turn: current model/provider/reasoning and developer instructions, current approval policy, reviewer, working directory and permission-profile snapshot. Requested or configured default subagent model/reasoning can override those values after model-catalog validation. A selected role is then applied as a constrained config overlay: it may supply instructions/model/personality/service tier and disable selected features or skills, but the implementation comment says roles cannot replace parent authority. For a full-history V2 fork, role application is skipped unless a role was explicitly named; V2 supports that explicit override while V1 rejects it. Source: [`child_config.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/child_config.rs) and [`role.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/role.rs).

This is source analysis at the matching public tag. It does not establish the resolved settings or exact inputs of a live child session.

Tool availability is also not copied as one opaque parent registry. Session startup materializes the selected child dynamic-tool snapshot into session configuration; each turn then builds a tool router from the child's turn config, environment snapshot, MCP binding, extensions, and dynamic tools. Role overlays only carry feature disables for the listed shell, apps, plugins, memory, and request-permissions features; they do not apply role feature enables. This supports the narrower conclusion that part of the tool surface is rebuilt under child config and policy, while V2 dynamic tools have an explicit gated inheritance path. It does not establish the exact tools exposed by any live child, since those depend on runtime config, MCP/extensions, model, and feature state. Sources: [`session/mod.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs), [`session/turn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs), [`tools/spec_plan.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs), and [`agent/role.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/role.rs).

The model-facing V2 tool schema requires `task_name` and `message`; optional fields and even `agent_type` / model override exposure depend on `SpawnAgentToolOptions`. The role description is generated from configured user roles followed by built-ins, and can append model/reasoning settings marked as non-overridable. There is a small contract mismatch worth documenting: the `fork_turns` description says only `all` and `none` are supported, while the runtime parser also accepts any positive integer and maps it to full-history fork without limiting turns. The schema models it as a string, so the narrower wording is descriptive rather than an enum validation. Sources: [`multi_agents_spec.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs), [`role.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/role.rs), and [`multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs).

## Local static evidence

Curated symbol names found in the executable include:

- `LocalAgentControl::prepare_thread_spawn`
- `LocalAgentControl::maybe_start_completion_watcher`
- `AgentControl for LocalAgentControl::turn_finished`
- V2 `spawn` and `wait` handler `spec` / `handle` methods
- V2 `list_agents`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, and `interrupt_agent` handler paths
- legacy `spawn`, `wait`, `send_input`, and `close_agent` handler paths
- callsite metadata associated with `notify_parent_of_terminal_turn`

The arm64 named entry for `LocalAgentControl::maybe_start_completion_watcher` begins at `0x1049d8348` and directly calls `LocalAgentRuntime::admit_start` at `0x1049d8478`. Mach-O `LC_FUNCTION_STARTS` places the next function boundary at `0x1049d8694`, so this named entry is only `0x34c` bytes; the next named symbol, `render_input_preview`, begins at `0x1049dd484`. Between those addresses the table contains seven anonymous starts: `0x1049d8694`, `0x1049d8e7c`, `0x1049d9230`, `0x1049d9508`, `0x1049d9644`, `0x1049d9dcc`, and `0x1049dad98`. Therefore, the interval to the next *named* symbol is not one watcher function or a trustworthy source-level call graph. The local entry confirms admission and contains an indirect setup call at `0x1049d854c`; the matching source identifies the async task setup, but that raw target has no named symbol here.

An exact-range `otool -tvV` extraction shows that the anonymous function beginning at `0x1049d8694` is an async poll state machine and directly calls the function beginning at `0x1049d9dcc` at `0x1049d8af4`. The latter contains named calls to `LocalAgentRuntime::admit_start`, `AgentRegistry::agent_metadata_for_thread`, `Mailboxes::enqueue`, and `agent_communication::emit_agent_communication_send`, which match the source-level submission of a queue-only inter-agent communication. A later cross-reference changed the interpretation of these anonymous functions: the `turn_finished` poll at `0x1049d2f94` also directly calls `0x1049d8694` as a nested future after constructing its parent-result communication state. The best current source-correlated mapping is therefore `0x1049d8694` to the shared `send_inter_agent_communication` future and `0x1049d9dcc` to its submission future, reused by more than one caller; they should not be described as uniquely belonging to the spawned completion-watcher wrapper. The watcher entry at `0x1049d8348` is separately bounded to `0x34c` bytes by `LC_FUNCTION_STARTS`. Exact anonymous future types and source statements remain unproven without DWARF/type metadata, and no call proves runtime execution. Status and routing semantics remain source-derived.

The `turn_finished` poll's direct callsite at `0x1049d2f94` prepares a `0x118`-byte nested state at `[x19+0x298]`, passes that state and a context pointer to `0x1049d8694`, then examines the returned poll/result storage. This call shape plus the downstream submission helpers is consistent with the source call to `send_inter_agent_communication` in `notify_parent_of_terminal_turn`. It is strong cross-caller corroboration for shared result delivery, but not a type-level identification of the stripped future.

The binary also contains the named arm64 entry for `AgentControl for LocalAgentControl::turn_finished` at `0x1049ddce0`; `LC_FUNCTION_STARTS` bounds it to `0x1049ddce0..0x1049ddda0` (0xc0 bytes). Exact-range disassembly shows allocation of an 8232-byte object and return of its pointer alongside a constant-data pointer. The pointed-to vtable begins at `0x10dffdd48`: its first entries decode as drop function `0x104cc794c`, size `0x2028`, alignment `8`, then the `Future::poll` method pointer `0x1049d1a70`. `dyld_info -function_starts` independently lists `0x1049d1a70` and the next function start `0x1049d406c`, delimiting a 0x25fc-byte poll entry. The exact-range disassembly contains async-state dispatch; decoding arm64 `BL` instructions across `__TEXT,__text` finds a direct call from this poll to `format_inter_agent_completion_message` at `0x1049d2df0` and a direct call to `format_guardian_interruption_message` at `0x1049d33c4`. A second direct call to the completion formatter exists elsewhere at `0x104dd4648`, outside this poll. Targeted `llvm-objdump` confirms the two poll callsites and their targets; the formatter symbol addresses are `0x10587d474` and `0x10587d248`, respectively.
