### Follow-up: indirect terminal callback candidate

Targeted LLDB disassembly of anonymous function `0x104ab366c` (bounded by the next `LC_FUNCTION_STARTS` entry at `0x104ab57dc`) found a substantially stronger candidate for the Session-to-controller edge. In this poll function, `agent_status_from_event` is called at `0x104ab3e90`; an async continuation loads the service-object pointer from `[x19 + 0x9e0]` at `0x104ab4090`, then loads words at service offsets `+0x968` and `+0x970` into `x23` and `x22` at `0x104ab4094` and `0x104ab4098`. Later, at `0x104ab4330`, it loads a function pointer from `[x22 + 0x80]` and invokes it with `blr` at `0x104ab4340`. The `+0x970`-derived value is therefore the proven base of this indirect-call slot; its interpretation as the runtime `AgentControl` vtable is source-correlated by the `SessionServices` layout and the pinned trait declaration, not by runtime inspection. The call arguments are `x0 = x8 + 0x10`, `x1 = sp + 0x30`, and `x2 = ([x19 + 0x9e0]) + 0x6b0`. Crucially, the earlier `+0x968` load into `x23` does flow into `x0`: `x24` is loaded from `[x22 + 0x10]`, then the code computes `x8 = x23 + ((x24 - 1) & 0xfffffffffffffff0)` and passes `x8 + 0x10`. In a Rust trait-object vtable, the `+0x10` word is the alignment metadata. The arithmetic matches the aligned data offset for an `ArcInner<T>` with a 16-byte header (two `Atomic<usize>` fields followed by `data: T`): `ArcInner` is `repr(C)`, and the standard library computes the data offset as the header size plus padding for `T`'s alignment ([Rust `ArcInner` and data-offset implementation](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/alloc/sync.rs.html#387-410), [offset calculation](https://doc.rust-lang.org/src/alloc/sync.rs.html#4227-4290)). This makes `x0` strongly consistent with the `Arc<dyn AgentControl>` data pointer loaded at service offset `+0x968`, and materially strengthens the receiver attribution. The exact runtime vtable value and a live invocation remain unobserved. After the indirect call, the returned two-word value is stored and its `Future::poll` slot is invoked at `0x104ab435c`.

This matches the pinned source contract unusually closely: `SessionServices` contains `rollout_thread_trace` and `agent_control: Arc<dyn AgentControl>`; `AgentControl::turn_finished` is declared after 13 preceding methods, so its trait-object vtable slot is `+0x80`; the two independently inspected LocalAgentControl tables place the named `turn_finished` entry `0x1049ddce0` at that slot; and the method returns `BoxFuture<'_, ()>`. The receiver arithmetic also matches the aligned `ArcInner<T>` data offset, strengthening the mapping from the service's `Arc<dyn AgentControl>` field to `x0`; this remains a layout correlation, not runtime pointer inspection or proof of the compiler's exact type lowering. The nearby event-status conversion and async lock path also match the terminal notification implementation's use of `agent_status_from_event` and `terminal_error.lock().await`. The candidate remains a **strong source-correlated static candidate** for the inlined `Session::maybe_notify_parent_of_terminal_turn` call to `AgentControl::turn_finished`: the service-field/vtable-slot chain, receiver calculation, and returned boxed-future polling align, while the runtime table value and invocation remain unobserved.

A further guard is visible before the indirect call: at `0x104ab4084` the code loads the status discriminant from `[x21]`, compares it with `2` at `0x104ab4088`, and branches to the early path at `0x104ab40fc` when the value is at most `2`. The named `agent_status_from_event` helper at `0x1058b6ce0` constructs status tags consistent with the pinned source mapping (including `Running` tag 1, `Interrupted` tag 2, `Completed` tag 3, and `Errored` tag 4). This boundary aligns with source `is_final`, which excludes `PendingInit`, `Running`, and `Interrupted`; it is additional static correlation for a final-status guard, not a full mapping of every helper branch or every caller guard. The V2 and accepted-event checks are mapped as strong static candidates below; the source guard is now correlated through the two same-field clone dispatches, with the `Option<AgentPath>` detail still inferred.

The helper's full relevant mapping is now visible in the LLDB range `0x1058b6ce0..0x1058b6ea8`: normalized EventMsg tags `15`, `17`, `58`, and `59` produce `Running`, the `TurnComplete` result, the `TurnAborted` result, and `Shutdown`, respectively; tag `0` produces `Errored` for `Error`, while unmatched tags return `None` (tag `7`). In the candidate poll function, a separate preceding sequence at `0x104ab3ca0` loads a byte from `[x8 + 0x525]`, compares it with `2`, and branches away when unequal. It then normalizes the EventMsg tag loaded through `x10` and admits only normalized tags `0x3a` and `0x11` (`TurnAborted` and `TurnComplete` in the helper's mapping) before later async work. This is a strong source-correlated candidate for the V2 and accepted-event guards. The captured future fields at `x8` and `x10` do not have independent type metadata, so their identity as `TurnContext` and `EventMsg` is inferred from adjacency, helper behavior, and matching source order; it is not a standalone type-level proof. A later same-field guard and the two source-clone dispatches now provide a strong static mapping for the nested `ThreadSpawn { agent_path: Some(_) }` filter; the exact future-field types and machine-level `Option` tag remain inferred rather than metadata-proven.

The returned `turn_finished` boxed future's poll entry is independently identified at `0x1049d1a70` through the concrete LocalAgentControl vtable. In its initial path-processing block, it calls `core::slice::memchr::memrchr` with byte `0x2f` (`/`) at `0x1049d1b78`, validates the returned position and separator, then directly calls the named `AgentPath::try_from` implementation at `0x1049d1ba8` on the prefix. This closely matches the source operation that takes `child_agent_path.as_str().rsplit_once('/')` and validates the parent path. It maps the parent-path extraction/validation portion of the concrete callback future. This callback alone does not prove the caller's preceding source match; the caller-side same-field guard and clone dispatches are analyzed separately below.

The attribution remains a source-correlated static mapping, not a complete type-level proof. The stripped/optimized binary provides no Rust future type or DWARF mapping, so the exact anonymous future identity and the runtime value of the loaded vtable are not independently observed. The V2, event-kind, and nested `ThreadSpawn` guards have strong static candidates; whether the high-bit niche is specifically the `None` encoding for `agent_path` remains inferred from source and layout behavior. The `ArcInner<T>` comparison uses the documented standard-library representation and alignment calculation; the exact compiler/toolchain provenance of the installed binary is not independently established. The earlier full `BL` scan remains valid: this edge is indirect and therefore was outside that scan's scope. No terminal event was induced or captured. Keep the source-derived behavior separate from this static callsite correlation, and do not claim that the desktop session executed it.

The earlier caller block's source-field guard is at `0x104ab3ccc`, before creation of the terminal-error lock future. It loads a word from `[x8 + 0x3d0]`; it exits at `0x104ab43a4` if the word is at least `0x8000000000000006`, then also exits if bit 63 is set. The same offset is decoded later in outcome construction (`0x104ab40c0..0x104ab4264`): the outer clone dispatch maps low-half values to `SessionSource::SubAgent` (case 6), and that arm calls `0x104df14b0`. The helper's nested dispatch maps low-half values to its variant-2 payload-cloning path, matching `SubAgentSource::ThreadSpawn` in the pinned source. This gives a strong static mapping from the pre-lock range guard through both enum layers. The source requires `agent_path: Some(_)`; the rejected high-bit/niche forms are consistent with the `None` case, but the stripped binary exposes no future type metadata or standalone `Option<AgentPath>` tag label, so that final field-level mapping remains a source-correlated inference rather than a type-level proof. The later dispatch is outcome-source cloning, not the pre-lock guard itself. No terminal event was induced or captured.

### Follow-up: completion activity event delivery loop

The pinned [`LocalAgentControl::emit_sub_agent_activity`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs#L186-L233) supplies the missing source-side operation sequence. It sends `ItemStarted` with `send_event_raw`, builds and sends `ItemCompleted`, then iterates `completed.as_legacy_events(false)` and awaits one `send_event_raw` for each legacy event. In `completion.rs`, the completed activity invokes this method and awaits it; any returned error is logged and does not stop construction/delivery of the parent result message.

In the installed CLI poll, `0x1049d2b00` calls `ItemCompletedEvent::as_legacy_events`. The following instructions read the returned vector representation, compute its end pointer using a `0x260`-byte element stride, and store the vector metadata in future fields (`0x1049d2b04..0x1049d2b24`). The same future later loads the vector begin/end pointers at `0x1049d3e10..0x1049d3e1c`, compares them, advances the begin pointer by `0x260` bytes per element, and copies each `0x260`-byte element into the current nested-future state at `0x1049d3e24..0x1049d3e58`. It then constructs/polls another async state before returning to the element loop. At `0x1049d3ed4`, the completed vector is passed to its drop helper, whose disassembly walks elements at the same `0x260` stride and drops them. This establishes element-by-element consumption and awaited nested work in the machine code. The source identifies that loop's purpose as sending each converted compatibility event; the stripped binary does not independently name the nested future or `Session::send_event_raw` call.

This is a source-correlated static mapping, not observation of emitted events. The event item layout/type attribution comes from the direct conversion call and matching element stride plus the pinned source; the nested future's exact Rust type and dynamic dispatch remain unresolved. No live desktop callback or session event was captured. Offsets are specific to the executable identity recorded above.

## V2 child cold-resume configuration

`ensure_v2_agent_loaded` has two materially different resume callers. Both require registered child metadata, a readable stored thread, loadable model-context history, and a resumed history marked V2. They reuse the existing thread ID and rollout; they do not create a replacement child. A caller that supplies the live parent additionally has to prove that the parent is still the same registered running V2 thread in the same runtime registry, and that source/history/stored parent IDs agree.

With a live parent, resume configuration is rebuilt from a fresh parent turn. The child’s stored reasoning effort and stored model/provider are then restored; a saved agent role is reapplied while the current runtime approval policy, cwd, and permission-profile snapshot are preserved. The controller rejects a changed parent execution policy. Cached environment selections must match a currently ready parent environment by environment ID, cwd, and workspace roots. Pending/failed selections are rejected; config differences are rejected after substituting the current owner permission profile. For local executors, a differing cached permission profile is intersected with current owner authority; a remote executor with changed permissions is rejected. This is a constrained resume, not blind restoration of the child’s old authority.

The sender-driven `send_message`/`followup_task` path passes no live parent object to the loader. It retains the supplied resume config, restores stored child model/provider/reasoning, and attempts to inherit environment and execution-policy handles by following the saved `ThreadSpawn` parent source if that parent is loaded. The exec-policy handle is inherited only if the parent/child policy compatibility predicate still passes. This path supplies no `client_mcp_extensions` from a parent object. Parent instructions come from a loaded parent when available, otherwise from the shared thread-instructions provider. Therefore “cold resume” alone does not identify one configuration policy; caller path and which parent/runtime state is available matter.

Sources: [`ensure_v2_agent_loaded`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs), [`inherited_environments_for_source` and `inherited_exec_policy_for_source`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs). This is static source analysis; no child was unloaded/reloaded in a live runtime, and the installed app's build provenance remains unverified.

## Multi-agent version selection

In the inspected tag, a configured `features.multi_agent_v2` override selects V2. An explicit global agent disable selects Disabled; otherwise the config override is absent. For a spawned thread, the controller next attempts to inherit the version from its parent/source or the resumed/forked history, and only then falls back to feature configuration (`collab` => V1, otherwise Disabled). Resumed history created before version metadata existed is treated as V1. At turn setup, model metadata can also supply a version when no thread version is already set, but explicit config override takes precedence; the session then records its selected version.

This explains why finding V2 handler strings in the executable does not establish that a particular conversation used V2: app feature/config state, model metadata, inherited parent/history version, and session initialization all participate. The local static artifact does not reveal the resolved values for the investigated session. Sources: [`Config` version precedence](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs), [`effective_multi_agent_version_for_spawn`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager.rs), and [`resolve_multi_agent_version`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs). Static source analysis only.

## V2 tool exposure defaults and gates

The source `MultiAgentV2Config::default()` sets `wait_agent_enabled=true`, `disable_direct_message=false`, `hide_spawn_agent_metadata=true`, `expose_spawn_agent_model_overrides=true`, and `non_code_mode_only=true`; resolved config may override these values. During tool-plan construction, V2 collaboration handlers are registered only when the turn passes `collab_tools_enabled` and V2 is enabled. With `non_code_mode_only=true`, the registered tools use `ToolExposure::DirectModelOnly`; setting it false selects `ToolExposure::Direct`. `disable_direct_message` omits both `send_message` and `followup_task`; `wait_agent_enabled` independently gates `wait_agent`. Spawn schema construction separately gates agent-type and model/reasoning fields using role/config flags.

These are source defaults and registration rules, not proof of the UI/model-visible tool set in the installed session. Sources: [`MultiAgentV2Config`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs), [`add_collaboration_tools`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs), and [`create_spawn_agent_tool_v2`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs). Static source analysis only.

## V2 collaboration tool exposure and model gating

`multi_agent_v2_enabled` means the turn's resolved `MultiAgentVersion` is V2. The separate `collab_tools_enabled` check is stricter for non-root V2 agents: it allows collaboration tools when the session source has no agent path, or when the current model metadata explicitly declares V2 support. Thus a child turn can have V2 session state while its spawn/message/management tools are omitted if its selected model is not marked V2-capable. V1 instead applies the next-spawn-depth limit.

V2 registration uses `DirectModelOnly` when `non_code_mode_only` is true, otherwise `Direct`. In the tool contract, the former enters the ordinary model-visible spec list but is excluded from the nested Code Mode set; the latter can participate in both surfaces when Code Mode is active. This is an exposure rule, not a claim about an actual conversation's resolved config. `disable_direct_message` removes `send_message` and `followup_task`, while `wait_agent_enabled` independently controls `wait_agent`; `interrupt_agent` and `list_agents` remain registered when collaboration tools pass the gate. `can_manage_children` is derived from whether the required tools for that selected version are exposed by the finalized router. For external sessions with direct messaging disabled, router construction also requires the configured message-board `post` tool to be available; otherwise the tool plan errors. Internal sessions skip that validation.

Sources: [`collab_tools_enabled`, `add_collaboration_tools`, and child-management tool requirements](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs), [`ToolExposure`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/tools/src/tool_executor.rs), and [`ToolRouter`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/router.rs). Static source analysis only; the inspected installed session's model metadata and resolved exposure config remain unknown.

Model selection uses a distinct predicate: for V2 it excludes only presets explicitly marked `MultiAgentVersion::Disabled`, so metadata `None` or `V1` is not excluded by this check. The child tool-plan gate, however, requires exactly `Some(V2)` for a non-root child. Therefore availability in the spawn model picker is not evidence that the child will receive recursive collaboration tools. This is a source-level difference between catalog eligibility and runtime tool registration; the actual preset metadata in the investigated session is unknown. Sources: [`model_supports_multi_agent_backend` and model override validation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/child_config.rs), [`spawn_agent_models_description`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs), and [`collab_tools_enabled`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs).

## V2 recursive depth and resource limits

The inspected `agent_max_depth` check is applied in V1 collaboration-tool eligibility and legacy spawn/resume handlers. The V2 `collab_tools_enabled` branch has no depth comparison; a non-root V2 child can retain collaboration tools when its current model metadata is V2. Its `ThreadSpawn` source still increments and records depth, but in this path that value is not used as a V2 maximum-depth gate.

V2 uses separate resource bounds. `max_concurrent_threads_per_session` feeds the V2 effective agent-thread capacity after subtracting the root thread. Spawn reserves a V2 residency slot against that capacity, while a session-scoped execution limiter separately bounds active V2 child turns; the spawn path also calls the execution-capacity check before creation. For V2-resident spawns, the registry spawn reservation is not given a second max-thread limit. This supports “no explicit V2 depth cap in the inspected tool gate; bounded by residency/execution capacity,” not “unlimited concurrent execution” or “the entire historical tree is capped at that many children.” Sources: [`collab_tools_enabled`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs), [`effective_agent_max_threads`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs), [`spawn_agent_internal`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs), [`execution limiter`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/execution.rs), and [`V2 residency`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs). Static source analysis only; actual session capacities and runtime behavior were not measured.

The source default is `max_concurrent_threads_per_session = 4`, yielding an effective capacity of three child threads after the root subtraction. An explicitly configured `features.multi_agent_v2.max_concurrent_threads_per_session` takes precedence over the legacy `agents.max_concurrent_threads_per_session` value, which is translated by adding one for the root before feeding the shared V2 capacity. The default is not evidence that this installed session used four/three; its resolved config was not observed. Source: [`resolve_multi_agent_v2_config` and `effective_agent_max_threads`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs).

## Interrupted status, completion, and residency

The V2 status mapper classifies both an explicit interrupt abort and a budget-limited abort as `Interrupted`. This status is deliberately non-final for status-wait logic, and completion-message formatting returns no ordinary completion message for `Interrupted`. By contrast, a completed turn can emit a `Completed` activity and a separate queue-only result message. Thus an interrupted child is not equivalent to a completed child whose result is empty.

Residency uses a separate predicate: `Completed`, `Errored`, and `Interrupted` are all unloadable when there is no active turn, durable sleep, or trigger-turn mailbox item. “Not final” therefore does not mean “must stay resident.” Manual interrupt also does not close the persisted spawn edge; a later trigger-turn follow-up can reload the same V2 child if its history and current resume checks pass. These statements describe different state surfaces—agent status, completion delivery, in-memory residency, and graph-edge lifecycle—and should not be collapsed into one done/running label.

Sources: [`agent/status.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/status.rs), [`session_prefix.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session_prefix.rs), [`control/completion.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs), and [`control/residency.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs). Static source analysis only; no live interruption/eviction/reload experiment was performed.

For a completed child turn, V2 may route the `Completed` activity to the agent that initiated the trigger-turn request when its recorded `initiating_agent_path` differs from the child’s structural parent path. The completion result communication is separately sent to the structural `parent_thread_id` with `trigger_turn=false`. The initiating path is populated from the author of trigger-turn inter-agent input, or from turn-start options; the input queue carries it into turn metadata. This separates “who requested this turn” activity attribution from parent-child result delivery, and the result still does not start an idle parent turn. Sources: [`tasks/mod.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tasks/mod.rs), [`session/turn_input.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn_input.rs), and [`control/completion.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs). Static source only; no nested live-agent interaction was run.

## Application bundle coverage

REA's `analyze-javascript-application` completed static parsing of 16,240 source files in the installed `app.asar` and reconstructed its application/Electron graph. The ASAR manifest declared SHA-256 `5f2f66c4fe19493bba7ca2e0c322ed256e39f66d4a58865875431c2ddd4d58a1` for the unpacked `node_modules/@worklouder/device-kit-oai/node_modules/@worklouder/wl-device-kit/dist/native/darwin/permissions.node`, while the companion file hashed to `6096151ba2644b594b0e0f3098e898c6a564b57371658f9fd80327bfa641d313`. Analysis continued under `record-and-continue`, so that file is explicitly untrusted.

Large startup bundles hit semantic-analysis result/heap constraints. The analyzer retained static/module facts for affected files, but the CLI's per-file limitation array exceeded the output transport and was truncated. This whole-app pass therefore does not add a verified subagent-specific UI call path; the UI claims above remain bounded to the individually inspected resources. This was static package analysis only, with no app execution or network observation.

## Targeted Codex subagent UI resources

After the whole-app pass, five files were extracted individually from the same `app.asar` and inspected. SHA-256 values below identify the extracted bytes; the files themselves are not redistributed.

| ASAR resource | SHA-256 | Static observation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `webview/assets/local-conversation-subagents-panel-tab-2ab06de7bd1d.js` | `345278adc9f056901c6e0b8d64c98b09d6d3e12019c2ec20be8567b100196eef` | Renders active/done groups and child detail/transcript view from local conversation state. |
| `webview/assets/open-local-conversation-subagents-panel-5abfac9190b9.js` | `676ecbb8172e729e5a77dc4f799cbca2f38f0aaf986bf44da9424f92e9237491` | Opens a durable Subagents panel route with parent id and optional selected child id; hydrates history as needed. |
| `webview/assets/subagents-6af1a27d0027.js` | `706c241e8bfb4399746842434ea79137889a05213f28990ed354171383d04db2` | Populates a thumbnail from `getSubagentDescendantSnapshot(parentConversationId)`. |
| `webview/assets/verify-descendant-d02f27822163.js` | `dc7baa2e1adc9b3b469f2e766c3b88ea25cdecd098534a9e39ee7c2b88596554` | Checks ancestry to the requested parent, rejects cycles and host mismatch, and hydrates missing ancestry/transcript data. |
| `webview/assets/chatgpt-subagent-final-response-query-b2b6816e81df.js` | `43b96d88ea4b71bdda82e04d2e137531c0ce726e17559de7bde9be018d9d5012` | Queries `/flora/subagent/thread/turns` with parent id, child id and `limit: 1`; selects the last non-empty agent message, preferring `phase=final_answer`. |
| `webview/assets/app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` | `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245` | Contains the `SubagentTopology` snapshot/discovery implementation used by the local subagents UI. |
| `webview/assets/subagents-525abfd8ce99.js` | `d7e724ae5d94e68233966a03fb35e3291c39d8d80b633673bf139991f360a7cd` | Work tab thumbnail reads subagent state from conversation state; selected-child preview uses the final-response query and truncates displayed preview text to 1,000 characters. |
| `webview/assets/chatgpt-subagents-panel-e608a4ff5e56.js` | `406de5e3b812c5fb5a60ab34c01085e829d13c69fec6eb0d11c22e830d231442` | Work right-side panel groups waiting/working and done/failed separately; done child detail retrieves final response, while failed child detail renders status message or unavailable state. |

The two Work UI resources were rechecked for a stop/close path. The subagents panel exposes list selection and a back-to-list callback, and renders child status, delegated task, and final response. The thumbnail renders status and a child preview. Neither extracted module contains `interrupt_agent`, `close_agent`, shutdown calls, or a corresponding action callback; this is bounded to these modules and does not rule out controls elsewhere in the app. In the same-version source, V2 `interrupt_agent` dispatches `Op::Interrupt`, returns the pre-dispatch status, emits Interrupted activity, and does not close the persisted spawn edge. Legacy `close_agent` closes the edge and stops the target's live descendants. Thus the inspected Work panel is evidenced as a status/result view, not as the caller of either source lifecycle operation. No UI action or runtime request was executed.

The same bundle's shared conversation manager does contain a root-stop cascade. After `interruptConversationSelf` succeeds (or no expected turn ID was supplied), `interruptConversation` schedules descendant cleanup in the background for `user-stop`; other eligible non-follower sources await cleanup. The helper first interrupts active descendants in its current snapshot, then rediscovers the subtree and handles still-active descendants not already processed. It tries a local conversation interruption first; otherwise it reads the most recent turn and sends `turn/interrupt` only when that turn is `inProgress`. Recursive `descendant-cleanup` calls suppress another cascade. Durable `thread/stop` success also triggers descendant cleanup. By contrast, the app-server's matching `turn/interrupt` handler loads only the named thread, validates its active turn ID, and submits one `Op::Interrupt`; it does not traverse descendants. This is client-side best-effort orchestration, not an atomic server tree shutdown. User stop may return before background cleanup finishes. This finding is static bundle/source evidence only; no stop action or remote-host request was observed.

The desktop client cleanup callback runs multiple descendants with `Promise.all`; errors for individual children and discovery failures are logged and do not change the direct per-thread interrupt API contract. A result of null from `interruptConversationSelf` does not count a child as handled, so the helper can fall back to inspecting its latest turn. Descendants from a snapshot can also change between enumeration and interrupt. The report therefore describes the algorithm's attempts and conditions, not a guarantee every running child is stopped.

The topology owner is `SubagentTopology` in the same app-shared bundle. On the local host it pages `thread/list` with `sourceKinds: ["subAgentThreadSpawn"]`, `useStateDbOnly: true`, and version-appropriate `ancestorThreadId` / `parentThreadId` filters (limit 200). Where those filters are unavailable, it combines known in-memory spawn activities with history-derived `subAgentActivity.started` and `spawnAgent` receiver IDs from `thread/items/list` or, for unloaded paginated history, `thread/turns/list`; it reads child threads and checks parent identity. Child hydration is bounded to concurrency 2. Discovery tracks `isComplete` and sets it false on cursor anomalies, failed reads, or missing/mismatched children. The stop helper consumes only `descendantThreads` and does not inspect `isComplete`, so an incomplete but non-throwing discovery can still cause interrupts for found children while leaving undiscovered descendants untouched. This is static bundle evidence; no discovery failure was injected.

The local-owner self-interrupt path (`Ien`) selects the latest in-progress turn and returns null when a supplied `expectedTurnId` no longer matches it. With an active turn it sends one `turn/interrupt`, updates the matching local turn status, and schedules cleanup of that turn's Node REPL executions; for `user-stop`, that cleanup is not awaited. With no active turn, it attempts background terminal cleanup and returns null, which lets the descendant helper fall back to the latest persisted turn lookup. This is client-side turn identity and execution cleanup behavior, not thread closure; no runtime race was exercised.

A stream follower forwards `thread-follower-interrupt-turn` over desktop IPC to the current owner, whose manager performs the interrupt and descendant cascade; the follower-side `finally` skips its own cascade while it remains a follower. If owner forwarding fails with timeout, no-client, or request-version-mismatch, the follower path clears the matching stale stream ownership, marks the thread `needs_resume`, and falls back to local interruption. With ownership cleared, the outer interrupt path is no longer follower-gated and attempts descendant cleanup locally. This control-flow conclusion is static and does not establish multi-window runtime race behavior.

The bundle's `stopThread` path has a separate caller from the conversation interrupt path. `CHn` calls the manager's `stopThread(..., "user")`, but its only operational callsite in this bundle is in the pause-restricted-runtime branch. That branch is reached from `beforeArchiveConversation`; for a matching subagent it first attempts a runtime pause, and when the pause succeeds with `interruptActiveTurn` enabled it stops the restricted thread and reports the interrupted turn through the user-interruption callback. For a durable subagent, the archive hook instead interrupts descendants. Separately, the manager's `stopThread` method uses `thread/stop` on durable hosts and falls back to `interruptConversation(..., "user-stop")` when the method is unavailable or the thread is non-durable. These distinct paths explain why the internal name `stopThread` alone does not identify the ordinary composer Stop action. This is static call-graph evidence; the React composer click path has not yet been located or exercised.

These client resources indicate that descendant discovery, child identity validation, history hydration, and final-response retrieval are distinct steps. The panel can show a child as done while its final response is still loading. This does not establish that a live session executed the code, nor does it reveal the server-side endpoint implementation or authorization rules. To reproduce extraction on macOS, use `npx --yes @electron/asar extract-file <app.asar> <resource-path>` and hash the resulting file with `shasum -a 256`. The final-response query uses infinite stale time and does not refetch on window focus. A missing-response error retries at most twice with randomized backoff capped at one second; retry-on-mount is disabled while the child turn is live. These are client query policies only and do not establish endpoint readiness or server retry behavior.

The separate Work thumbnail module consumes the same conversation-scoped subagent state. It displays up to four children in the thumbnail and, when a child is selected, queries the cached final response and limits the preview excerpt to 1,000 characters. The Work panel groups waiting/working under Active and done/failed under Done, with distinct row limits; detail rendering for a completed child performs final-response retrieval, while failed children use their status message or an unavailable fallback. A nearby `hosted-thread-launch` asset is a generic MCP connector/resource opener that sends `tools/call`, `tools/list`, or `resources/read` requests to `/ps/mcp`; inspection found no subagent spawn path in that module. Similar naming alone is not evidence that it implements Codex subagent launch.

### Local desktop panel entry point

The extracted `local-conversation-page-a83a1af2c24b.js` wires one `onOpenSubagentsPanel` callback into both the thread summary popover and the utility bar. The local thread handler closes over the current `conversationId` and resolved `hostId`. The collapsed background-subagents summary row exposes an “Open subagents” action when the callback is present; the utility-bar section is rendered from filtered background-agent state. Clicking an individual child avatar routes through the thread-page handler: `canInteract` children use the direct background-agent opener, while non-interactable children call the panel opener with `hostId`, the current parent ID, the selected child ID, and display name. The panel opener stores the selected child in its durable route and validates/hydrates it as described above. This is static client call-path evidence; it does not establish visibility for every UI state or runtime behavior.

Relevant extracted assets: `local-conversation-page-a83a1af2c24b.js`, `local-conversation-thread-c8c5e92ea89e.js`, `open-local-conversation-subagents-panel-5abfac9190b9.js`; all are within app-shared bundle SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`.

The `canInteract` field is assembled in `app-primary-6f60e9ac4103.js` from indexed references. A `collabAgentToolCall(spawnAgent)` sets it true; later collab tool references independently carry forward a prior true value. The special `resumeAgent`/previous-`closeAgent` condition affects the merged tool field, not `canInteract`. A `subAgentActivity` reference preserves the prior value only when its parent-turn key matches; otherwise it falls back to false. The UI uses this field to choose the direct background-agent opener versus the Subagents panel. This establishes client routing semantics only, not server authorization or current runtime status.

The direct background-agent opener separately activates the child thread summary with `addToRecent: false`, ensures its first page is loaded, then creates or reuses a durable `background-agent` tab keyed by the child conversation ID. The Subagents opener instead creates/updates a right-side `subagents` panel tab and stores the selected child ID in its route. These are distinct client navigation containers; neither establishes runtime execution by itself.

The shared thread renderer makes the interaction split concrete. `canInteract: false` maps to a read-only thread and hides the composer; when true and there is no writer conflict, the composer is shown. The resume-state hook receives the child ID only when both `shouldResume` and `canInteract` are true and the thread is available through the local or shared-thread path. The renderer has separate unavailable, resume-failed, and writer-conflict UI branches. The Subagents detail explicitly passes both flags as false, so it does not resume through this renderer path and does not show the composer. This is evidence of client behavior, not proof that a backend accepts input or resume requests.

The installed `use-resume-conversation-if-needed` module calls the shared session client's `resumeConversation` with the child conversation ID, thread access, current collaboration mode, workspace roots, permission default, and any known catalog entry. It passes null model and reasoning-effort overrides and disables thread-goal resume confirmation. Error handling distinguishes a missing shared thread, an archived session, a writer conflict, and ordinary resume failures. Ordinary retryable failures use a backoff path initialized at 750 ms, doubling up to 60 seconds, with a retry counter threshold of 5. When the child is bound, the hook dispatches `subagent-thread-full-fidelity-changed` enable/disable messages through the desktop bridge. This documents client intent and control flow; it does not prove request success or backend implementation.

The earlier negative result applied only to the extracted `webview/assets` corpus. A follow-up inspection of the installed ChatGPT app's Electron main and app-server bundles found the receiver. The main-process bridge handles `subagent-thread-full-fidelity-changed` by calling the selected host connection's `setSubagentThreadFullFidelityForSurface(threadId, surfaceId, enabled)`; the surface ID is the bridge recipient's `e.id`. The app-server connection stores a set of enabled surface IDs per thread; disabling removes only that surface, and destroying a window removes its surface from every thread entry. Multiple surfaces can therefore keep full-fidelity enabled for the same child.

The sender hook's separate effect watches shared state keyed by child ID. When a host mapping exists and a child-state selector is non-null, it sends `enabled: true`; the effect cleanup sends `enabled: false`. The renderer calls this hook only when `canInteract` and `shouldResume` are both true. This connects the client-side child renderer lifetime to the surface registration, but does not prove that resume succeeds or that opening every UI route registers a surface.

The app-server connection recognizes a child from `thread/started` only when `source.subAgent.thread_spawn.parent_thread_id` is a string. Full-fidelity surface IDs are stored in a set per child thread, which allows independent enable/disable bookkeeping and cleanup when a window is destroyed. However, the filter checks only whether any surface is registered for that child; it does not scope the notification to the registering surface. Once allowed, the notification goes through `broadcastToWindows`; the inspected helper iterates every live window listener and sends the payload to each. Thus one registered surface opens connection-wide broadcast of the five high-volume delta methods for that child, and the per-surface set is bookkeeping rather than recipient scoping. Without any registered surface, internal notification callbacks and the host event handler still run before the webview broadcast filter, which drops exactly: `item/agentMessage/delta`, `item/plan/delta`, `item/reasoning/summaryTextDelta`, `item/reasoning/textDelta`, and `item/commandExecution/outputDelta`. This is static evidence from one installed build, not a runtime trace. It does not show server-side generation or transport suppression, and it does not show that internal consumers miss notifications. The inspected app-server connection initializes `subagentThreadIds` as an empty per-connection `Set`, and the only insertion site in the extracted connection bundle adds an ID while processing `thread/started` when `source.subAgent.thread_spawn.parent_thread_id` is a string. Disposal clears the set. No explicit snapshot/list/history backfill into this filter set was found in the inspected module. This narrows the client-side finding: the bundle shows no proactive preload path, but it remains unknown whether transport/server initialization replays `thread/started` for pre-existing child threads after reconnect. In `src-BJv5Mk7n.js` (SHA-256 `72e2b366d574206f76fc079c79b64c31ce378970a797475ded8709908d338f08`), initialization is near byte offset `1189433`, disposal/clear near `1219027`, and the notification insertion/filter path near `1252395–1252969`. Static bundle evidence only; no reconnect was triggered.

### Full-fidelity receiver artifact identity

Static observations are from the locally installed ChatGPT `26.1007.30912` (build `20156`). The `app.asar` SHA-256 is `75e397e2280dde93b70a85aa926f4282c728eacc84dfeb15ba7fb6ddd84628af`. In `/.vite/build/main-CqEoQDOU.js` (SHA-256 `09d19386e1f23cf4ccbc950fc40d85a1e8a064a08be588b2a4be6a89f6ada9e6`), the bridge receiver string is at byte offset `2104281`. In `/.vite/build/src-BJv5Mk7n.js` (SHA-256 `72e2b366d574206f76fc079c79b64c31ce378970a797475ded8709908d338f08`), the broadcast helper `OR` is at `1146245`, `broadcastToWindows` at `1151010`, the surface-registration method at `1202228`, and the filter marker at `1257229`. The sender module `webview/assets/use-resume-conversation-if-needed-cd15159177f0.js` has SHA-256 `2299468dc1ce305855a9fc93cd9d11c2956b60023563d8360483ee2d44a818f4`, matching the previously extracted webview resource. These identities locate one installed build's static code; they do not identify a Codex source commit or establish behavior across platforms and releases.

The direct tab renderer has a separate history query keyed by `(hostId, threadId)`. If cached background-history data is unavailable, it calls `loadBackgroundThreadHistoryPage(threadId, {prioritize: true})`. It passes `canInteract` and `shouldResume` to the transcript renderer only when the thread is not archived and the tab props do not disable them. The Subagents panel detail renderer explicitly passes both interaction and resume as false. These are client-side render and query paths, not evidence of successful runtime requests, authorization, or actual interaction.

### Direct background-agent tab resume default

The installed `open-local-conversation-background-agent-906b366ceacd.js` opener defaults `shouldResume` to the negation of the shared `isDurableHost(hostId)` predicate, then carries that value into the durable `background-agent` tab and any nested background-agent open. In the matching `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js`, the predicate is `hostId === "durable"`; this is a host-kind check, not a local-versus-remote check. Therefore the opener's default is enabled for non-durable host IDs and disabled for the literal durable host ID, unless the caller supplies an explicit value. The shared thread renderer still requires its interaction/availability gates before passing the child ID to `use-resume-conversation-if-needed`; archived tabs also disable the props. This identifies an eligible client resume path for interactive child tabs, distinct from the read-only Subagents panel detail. It does not prove that a user action triggered `thread/resume`, that the hook's other state conditions were met, or that the request succeeded. Static evidence from the installed ChatGPT `26.1007.30912` build only.

### SubagentTopology static path

The extracted `app-shared` bundle exposes a store facade: `getSubagentDescendantSnapshot(parentId)` returns currently known descendants, `isComplete: false`, and an `isLoading` flag based on in-flight discovery. Discovery is separate and can reconcile its result with a snapshot of runtime status captured before the asynchronous reads. Full-subtree and direct-child discovery use separate in-flight maps and reuse matching work.

The local panel and its thumbnail subscribe to `getSubagentDescendantSnapshot`; the getter returns the current store view and does not call `discover`. The inspected panel route opener also does not call `discoverSubagentDescendantSnapshot`. When a selected child or ancestry is missing, the opener can hydrate the parent (including turns) and load a background history page for the child. This restores the selected conversation's display/history data; it is a separate path from discovering the parent's descendant topology. The runtime item-event handler separately invokes `discoverForSpawn` for spawn calls and started activity items. These are explicit static call paths; this analysis does not establish whether a history hydration can indirectly coincide with other runtime notifications.

- With thread-list filters supported, discovery queries `thread/list` for `sourceKinds: ["subAgentThreadSpawn"]`, `archived: false`, `sortKey: "created_at"`, descending, `limit: 200`, and `useStateDbOnly: true`. At app-server versions at least `0.143.0-alpha.16`, it uses `ancestorThreadId`; on older versions, it queries each `parentThreadId` and walks descendants. Paging stops at the parent's creation-time boundary and repeated cursors make the result incomplete.
- Without thread-list filters, observed spawn IDs are supplemented from history. `thread/items/list` extracts `subAgentActivity(started)` and `collabAgentToolCall(spawnAgent)` IDs. For a paginated-history thread not yet cached, `thread/turns/list` is scanned page by page to reconstruct child IDs from turn items.
- Candidate children not already known are loaded with `thread/read(includeTurns: false)`. A result is accepted only if its ID matches the requested child and its parent matches the current node. At most two children are fetched concurrently. Failures, identity mismatches, or incomplete nested-history reads preserve `isComplete: false`.
- When a spawn event identifies exactly one child already in the store with a matching parent, the app first hydrates that child in the background and discovers its subtree. Otherwise it falls back to discovering the parent's full descendant set.
- The runtime item-event path calls `discoverForSpawn` when it receives `collabAgentToolCall(spawnAgent)` or `subAgentActivity(started)`. This is an event-driven discovery trigger distinct from the UI's read of the current store snapshot; static code does not establish that opening the panel always launches a fresh query.

These are client-side static observations. The bundle does not reveal server persistence, filtering implementation, authorization, or whether each host uses the same route. `isComplete` describes discovery coverage in this client algorithm; it is not evidence that all remotely existing subagents are known.

### Activity item path into desktop state

Core's `AgentControl::emit_sub_agent_activity` constructs one `TurnItem::SubAgentActivity` and emits `EventMsg::ItemStarted` followed immediately by `EventMsg::ItemCompleted`. In app-server v2, these canonical item events are mapped to typed `item/started` and `item/completed` notifications. The v2 event path explicitly skips the deprecated `EventMsg::SubAgentActivity` lifecycle event; that legacy event remains available to raw-event and rollout consumers. The app-shared notification dispatcher routes the typed notifications to its item handler, which inserts or updates the conversation turn. After processing a completed `collabAgentToolCall(spawnAgent)` or `subAgentActivity(started)` item, it invokes `SubagentTopology.discoverForSpawn`.

The activity item is not limited to creation and completion: the V2 tool handlers emit `started` after spawn, `interacted` after message delivery, and `interrupted` after a successful interrupt request. Completion routing emits `completed` on the initiating thread when it can resolve that initiator, otherwise on the structural parent. It separately routes the completion result to the structural parent as inter-agent communication. Thus an activity is a record in the caller/initiator's turn, while the affected child is identified inside the item; those identities can differ in nested collaboration.

In the installed app-shared projection, `subAgentActivity` becomes a `subagent-activity` UI item with the affected thread ID, a display name derived from the last non-root path segment, `isMessage` true only for `interacted`, and display statuses `started → active`, `interacted → updated`, `interrupted → interrupted`, `completed → completed`. The activity is included only when the host's background-subagents flag is enabled and its `agentPath` passes the current projection filter. This is a presentation mapping, not the authoritative child-thread status; the separate topology/thread snapshot drives the active/done grouping. In particular, `interrupt_agent` emits an `interrupted` activity after its interrupt operation succeeds and returns the status observed before dispatch; its contract says the agent remains available for later messages/follow-up tasks. So the activity records an accepted interrupt operation, not proof that the thread reached a terminal state.

This explains the client path from activity emission to state update and descendant discovery. The `started`, `interacted`, `interrupted`, and `completed` business activities are each represented as instantaneous item lifecycles, so protocol item completion must not be read as the child having finished. Completion activity is written into the initiating thread's turn when resolvable (falling back to the structural parent); the child result is delivered separately to the structural parent. No desktop notification stream was captured, and the bundle does not establish server persistence or behavior on every host. Sources: [`AgentControl::emit_sub_agent_activity`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs), [spawn activity](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs), [interaction activity](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/message_tool.rs), [interrupt activity](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/interrupt_agent.rs), [completion routing](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs), [app-server event handling](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/app-server/src/bespoke_event_handling.rs), [v2 event mapping](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/app-server-protocol/src/protocol/event_mapping.rs), and [v2 item conversion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/app-server-protocol/src/protocol/v2/item.rs).

### Renderer handling after full-fidelity IPC broadcast

Followed the admitted delta into the inspected renderer bundle `webview/assets/app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`). The notification dispatcher handles item notifications through `Ktn` and then calls `events.emitNotification` (byte offset `2884087`; `Ktn` callsite at `2885455`, definition at `2821429`). `Ktn` passes the event to that renderer's `itemStreamState.handleNotification`.

The `itemStreamState` handler (class begins at byte offset `2843620`) maps `threadId` to `conversationId` using `Y(threadId)`; the bundle defines `Y(e)` as identity. It queues text deltas with `turnId`, `itemId`, target type, and delta. Command output uses a separate queue with `flushIntervalMs: 50`; text uses the renderer scheduler/frame queue. Queue construction begins at byte offset `2846879`, `applyOutputDeltas` at `2847234`, and `applyFrameTextDeltas` at `2849058`; the `Z0t` guard counts canonical history-island entries (or legacy turns) at byte offset `2518740`.

At flush, both paths group by conversation ID, look up that conversation in the receiving renderer's `threadStore.conversations`, and skip the group when the conversation is absent or its canonical history-island/turn count is zero. Text application resolves the relevant turn and updates only matching item IDs/types; output application updates a matching `commandExecution` item, while a missing item is dropped with a debug log. This establishes a thread-scoped state-update gate after the connection-wide IPC broadcast. It does not establish that every renderer has the conversation loaded, that every delta mutates state, or that a resulting state change is visibly rendered. Static bundle inspection only; no multi-window runtime trace was captured.

### Inline activity group helper

Followed the turn renderer's `da` import into the shared resource `webview/assets/sites-end-resource-78b98c374310.js` (SHA-256 `4897e9b701726b0874a5036157ea32eb85932a04669ab9e205b820623f8be4a3`). Exported helper `bE` filters background-agent snapshot rows to the current `turnKey`, excludes IDs from `excludedSubagentThreadIds`, and requires a usable display name. It merges those rows with activity groups, deduplicating by conversation ID; activity groups retain their anchor, while unrepresented current-turn children are appended as an unanchored group. With no activity groups, a standalone group can still be produced from the snapshot.

The helper reconciles display names/status from snapshot, current-turn, and later activity data. Group status precedence is interrupted, updated, all children completed, then active. `Mc` builds the inline group; row component `wE` creates a button with an “Open … subagent” label and open callback only when contextual `canOpen(row)` is true, otherwise it creates a non-button row. This is static element-construction evidence, not proof that a particular session rendered the button or that it was clicked. The helper begins near byte offset `248649`; offsets are valid only for the exact resource hash above. No runtime UI, DOM, service authorization, or live child status was verified.

### Inline row click target resolution

Followed `Mc`'s context callback into the local-thread context in `local-conversation-thread-c8c5e92ea89e.js` (SHA-256 `ee192ab3f19d493d64580f7690c94b05c589fe59c1757984e1c5a076976a47eb`). Its `open` handler resolves the child through `open-target-622f980eb330.js` before invoking a supplied opener or the default background-agent opener. The default opener activates the child thread summary with `addToRecent: false`, then routes to the background-agent tab or the host thread opener according to window mode.

The target resolver checks the parent's background-agent snapshot, then parent history for spawn/activity evidence, then child ancestry followed by history validation. A matching `collabAgentToolCall(spawnAgent)` requires the parent sender and receiver ID and validates known parent identity; it returns `canInteract: true`. A matching `subAgentActivity(started)` alone returns `canInteract: false` and derives the display name from the activity path. The separate `canOpen` predicate allows a row when the child is present in the snapshot or the parent history contains a spawn/start record. A click still resolves the target first, and no navigation occurs when resolution returns null. Thus row clickability, verified child identity, and interactivity are separate conditions.

`open-target-622f980eb330.js` SHA-256: `04a527af414610472558067a99e6827f4ae73c732f0f5b2ee4b5101d66a5719d`. Navigation aids: resolver begins near byte offset `260`; thread context construction near `315432`; default opener near `181440`; turn context consumer in `local-conversation-turn-632904a8790c.js` (SHA-256 `f16c75de8c65e6849f3976cc8e0efec198e7b063ec81ce514ff85b348967b376`) near `57360`. Static call-graph evidence only; no click/navigation or server authorization was exercised.

### Work subagents panel and final-response query

Inspected `chatgpt-subagents-panel-e608a4ff5e56.js` (SHA-256 `406de5e3b812c5fb5a60ab34c01085e829d13c69fec6eb0d11c22e830d231442`). The Work panel reads the current conversation's subagent collection and partitions `status === "done"` into Done; every other status remains in Active. Selecting a completed child creates a final-response query keyed by parent conversation ID, child thread ID, and activation ID. The query is enabled only when those three identifiers are present and calls `safeGet("/flora/subagent/thread/turns", {conversationId, threadId, limit: 1})`.

The response parser scans the returned turn's items from the end: it prefers the first nonempty `agentMessage` with `phase === "final_answer"`; if none exists, it falls back to the newest nonempty agent message. When no response is available yet, the query raises a specific retryable error, allows two retries with a short capped delay, disables refetch-on-window-focus, and marks successful data infinitely fresh. The panel shows a loading/unavailable state when neither fetched text nor the snapshot's `statusMessage` is available. These are client query and presentation rules; they do not establish endpoint authorization, server data selection, or when the endpoint becomes populated.

The local conversation panel is a separate presentation path in `local-conversation-subagents-panel-tab-2ab06de7bd1d.js` (SHA-256 `345278adc9f056901c6e0b8d64c98b09d6d3e12019c2ec20be8567b100196eef`): it also separates `done` from non-`done`, but its detail opens a local transcript component with `canInteract` derived from the selected row rather than using the Work panel's final-response query. This confirms that similarly named panels do not imply identical data loading or interaction semantics. Navigation aids: Work panel selection/detail/list mapping near byte offsets `537`, `2554`, and `3186`; row projection near `4991`; local panel detail and list grouping near `615` and `2232`; final response parser/query near `169` and `633`. Static extracted-bundle evidence only; no panel was opened and no endpoint was called.

### Work panel status is projected from conversation message metadata

Resolved the Work panel's `p` import to `vin` in `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`). The selector returns null unless input messages carry `codex_sub_agent_activity` or `codex_collab_agent_tool_call` metadata; it schema-parses those records and returns child rows, per-thread activation IDs, and activity groups. The panel consumes this projection's `subagents` list and uses its activation IDs to key the separate final-response query. This establishes that panel status and final response have distinct client-side sources.

The reducer creates child rows from activity path/thread IDs and collaboration-tool receiver IDs. `pendingInit` maps to waiting; running maps to working; completed/interrupted/shutdown map to done; errored/notFound map to failed. Spawn prompt text is retained when present. `spawnAgent`, `sendInput`, or `resumeAgent` activity can mark receivers working; a completed `wait` marks currently waiting/working children done. For a live parent turn, children whose last event precedes the latest user-message boundary are treated as done; when the parent turn is not live, remaining waiting/working rows are also closed as done. Activity records of kind completed/interrupted independently set their child row to done. These are client projection rules over supplied messages, not proof of backend lifecycle state.

The `vin` projection begins near byte offset `1786793`; status reduction in `yin` begins near `1787726`; activity-group projection in `bin` begins near `1789278`; metadata parsing and provider-status mapping are nearby (`Sin` near `1790359`, `Ein` near `1791077`). The app-initial hash identifies the exact extracted resource. Static bundle evidence only; no live conversation selector values or panel render were captured.

### Work detail selection is branch-projection-bound

In `chatgpt-subagents-panel-e608a4ff5e56.js` (SHA-256 `406de5e3b812c5fb5a60ab34c01085e829d13c69fec6eb0d11c22e830d231442`), the detail view resolves `selectedThreadId` by searching the currently projected `subagents` list. If a selection exists but no matching row is present, the panel renders “This subagent is no longer available on the current branch.” The back action clears the selected thread ID. This is a client-side lookup/presentation rule; it does not establish why a row is absent or whether the child still exists elsewhere.

For a completed child, the detail component forwards its `shouldBlockExternalEgress` prop to the Markdown renderer as `externalResourcePolicy: "restricted"` when true and `"allow"` otherwise. The inspected panel code does not establish where that prop is computed or the complete effect of either policy, so this finding records only the conditional passed to the renderer. Relevant code begins near byte offsets `537` (selection and missing-row branch), `6004` (detail renderer), `6442` (branch-unavailable message), and `7757` (policy selection). Static bundle evidence only; no branch switch, rendered UI, or external navigation was exercised.

### Work final-response query enters the shared request client

Followed the query's `safeGet` call from `chatgpt-subagent-final-response-query-b2b6816e81df.js` (SHA-256 recorded above) into `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`). The query passes `/flora/subagent/thread/turns` and `{parameters:{query:{conversationId,threadId,limit:1}}}` to `safeGet`. The shared client maps that method to `GET`, serializes the query through its common URL builder, and combines request-client default headers with any per-call `additionalHeaders`; this call supplies no explicit `additionalHeaders`. For this ordinary `/flora/...` route, the configured defaults include `OAI-Language: en`, `X-OpenAI-Attach-Auth: 1`, `X-OpenAI-Attach-Integrity-State: 1`, and `originator: Codex Desktop`. The two `Attach-*` headers are request-layer attachment flags; this bundle path does not expose the credential or integrity material. The `safeGet` wrapper then uses the shared request path rather than a panel-specific fetch implementation. Relevant definitions are near byte offsets `3391383` (`getRequestTarget`), `3392031` (`makeRequest`), `3396050` (`safeGet`), and `3391067` (`Uxn`/`Wxn` default-header builder); header-name constants are initialized near `1914622`.

The lower request transport calls the injected `e6.httpFetch.fetch(requestId, request, signal)` service and awaits its response; cancellation uses that same service's `cancel(requestId)`. The main-process implementation is in the hidden `.vite/build/main-CqEoQDOU.js` resource (SHA-256 `09d19386e1f23cf4ccbc950fc40d85a1e8a064a08be588b2a4be6a89f6ada9e6`). `fetchHttp` resolves a relative route against the configured backend URL, parses the `X-OpenAI-Attach-*` flags, and enters `performDesktopFetch` (offsets `691815`, `701789`, `694247`). A corrected search using `rg --hidden` found why the initial search missed this: default ripgrep excluded `.vite`.

The URL helper is imported from `.vite/build/bootstrap-CooIJpZm.js` (SHA-256 `d1ab6d0bb5aabf4d56a3cb39d979dc1145e41b47be6821b967cba3802af3fbf0`). Its base selection uses `CODEX_API_BASE_URL` when non-empty (trailing slashes removed); otherwise `CODEX_API_ENDPOINT=localhost` selects the development base, and other cases select the production base. The bundle constants are `http://localhost:8000/api` and `https://chatgpt.com/backend-api`, respectively. The helper then joins the selected base and relative path. The host transport can subsequently replace the origin with `routing.workspace.backendOrigin` for non-legacy workspace routing while preserving the resolved path and query; legacy routing leaves the URL unchanged (bootstrap offsets around `11850–13500`; main bundle `isWorkspaceBackendUrl` around `702944`). Thus the exact destination for this installed run remains unknown without the runtime environment and routing state; neither was read or captured.

In `performDesktopFetch`, `X-OpenAI-Attach-Auth: 1` sets `attachAuth`. The host obtains a cached or app-server-provided auth token and adds authorization headers only when that flag is active; before attaching it, the host rejects URLs outside its desktop-auth allowlist. The allowlist accepts OpenAI/ChatGPT domains and the configured backend origin (with explicit exclusions), but this static inspection does not establish the runtime-resolved URL for this route. The common header parser is in `.vite/build/src-CP7XXlpZ.js` (SHA-256 `79623d50989f9efbcb8e5752c56b3cc608a0f5993c05112e03e11b718ac7a586`, offsets `87900–88328`): it finds control headers case-insensitively, reads their values, and deletes them from the header object. Therefore `X-OpenAI-Attach-Auth`, `X-OpenAI-Attach-Desktop-Surface`, `X-OpenAI-Attach-DeviceCheck-Token`, and `X-OpenAI-Attach-Integrity-State` are host-side IPC controls, not forwarded as same-named HTTP headers. `X-OpenAI-Expected-Account-Id` is likewise extracted and deleted; the host compares it to the account ID in the auth token and aborts on mismatch. `X-OpenAI-Attach-Integrity-State: 1` separately enables loading integrity state into `X-OAI-IS` and conditionally storing a valid `X-OAI-IS-Update` response value. The store is instantiated once per main-process host with `this.scopedState`; updates are accepted only when the current value still matches the request's expected state, preventing a stale response from overwriting newer state. Values must match a bounded `ois1.<segment>.<segment>.<segment>` form. The host clears this state on the observed auth-mode/account-reset branch. Main-process store/auth/header logic is near `655416`, `694247–696000`, and `3316000–3317800`; URL policy is near `702944`.

This recovers the host-side credential/integrity attachment policy without exposing any credential values. It still does not reveal the server's authorization checks, endpoint implementation, response selection, or whether this request was made in the installed app; no request was sent and no live headers were captured. Static extracted-bundle evidence only.

### Resume does not explicitly replay `thread/started` in the matching app-server source

At pinned commit `740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74` (`rust-v0.162.0-alpha.17.2`), `thread/start` constructs and sends `ThreadStarted` after its response ([source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L1638-L1655)). The `thread/fork` path also emits it ([source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L5468-L5492)). In the resume path, the server sends `ThreadResumeResponse`, then may send restored token usage, a goal snapshot, and an idle lifecycle event; this path contains no `ThreadStarted` send ([source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L4184-L4230)). A read-only scan of all Rust files under this revision's `codex-rs/app-server/src/` found production `ThreadStarted` sends at start, fork, and review-thread restoration; it found no send in the resume handler.

A second source snapshot, the public `rust-v0.162.1` tag at commit [`092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d), has the same relevant shape: `thread/start` emits `ThreadStarted` ([source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L1638-L1655)), `thread/fork` emits it ([source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L5415-L5423)), while the cold-resume response path sends the resume response, restored usage if available, a goal snapshot, and idle lifecycle handling without a `ThreadStarted` send ([source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L4206-L4230)). The running-thread resume path similarly returns through its listener response command without an explicit `ThreadStarted`. This is independent static corroboration across two public revisions, not proof that the installed ChatGPT bundle uses either exact server commit or that another connection-initialization path cannot replay notifications.

This corroborates the installed desktop connection finding: if it creates a fresh connection-local `subagentThreadIds` set and repopulates it only from `thread/started`, the matching public app-server resume path does not itself provide that notification for the resumed child. It does **not** prove the installed ChatGPT build uses this exact server commit, that resume is the only connection setup path, or that no other client-side state transfer exists. The local bundle/source provenance is not established, and no reconnect was triggered. Thus “no explicit client backfill” is directly established for the inspected bundle; “a real reconnect necessarily misses child registration” remains unverified.

The installed client bundle has a separate resume-and-hydrate path. In `webview/assets/app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`), `resumeConversation` delegates to its conversation hydration routine (around byte offset `3182360`). That routine reads thread metadata, sends `thread/resume` (around `2936270`), merges the response's `source`, `threadSource`, runtime status, and history into the conversation store (around `2941699`), then broadcasts a conversation snapshot (around `2940000`). This proves the client restores conversation/thread state through resume; it does not populate the distinct `subagentThreadIds` set in the app-server connection bundle. Surface enable/disable is dispatched separately by `use-resume-conversation-if-needed-cd15159177f0.js` (SHA-256 `2299468dc1ce305855a9fc93cd9d11c2956b60023563d8360483ee2d44a818f4`, around byte offset `5428`). The connection bundle's only set insertion remains the qualifying `thread/started` notification path documented above. These are static observations from the installed build; no live reconnect or notification stream was exercised.

The connection lifecycle narrows the reconnect concern further. In the same `src-BJv5Mk7n.js` class, an ordinary transport close resets `connection`, `initialized`, host metadata, pending requests, and auth cache, then schedules/retries initialization on that connection object; `stopProcess()` similarly stops the transport and resets initialization state. Neither path clears `subagentThreadIds`. The set is cleared in `dispose()`, which marks the instance disposed. Thus the static code supports retention of already registered child IDs across an ordinary reconnect/restart of the same connection instance. The missing-backfill concern applies only if the app disposes that instance and constructs a replacement while previously running child threads remain relevant; registry replacement semantics and any replacement-time replay remain unverified. This supersedes the earlier implication that ordinary reconnect necessarily loses the filter registration. Evidence is in the same bundle (SHA-256 `72e2b366d574206f76fc079c79b64c31ce378970a797475ded8709908d338f08`): `handleTransportClosed` around byte offset `1232157`, `stopProcess` around `1231052`, `ensureReady` around `1219879`, and `dispose`/set clearing around `1218256–1219032` (offsets are approximate and hash-specific). Static inspection only; no reconnect was triggered.

The Electron host registry shows a concrete instance-replacement path for remote hosts. In `main-CqEoQDOU.js` (SHA-256 `09d19386e1f23cf4ccbc950fc40d85a1e8a064a08be588b2a4be6a89f6ada9e6`), `connectRemoteConnection` disposes an existing registered connection, creates a new one with `createAndRegisterRemoteConnection`, then adds it to the registry; `disposeRemoteConnection` calls `dispose()` before removing it. Remote-connection reconciliation also disposes/recreates when the existing connection/configuration no longer matches, and when auto-connect is disabled or the host disappears. In contrast, `ensureRemoteConnectionConnected` calls `.connect()` on an existing disconnected instance. Therefore ordinary remote transport reconnect retains the set as above, while an explicit remote reconnect or replacement creates a fresh empty set. No registry path inspected here replays active child IDs into that new instance. Whether a child remains active/relevant across those user-visible host lifecycle changes, and whether another hidden initialization path registers it, was not runtime-verified. Relevant host methods are around byte offsets `3078700–3079500`, `3064000–3066000`, and `3353500`; offsets are hash-specific.

### Existing webview registration does not itself replay subagent state

Followed remote replacement through `createAndRegisterRemoteConnection`: after creating the new connection, Electron iterates `registeredWindows` and calls `registerWebviewWindow` for every existing window. The connection method adds the window listener, installs destruction cleanup, and sends a connection-state snapshot. It does not enumerate child conversations, emit `thread/started`, or replay `subagent-thread-full-fidelity-changed`. The IPC receiver for that event separately updates the connection's per-thread surface map; it does not insert into `subagentThreadIds`. In the entire connection bundle, the only direct insertion call found is in the `thread/started` notification handler, gated by `UR(n)`. That predicate requires `source.subAgent.thread_spawn.parent_thread_id` to be a string; the other field references initialize/clear the set or check it for filtering. This rules out a separate direct IPC or snapshot insertion site in this bundle, but not server/transport replay of a qualifying notification.

The renderer's `use-resume-conversation-if-needed` effect watches two derived shared-store values for the current conversation: its owning host ID and its `subagentParentThreadId`. The latter is derived from the conversation's `parentThreadId` or its source's parent ID, including the loaded/archived thread-title state; the host selector resolves the conversation's manager/summary host, with a host-list fallback. When both values are available, the effect sends `subagent-thread-full-fidelity-changed(enabled: true)` to that host and installs cleanup that sends `enabled: false`. The shared-store `watch` is backed by an effect atom: subscribing mounts it and schedules an initial effect run, and changes to values read by the watcher schedule later runs. Its React effect dependencies are the child conversation ID and store object; the Electron connection instance/registry is not an explicit dependency. The inspected new-connection registration path broadcasts connection state but does not update the conversation's parent relation. Thus a newly mounted/recreated watcher can resend enable from already-present relationship/host state, but replacement alone does not re-mount the existing effect or directly rewrite those values in the path inspected. Whether another path changes either watched value or re-registers the child ID remains unknown.

Evidence: `main-CqEoQDOU.js` offset `3353690` (`createAndRegisterRemoteConnection`); `src-BJv5Mk7n.js` (SHA-256 `72e2b366d574206f76fc079c79b64c31ce378970a797475ded8709908d338f08`) initializes `subagentThreadIds` near `1189433`, clears it during dispose near `1219032`, adds IDs only in the qualifying `thread/started` handler near `1252400`, and checks membership for notification filtering near `1252969`; predicate `UR` checks the `source.subAgent.thread_spawn.parent_thread_id` field near `1154174`. `registerWebviewWindow` is near `1196846`, surface setter `setSubagentThreadFullFidelityForSurface` near `1198170`, and the main-process IPC case near `2104239`. Host registry replacement is in `main-CqEoQDOU.js` near `3353690`. Renderer sender module `use-resume-conversation-if-needed-cd15159177f0.js` SHA-256 `2299468dc1ce305855a9fc93cd9d11c2956b60023563d8360483ee2d44a818f4`, event/effect near offset `5325`. In `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`), `q6t` derives the summary's parent relation near `2620356`; `titleState` preserves it for loaded/archived state near `2632928`; host selector `F3` is near `5689682`; parent selector `Wsi` is near `5694550`. The store watch implementation: `Ost` mounts the effect atom via `store.sub` near `1679810`; `wst` registers its effect and mount lifecycle near `1678048`; `Nk` exposes `watch` near `1684368`; `Xst` implements the predicate watcher near `1684904`. Static bundle evidence only; no remote reconnect was triggered.

### Desktop local and remote connection ownership

The remote replacement analysis above is scoped to remote hosts.
