In the extracted Electron main bundle `main-CqEoQDOU.js` (SHA-256 `09d19386e1f23cf4ccbc950fc40d85a1e8a064a08be588b2a4be6a89f6ada9e6`), manager construction creates its local app-server connection directly with `createAppServerConnection(localHostId)` (around byte offset `3302289`), then inserts that same object into `appServerConnectionRegistry` (around `3303344`). If a durable thread host is configured, the constructor creates and registers it separately (around `3306487`). The `createAndRegisterRemoteConnection` factory (around `3353690`) is used by the remote-connection handler; it is not the local connection's initial registration path. This distinguishes the inspected remote instance-replacement path from the local app-server connection owner. Static bundle evidence; this does not establish every local process-restart path.

A sanitized process snapshot taken on 2026-10-14 also showed the desktop main process running a local embedded Codex CLI `app-server` and a separate embedded `exec-server --remote` process for the cloud environments endpoint. This confirms that a local app-server process and remote execution process coexisted in that snapshot. Process arguments containing environment/session identifiers are intentionally omitted. A process snapshot does not establish which host served any particular subagent thread or notification.

The connection-local `subagentThreadIds` evidence therefore has two different lifecycle scopes: same-instance transport reconnect retains the set; remote host replacement constructs a fresh connection with a fresh set, with no direct child-ID backfill found in the inspected bundle. The local constructor path identified here does not pass through that remote replacement factory. The local connection's behavior under a full local manager/process reconstruction, and the host serving any cloud-backed subagent thread, remain unknown. No restart, disconnect, or notification capture was performed.

### Local manager shutdown path in the desktop main bundle

The same `main-CqEoQDOU.js` bundle has one `new hXe(...)` callsite (around offset `3358549`), in the main startup routine. The host manager's `dispose()` (around `3344603`) first disposes remote-connection and host-app-server managers, then gets all registered host IDs; for each it invokes the connection's `dispose()`, removes that connection from the registry, and removes the execution-host entry. Since the local connection was registered in that registry during construction, this loop includes it if it remains registered. The call to each individual connection's `dispose()` is not awaited in the loop.

The normal `will-quit` handler (around `3384353`) prevents the immediate quit, flushes global/settings state and other services, then calls the injected `flushAndDisposeContext` callback through a helper with a 5-second timeout. That callback invokes and awaits the host manager's `dispose()` and the secondary service manager's `dispose()`, then flushes tracing in `finally`; the timeout helper can continue to quit after five seconds even if the callback has not settled. The update-install immediate-exit path also calls the same callback through a five-second bounded wait before exiting. Thus the bundle establishes that shutdown initiates full manager/connection cleanup, not that every connection's asynchronous disposal necessarily finishes before process exit.

The single constructor callsite found in this main bundle supports a fresh manager being built when the app startup routine runs again; it does not establish whether any remote subagent work survives app exit, how its thread/parent relation is rediscovered, or whether a later server notification repopulates the new connection's child-ID set. This is static bundle evidence; no app restart or update exit was triggered.

### `thread/list` and prewarmed `thread/started` do not backfill the connection child filter

In the installed connection bundle `src-BJv5Mk7n.js` (SHA-256 `72e2b366d574206f76fc079c79b64c31ce378970a797475ded8709908d338f08`), `listThreads()` is a request wrapper around `thread/list` (byte offset about `1205725`). The shared conversation-list helper independently issues `thread/list` through `sendRequest` (about `438282`) and consumes the returned rows for conversation state. Neither path writes to the connection's `subagentThreadIds`; the bundle-wide reference scan still finds only one direct insertion, in the qualifying `thread/started` route (about `1256453`). So persisted list results and conversation hydration are distinct from registering IDs in the notification filter.

There is also a prewarm-specific exception to the ordinary notification route. The installed bundle search found a single `prewarmThreadStart()` caller: the conversation prewarm manager, which prepares a normal `thread/start` request from new-conversation inputs before a conversation is created (about byte offsets `900036–900508` in `src-BJv5Mk7n.js`; the request adapter is in `main-CqEoQDOU.js` around `733031`). No call from a subagent creation path was found. If a request is marked `prewarmThread`, its successful response thread is tracked (about `1259615`). When the corresponding raw `thread/started` notification arrives, `suppressThreadStarted()` returns early from `routeIncomingMessage` (about `1255164`), before the `subagentThreadIds.add` condition. Later, on `turn/start`, the connection publishes a synthetic `thread/started`: that callback invokes internal notification handlers and broadcasts to windows directly (about `1197380`); it does not call `routeIncomingMessage`. Therefore the synthetic publication itself cannot populate `subagentThreadIds`.

This is a conditional path analysis. The inspected client-side call graph points to ordinary conversation prewarming, not subagent creation, so the current evidence does not show that subagents use this path. A server-side or unbundled caller cannot be ruled out; whether another lifecycle event subsequently registers a prewarmed child also remains unknown. Static inspection only; no request, reconnect, or notification was triggered.

## Public-source snapshot comparison: V2 resident eviction and conditional reload

The installed desktop CLI matched source tag `rust-v0.162.0-alpha.17.2` (commit `740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74`). Public tags `rust-v0.162.0-alpha.20` (commit `15c477d788a575de432f661def200ae7bc4a835e`) and `rust-v0.163.0-alpha.9` (commit `4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589`) are distinct source snapshots. After fetching complete history, their commits have merge base `c333d755d44d4eb764cffd2bd862d86d790e91e6`; `git rev-list --left-right --count alpha.20...alpha.9` reports `1 166`. Treat this as a branched comparison, not a linear upgrade sequence. The V2 residency path is present in both snapshots, so its public appearance is bounded to “present by alpha.20”; this does not identify the first introducing commit or establish the installed desktop's behavior.

In alpha.20, a per-session resident queue is touched on access and appends the touched thread at the back. When capacity is full, reservation walks candidates from the front, skips a protected thread, and tries candidates whose residency gate is free. A candidate must still be the same registered runtime and must have no active turn, durable sleep, or trigger-turn mailbox items; its status must be `Completed`, `Errored`, or `Interrupted`. Shutdown is awaited while delivery is excluded. Queue-only mail is drained back into the local mailbox, environment selections are saved in the in-memory agent registry, then the runtime and residency entry are removed. If no candidate can be unloaded, reservation returns `AgentLimitReached` with `ResidencyCapacity` context. Sources: [alpha.20 residency implementation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/15c477d788a575de432f661def200ae7bc4a835e/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs) and [alpha.20 tests](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/15c477d788a575de432f661def200ae7bc4a835e/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency_tests.rs).

Reload is conditional, not a guarantee for every child. `ensure_v2_agent_loaded` requires registered agent metadata, stored thread/history, a V2 history marker, and consistent source/parent identity; it rebuilds configuration and revalidates parent-derived execution/environment policy before resuming the same thread ID. The optional parent path requires a currently registered, running V2 parent from the same registry. The residency tests explicitly show an `Interrupted` child can qualify for eviction and then remain unavailable (`ThreadNotFound`) to the reload path. Therefore “runtime eviction with reload” describes an eligible subset and must not be generalized to all terminal children. Sources: [alpha.20 reload path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/15c477d788a575de432f661def200ae7bc4a835e/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs) and the named interrupted-child test above.

This is a static comparison of public source tags. It does not show that the installed desktop process used alpha.20/alpha.9, that a local eviction occurred, or that the in-memory registry survives process restart. The alpha.9 comparison additionally confirms the path remains present in a later tag; source links: [alpha.9 residency](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs) and [alpha.9 reload](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs).

## App-server idle-unload path in the alpha.9 source snapshot

A separate trigger appears by `rust-v0.163.0-alpha.9`: the app-server lifecycle listener detects a V2 child (`MultiAgentVersion::V2` plus `SessionSource::SubAgent`) that has no subscribed clients and remains inactive for `thread_unload_delay`. It records a pending-unload guard, then delegates to core `try_evict_v2_thread`; on successful removal it drops listener/watch state and sends `thread/closed`. Non-V2 threads continue through the app-server's generic `unload_thread_without_subscribers` path. In `rust-v0.162.0-alpha.20`, the lifecycle listener has only the generic path; it does not call `try_evict_v2_thread`. This is distinct from the core residency-capacity path: one is triggered by subscriber inactivity and a configured delay, the other when a V2 residency reservation reaches capacity. Sources: [alpha.9 thread lifecycle](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_lifecycle.rs) and [alpha.20 thread lifecycle](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/15c477d788a575de432f661def200ae7bc4a835e/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_lifecycle.rs).

The alpha.9 app-server also starts a lifecycle listener for a newly created thread even when the event arrives with no initialized connection IDs, but this no-connection branch only does so for a currently loaded, non-ephemeral V2 child and waits out any pending unload for the same ID. The source test `disconnected_v2_child_lifecycle` exercises a child created while its parent connection is closing, asserts the running child is not prematurely closed, and in the idle-deadline follow-up case invokes the parent's real `followup_task` then checks that the child's previous assistant reply is present in its next model request. Another path in the test checks parent/child runtimes are eventually released. These are assertions in source tests, not tests run for this report. Source: [alpha.9 lifecycle test](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/app-server/src/message_processor_thread_lifecycle_tests.rs).

This shows the public alpha.9 snapshot connects app-server idle lifecycle handling to the core V2 eviction/reload machinery and contains a regression test for disconnected-child continuity. It does not prove the installed desktop embeds this app-server code or that its own child experienced this path. No runtime disconnect, idle wait, or follow-up was triggered here.

## Delivery pins and spawn-cancellation boundary in alpha.9

For an already loaded V2 recipient, message delivery takes a residency read pin before sending. The pin verifies that the same thread runtime is still registered and touches its LRU position, so a concurrent eviction cannot remove that runtime during delivery. A cold-restored child is lazily loaded before any message. A locally evicted child is treated differently: `QueueOnly` mail can be submitted to its persisted mailbox without loading the runtime; `TriggerTurn` first attempts to load it and then submits a follow-up. This distinguishes queue acceptance from turn execution; the receipt does not prove a model consumed the message. Sources: [alpha.9 send path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs#L100-L170), [residency pin](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L156-L173).

The same snapshot keeps provisional spawn ownership through the final awaited status and config reads after initial-input admission. Only then does it commit registry/residency reservations and disarm `PendingSpawn`, immediately before notifying the newly created thread. This narrows the source-defined cancellation cleanup boundary: cancellation during those final reads still leaves the provisional guard responsible for cleanup; after commit/disarm, later tool-result publication is outside that guard. This is source ordering, not a cancellation fault-injection result. Source: [alpha.9 spawn path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L937-L974).

## Sender-context provenance and inherited child settings in alpha.9

Turn-input admission recognizes only standalone outputs from the named host message-delivery tools before attempting to attach sender context. The wrapper's source thread ID is used as a lookup key, not as authorization evidence for message contents. If that sender runtime is not loaded, the code asks the host thread store for its latest model context with a five-second timeout, validates the returned thread ID and non-internal session metadata, and reconstructs context with tool bodies excluded. It retains at most the three latest local user/assistant exchanges; inherited entries and verified answers are omitted, and incomplete user messages are not copied as complete text. A failed, unauthorized, or timed-out storage read yields no recovered sender context rather than blocking admission indefinitely. Sources: [turn-input admission](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn_input.rs#L363-L384), [sender-context capture](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/sender_context.rs#L22-L147).

Spawn resolves the effective parent from the subagent session source, falling back to the explicit parent option, before selecting the multi-agent version, checking execution capacity, or inheriting settings. An omitted `disabled_plugin_ids` option inherits the live parent's setting; an explicitly supplied empty list remains empty. This makes omission and explicit opt-out semantically distinct. Sources: [parent resolution and admission](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L676-L710), [plugin inheritance](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L736-L797).

## V2 `spawn_agent` entry, child configuration, and initial turn in alpha.9

The V2 tool schema requires `task_name` and `message`; its `fork_turns` description advertises only `all` and `none`, while `agent_type`, `model`, and `reasoning_effort` are conditionally exposed. The handler deserializes with unknown fields denied. It rejects `fork_context`, defaults an absent/blank `fork_turns` to `all`, maps `none` to a fresh-history spawn, and maps `all` or a legacy positive integer to the same `FullHistory` mode—the integer does not limit inherited turns. Thus the runtime parser remains more permissive than the advertised schema for legacy counts. Sources: [V2 tool schema](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs#L108-L163), [V2 parameters](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_spec.rs#L649-L683), [handler and result](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs#L105-L192), [argument parser](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs#L201-L239).

The handler converts the tool message according to its invocation source, then delegates to shared `thread_spawn::spawn`. That layer rejects whitespace-only input, resolves V2 model/reasoning/role configuration, and creates a canonical agent path by joining the requested task name to the parent's path. Its `SessionSource::SubAgent::ThreadSpawn` records the parent thread ID, incremented depth, canonical path, and prepared role. Child configuration starts from the invoking turn and captured step settings: it refreshes model/provider/reasoning and base-instruction provenance, applies requested or configured model/reasoning overrides and role rules, then copies runtime approval policy, reviewer, cwd, and permission-profile state. Requested model availability and reasoning support are validated. Sources: [shared spawn preparation and request](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/thread_spawn.rs#L58-L166), [config preparation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/child_config.rs#L52-L121), [shared/runtime config](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/child_config.rs#L132-L202), [model overrides](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/child_config.rs#L204-L260).

History selection and turn execution are separate: both fresh and full-history variants pass the initial `AgentInput::Message` with `TriggerTurn`; the fork mode controls whether native fork preparation supplies parent history. That preparation validates the spawn call/source, flushes pending parent rollout writes before reading stored history, and then prepares the fork snapshot. Once the controller returns an admitted child, the handler emits a `Started` activity with the real child thread ID and canonical path; the model-facing tool result instead contains the task path and, when metadata is visible, an optional nickname. Sources: [initial input and spawn options](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/thread_spawn.rs#L137-L166), [fork snapshot preparation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L1015-L1088), [activity and tool result](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs#L153-L192).

This is static analysis of the public alpha.9 source, not a live observation of the installed desktop session. It establishes the source-defined argument/config/input handoff; it does not establish which options a particular model selected or that this exact source snapshot built the installed app.

## Spawn admission versus child turn task start in alpha.9

The initial `TriggerTurn` communication is submitted after `spawn_child_thread` has returned a registered child runtime. For a loaded child, `submit_inter_agent_communication` sends an ordered `Op::InterAgentCommunication`; the session handler enqueues the communication in the mailbox and, for `trigger_turn`, awaits the shared pending-work scheduler. If the session is idle, that scheduler reserves a turn, builds its context, places mailbox input into the turn state, and calls `start_task`. `start_task` records the turn as started, registers it as the active task, and uses `tokio::spawn` for the task future. The communication submission therefore waits through turn-task registration on this path; it does not wait for model inference or completion. Sources: [communication submission](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs#L236-L323), [mailbox handler](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/handlers.rs#L80-L97), [pending-work turn start](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/tasks/mod.rs#L452-L548), [task registration and Tokio spawn](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/tasks/mod.rs#L287-L425).

Only after that input-submission call succeeds does `AgentControl::spawn` await child status/config, commit registry and V2 residency reservations, disarm provisional-spawn cleanup, and emit `notify_thread_created`. Thus the source order is: child runtime exists; first message is admitted; an idle child's turn task is registered/launched; spawn commits ownership and notifies clients; the asynchronous task may then proceed to model work. A non-idle or otherwise not-startable recipient can accept mailbox input without a new model turn at that moment. None of these static orderings proves that inference began before `spawn_agent` returned, nor that the model consumed or completed the task. Source: [spawn admission and notification](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L937-L985).

This is source-defined ordering in alpha.9; no live spawn, delayed model request, or cancellation fault injection was performed.

## Initial subagent message becomes model history before sampling in alpha.9

When the regular turn consumes the mailbox `TurnInput::InterAgentCommunication`, input recording converts it with `InterAgentCommunication::to_model_input_item` and stores the resulting `ResponseItem::AgentMessage` in conversation history and the rollout. On a later reconstruction, persisted inter-agent communication is converted through the same method and replayed into history. Plaintext communication becomes an `AgentMessage` with the communication's text and author/recipient fields. Encrypted communication keeps encrypted content separate and adds model-facing text identifying `NEW_TASK` versus `MESSAGE`, task name, sender, and payload boundary. Sources: [input recording](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/hook_runtime.rs#L716-L771), [conversion to model input](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs#L987-L1019), [rollout reconstruction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/rollout_reconstruction.rs#L513-L520).

For the initial task, `run_turn` records the accepted input before its first normal sampling request; the first request input is then rebuilt from conversation history, passed through `build_prompt`, and sent through `ModelClientSession::stream`. Pre-sampling work includes optional compaction, MCP/plugin/context preparation, and turn-start hooks, so task registration does not imply immediate network sampling. If pre-sampling compaction fails, the code records/preserves the pending inputs before returning the error. The source proves the request reaches the provider client stream call when that path proceeds; it does not prove server receipt, model token generation, or task success. Sources: [turn preparation and input recording](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L181-L223), [input recording before sampling](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L259-L378), [history to prompt](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L525-L544), [provider stream call](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L2600-L2617).

This traces the alpha.9 core path only. No live provider request was captured, and no claim is made about the remote model's exact interpretation of the `AgentMessage` item.

## Mailbox arrival during an active response in alpha.9

An inter-agent message arriving during sampling does not necessarily cancel the stream immediately. The response loop checks only after a completed output item. Its mailbox-preemption checkpoint applies to assistant `Message` items whose phase is `commentary` or `partial_answer`, and to `Reasoning` items; it excludes `AgentMessage` and tool-call/output items. At an eligible checkpoint, pending mailbox work ends the current sampling request with `needs_follow_up = true` when `Feature::DeferMailboxPreemption` is disabled. The feature spec declares `defer_mailbox_preemption` default-disabled in this source snapshot. This is item-boundary scheduling, not token-by-token preemption. Sources: [`turn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L2804-L2868) and [`features/src/lib.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/features/src/lib.rs#L1498-L1505).

The alpha.9 scenario fixture models a response paused after a reasoning, commentary, or partial-answer item, queues two `trigger_turn = false` messages, then releases the response containing an `update_plan` tool call. Its assertions expect a mailbox-preemption trace in the non-deferred configuration; with deferral enabled, the planned tool result appears in the next request alongside both queued agent messages. The fixture expects two model requests in either configuration. This is evidence of intended/source-tested behavior, not a test run or live-provider observation. Runtime feature configuration and exact behavior of the installed desktop session remain unknown. Source: [`scenarios_mailbox_preemption_tests.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/tests/suite/scenarios_mailbox_preemption_tests.rs#L35-L197).

The outer `run_turn` loop explains how the checkpoint becomes another request: when sampling reports `needs_follow_up`, it asks the input queue to accept mailbox delivery for the active turn, then checks for pending input after sampling and tool processing. The loop therefore schedules another sampling step when follow-up is needed. This code path establishes in-process control flow for the alpha.9 snapshot, not a live desktop observation. Source: [`turn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L550-L578).

## REA focused parse of the two desktop bundles

On 2026-10-14, REA CLI 6.4.0 parsed inert copies of `src-BJv5Mk7n.js` and `main-CqEoQDOU.js` from the installed ChatGPT app. The input root SHA-256 was `517ac14071fb53e395d1e1d772d41a2a639d66e2f813372b216935450b428ac1`; the individual bundle hashes are recorded above. The run read 5,415,772 text bytes, visited 1,369,227 AST nodes, reported 306 findings, and had zero JavaScript parse failures. Evidence ID: `ev_2b28ebed0394837f1ff46d180985565056994d68310abbb8f7e015a4bf1b4290`.

The semantic graph is explicitly partial. Both large bundles hit the owned worker heap limit during semantic analysis; the normalized result reports zero resolved modules and zero IPC operations for this two-file selection. A `trace_application_feature` request seeded with `discoverForSpawn` returned no matching node, while a seed using the asset label returned the partial graph rather than an isolated subagent path. These are limits of this analysis and seed representation, not evidence that the source has no topology refresh or IPC. The bundle strings and callsites remain supported by direct static inspection elsewhere in this report. REA parsed the files as inert text and did not execute bundle bootstrap code. No desktop runtime event was captured.

### REA analysis of extracted subagent slices

To test whether the large-bundle heap limit was hiding a useful local graph, I used Babel only to extract two exact AST ranges from `src-BJv5Mk7n.js`: the complete `Qx` reducer function (source range `579720..584000`, 4,280 bytes) and the `Jj` class expression that defines `SubagentTopology` (source range `865659..871655`, 5,996 bytes). The class expression was assigned the descriptive name `SubagentTopology` in the inert analysis copy; no application code was executed. REA CLI 6.4.0 read 10,304 text bytes across these two files, visited 2,769 AST nodes, and reported zero parse failures. Evidence ID: `ev_8a4d03f28d3ee99f77511666e50e9235bc46d1681c59b916e946d3be66a75dfb`.

The smaller input completed without the earlier worker-heap failure and produced a semantic graph of 1,682 nodes and 1,469 relations. It represents the reducer's `/subagentTopology/discoverForSpawn` property read and the class's `discoverForSpawn` method as separate observations, but does not resolve them into a cross-file call edge. The application summary still reports zero resolved modules, zero findings, and zero IPC operations; semantic family coverage remains partial/unknown with unresolved relations. This is useful as a resource-bound comparison, not an independent proof of invocation or complete topology behavior. The direct reducer callsite and class implementation remain the supporting source evidence; runtime execution and UI effects remain unobserved.

I then extended the inert slice with the renderer host class `kM` (source range `951454..1007512`, 56,058 bytes), retaining the exact `Jj` topology class and `Qx` reducer ranges in one file. REA completed on 66,361 input bytes, 13,593 AST nodes, and zero parse failures; it produced 8,626 semantic nodes and 5,665 relations, with partial/unknown family coverage. Evidence ID: `ev_133eec3cdc29ec99983d2803c68608b0765261f3d8e6dc79e4ff0997253f7295`. This graph resolves the host's constructor call to `Jj`, which strengthens the static link that the host constructs the topology object. It still leaves the reducer's `discoverForSpawn` property read as a candidate and does not connect that receiver to the host instance. The application summary has zero resolved modules and zero IPC operations; this remains a focused local graph, not proof of runtime invocation.

Finally, I included the exact `NS` notification-dispatch function (source range `602483..605761`, 3,278 bytes) alongside those three nodes. REA completed on 69,640 input bytes and 14,240 AST nodes with zero parse failures, producing 8,968 semantic nodes and 5,932 relations. Evidence ID: `ev_fedada22a6ccce56e3225d9096e43d68cc962fff9b9d592501593b1c853c950c`. The graph resolves `NS → Qx` and the host constructor's `construct → Jj` call, but still models `s.subagentTopology.discoverForSpawn` as a candidate property access. The graph itself does not identify `s` as the `kM` instance; the static source assignment chain below supplies that missing identity link. Coverage remains unknown/partial, with zero resolved modules and zero IPC operations. No runtime execution is established.

#### Direct static receiver-identity chain

To resolve the receiver identity left open by the REA graph, I inspected the Babel AST ranges and assignments in the same renderer bundle (SHA-256 `72e2b366d574206f76fc079c79b64c31ce378970a797475ded8709908d338f08`). In the `kM` constructor, `this.subagentTopology = new Jj(...)` is at source range `959668..959834`. The constructor then assigns `this.notificationContext` at `963180..964250`, and that object literal contains `subagentTopology: this.subagentTopology`. Immediately afterward, `this.notificationReductionContext` is assigned at `964334..964727`, with `notificationContext: this.notificationContext`. The `onNotification` method calls `NS(this.notificationReductionContext, ...)` at approximately `999597`.

Inside `NS` (range `602483..605761`), the relevant notification cases call `Qx(e, s, c, a)`, passing `NS`'s first parameter through as `Qx`'s first parameter. `Qx` (range `579720..584000`) begins with `let {manager:o,notificationContext:s,createId:c}=e`, then reaches `s.subagentTopology.discoverForSpawn(...)` for the qualifying completed spawn/activity items. Together, these source-level assignments and calls establish the static object path `kM.subagentTopology → kM.notificationContext.subagentTopology → kM.notificationReductionContext.notificationContext.subagentTopology → NS first argument → Qx local s → discoverForSpawn`.

This closes the *static receiver-identity* gap in the bundle reading; it does not change the REA graph's unresolved relation, and it is not runtime evidence. It does not prove that a qualifying notification occurred in this desktop session, that `discoverForSpawn` completed successfully, or what UI state resulted. Offsets above are Babel source offsets in the extracted JavaScript string, not claims about runtime addresses.

#### Snapshot freshness and reconciliation

The same bundle separates the synchronous store snapshot from completed discovery. `getKnownSubagentDescendantThreads(parent)` combines thread summaries with loaded thread records, then follows each candidate's parent chain to include known descendants; it only returns records available in `threadsById`. `SubagentTopology.getSnapshot(parent)` wraps that view with `isComplete: false` and sets `isLoading` when a full or direct-child discovery for that parent is in flight. Thus a UI subscriber can render known children immediately, but this getter alone never claims the descendant list is exhaustive.

`discover(parent)` coalesces work by parent in separate full-tree and direct-child in-flight maps, captures runtime status evidence before starting `listDescendantThreads`, and then reconciles the fetched result into the thread store. The reconciler treats omitted known descendants as meaningful only when the discovery result says `isComplete`; when complete, an omitted descendant with current `active` or `systemError` runtime evidence is retained, while other omitted descendants have runtime evidence cleared and are represented as idle. Returned thread records prefer a locally stored record when its `updatedAt` is newer, and runtime status changes observed since discovery began take precedence over the fetched status. The resulting snapshot also retains known records that are active or lie on the stored parent chain to an active descendant, even if they were absent from the fresh query result.

This is source-level evidence for why the UI distinguishes “known now” from “complete”, and why an incomplete scan must not be interpreted as an exhaustive negative result. It describes merge policy, not a guarantee that all races or remote failures are recovered; no runtime scan was observed or fault-injected.

## Reproduction and limits

Run `scripts/collect-macos-evidence.sh` to re-check the installed artifact identity and selected static symbol/string names. The report intentionally omits full disassembly and the app bundle. It makes no claim about server-side routing, remote executors, UI service internals, token cost, or actual completion timing.

## Renderer row-status derivation in the Subagents panel

For the installed ChatGPT `26.1007.30912` bundle (`app.asar` SHA-256 `75e397e2280dde93b70a85aa926f4282c728eacc84dfeb15ba7fb6ddd84628af`), I extracted and inspected `webview/assets/app-primary-6f60e9ac4103.js` (SHA-256 `7fe9c00b03cb1de3ebc3a4bf298fc44b13a7fe67ca9cc335a9e2ecda969a1321`) and `webview/assets/local-conversation-subagents-panel-tab-2ab06de7bd1d.js` (SHA-256 `345278adc9f056901c6e0b8d64c98b09d6d3e12019c2ec20be8567b100196eef`). The extracted text was read statically; application code was not executed. Relevant source-string offsets in `app-primary` are approximately `1108149` (`Sat` row construction), `1114902` (`kat` row derivation), `1117504` (`Rat` turn/runtime interpretation), `1117783` (`zat` agent-state normalization), and `1119900` (`Gat` runtime-to-agent-state normalization). These are navigation aids for the exact hashed resource, not stable offsets across builds.

`kat` first drops a row when it has no latest parent reference. It also filters `closeAgent`, `systemError`, hidden agent state unless the runtime thread is active, and failed/interrupted child turns unless runtime status is active. For retained rows, it derives turn state from the child conversation's latest visible turn when available, otherwise its latest turn/history. A known runtime `active` status means in progress; a known non-`notLoaded` non-active runtime status means not in progress; otherwise it falls back to the latest stored turn status.

The displayed `status` is derived, not copied from one source field. If runtime status exists, `active` maps to `active` and every other runtime type maps to `done` (with `systemError` already filtered above). Without runtime status, complete topology discovery forces `done`; otherwise an in-progress turn maps to `active`, a non-progressing turn maps to `done`, and only when turn state is unknown does normalized agent state choose `waiting` or `done`, with other/unknown state defaulting to `active`. The active row's `statusSummary` is derived from the latest reasoning summary when available.

The separate panel chunk sorts by `recencyAtMs`, puts exactly `status === "done"` into Done and every other status into Active, and counts Waiting with the separate predicate `status === "waiting"`. Consequently a Waiting row belongs to the Active display group as well as contributing to the waiting count; the section label is a presentation grouping, not a one-to-one runtime status. The panel limits the Active list to four rows and Done to ten. These findings establish static UI derivation for this bundle only. No live renderer state or notification sequence was observed, and no conclusion is made about another app version.

## Panel subscription indirectly starts descendant discovery

The same renderer artifacts show an important distinction between the snapshot getter and the panel's subscription path. In `local-conversation-subagents-panel-tab-2ab06de7bd1d.js` (SHA-256 `345278adc9f056901c6e0b8d64c98b09d6d3e12019c2ec20be8567b100196eef`), component `F` calls shared hook `l(T,d)` before choosing the list/detail view. `T` maps to app-primary selector `kJ`, and `d` is `{conversationId, hostId}` when the feature gate permits it, otherwise null. In app-shared (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`), that hook resolves the atom and subscribes through the external-store adapter; its layout effect retains the atom for the mounted consumer.

The `kJ` selector (app-primary source-string offset about `1122794`) reads keyed atom `OJ` after it has resolved the parent conversation. `OJ` is defined with an `onMount` callback (about `1120761`). For a non-null key and available host connection, this callback watches the host/parent state, calls `discoverSubagentDescendantSnapshot(parent,{reconcile:true})` immediately, and schedules later refreshes for relevant thread start/archive/delete/unarchive events, child `spawnAgent` or started-activity turn items, and transition to `needs_resume`. This means the panel's mounted subscription can indirectly start discovery through its derived selector dependency, even though `getSnapshot` itself only returns the currently known snapshot and does not perform the RPC. If the gate is false, the key is null; if the parent is not yet resolvable or the host connection is unavailable, this path does not start that initial request.

This is a static dependency and callback chain, not proof that the panel mounted or that an RPC succeeded in this session. It refines the earlier snapshot statement: the getter is read-only, while subscribing to the panel's derived selector can mount a dependency that initiates discovery. Source-string offsets are build-specific navigation aids. The `app-primary` hash and ASAR identity are recorded above.



## Stop-all panel action

The installed renderer bundle's composer view builds the background-subagents panel with `canStopAll` set when at least one row passes the local `canInteract && status !== "done"` predicate, and passes its `onStopAll` handler to `Olt`. The handler first requests `discoverSubagentDescendantSnapshot(parent,{reconcile:true})` and writes a successful snapshot into the keyed topology atom; discovery failure is swallowed and the action continues from the current store. It then re-reads the parent's derived rows, selects interactable non-done rows, and calls `hydrateBackgroundThreads(ids,{includeTurns:true})` for those whose manager has no conversation record or whose known thread status is not `inProgress`. Hydration errors are also swallowed. Finally it calls the supplied `interruptThread({conversationId,hostId})` for every interactable non-done row, including rows that were already in progress and therefore were not hydrated. The handler uses a ref-like guard and loading state to prevent duplicate concurrent invocation in the UI.

This is a user-invoked panel action named “Stop all”, not evidence that ordinary parent submission automatically stops descendants. The exact renderer source is the installed `app-primary-6f60e9ac4103.js` SHA-256 `7fe9c00b03cb1de3ebc3a4bf298fc44b13a7fe67ca9cc335a9e2ecda969a1321`: `lX` and `Olt` are around source-string offsets `1243059..1243550`; the composer binds the handler around `1369981..1370200`; the panel receives `onStopAll:or` around `1406469`. Offsets are build-specific navigation aids. Static analysis establishes the call order, not that the button was clicked, each interrupt RPC succeeded, or the child turns stopped.

The panel's loading lifetime does not include per-child interrupt completion. In this build, `Olt` invokes its callback in a plain loop without awaiting it. The composer callback supplied as `interruptThread` calls `yda(...,{initiatedBy:"user"})` and attaches a rejection handler, but does not return that promise. `yda` itself awaits the selected manager's `interruptConversation(childId,"user-stop",...)`; that asynchronous work therefore continues after the callback returns. The `Olt` promise and the handler's `.finally(...)` can settle and clear the panel loading state after dispatch, before any child interrupt has acknowledged or finished. Relevant app-initial bundle: `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js`, SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`; `yda` is around source-string offset `5441899`, and the callback wrapper around `5454015`. This describes the static promise/return path only: it does not establish that a request was sent in a live session, that the manager accepted it, or that a child reached a terminal status.

## Installed thread-manager descendant interruption

The installed `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` bundle (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`) makes the manager-side cascade conditions explicit. `interruptConversation(id, mode, expectedTurnId)` first awaits the conversation's self-interrupt. Its `finally` starts descendant cleanup only when no expected turn ID was supplied or a non-null interrupted turn ID was returned; it also suppresses recursive cleanup for mode `descendant-cleanup` and for a stream owned as a follower. For `user-stop`, it schedules descendant cleanup asynchronously and logs a warning if the sweep rejects. For other eligible modes, it awaits the sweep. The condition is in the manager method around source-string offset `3202399`; `interruptSubagentDescendantsInBackground` is adjacent.

The sweep helper `Hen` (around offset `2799025`) uses two passes. It first reads the currently stored descendant snapshot and concurrently calls the manager interrupt path for descendants considered active by `Uen`; successful non-null interrupted-turn results are recorded to avoid repeating those children in the next pass. It then calls `discoverSubagentDescendantSnapshot` for a fresh snapshot. If discovery fails, it logs and ends the sweep after the first pass. Otherwise it considers only newly seen active descendants: if the conversation manager has that child and `descendant-cleanup` returns a non-null turn ID, it skips further work for that child; otherwise it reads the first returned turn record and sends `turn/interrupt` only when that turn is `inProgress`. Per-child failures are caught and logged. `Uen` (around offset `2799979`) considers an in-progress local turn active; otherwise it uses local runtime status when usable, falling back to the snapshot status.

Combined with the panel chain above, Stop all dispatches user-stop callbacks for its eligible rows, while each resolved manager may schedule its own later descendant sweep. The panel does not await those requests or sweeps, and manager-side best-effort handling does not provide a tree-wide completion acknowledgement to the panel. These are static relationships from this exact installed bundle; no interrupt button, live request, scheduler callback, descendant status transition, or failure was observed. Offsets are build-specific.

## Opening an individual child from composer panel

The composer derives parent-scoped background-agent rows through `Mlt`: it reads `kJ` for the active conversation, filters to rows whose `parentConversationId` matches that conversation, derives mentionable rows from `canInteract` plus a non-empty display name, and derives the visible panel rows from `isCurrentParentTurn`. Each panel row's click handler calls `zQe(scope,{conversationId,hostId,navigate})`. `zQe` first awaits `ensureConversationFirstPage(childId)` and returns without navigation if it fails; on success it calls `Ch(scope,childId,hostId)`, computes a conversation path with `BQe`, then opens that path through the hotkey-window opener when running in a hotkey window, otherwise through the supplied router navigation callback. Relevant exact app-primary resource is SHA-256 `7fe9c00b03cb1de3ebc3a4bf298fc44b13a7fe67ca9cc335a9e2ecda969a1321`: `zQe` around source-string offset `485618`; `Mlt` around `1246642`; composer row binding around `1406412`. These offsets are build-specific.

This is the static navigation path for clicking an individual composer-panel row. It does not establish that the child load succeeded in this session or that navigation occurred. Whether `ensureConversationFirstPage` loads from a remote service or cache is outside what this callsite alone proves.

## Child host association before navigation

The `Ch` call in `zQe` is not itself a child activation operation. In `app-primary`, `Ch` is imported from `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` as its export `rF`; that export maps to `Eoi`. `Eoi(scope,threadId,hostId)` writes the host ID into keyed state `dli` for that thread (`Eoi` around source-string offset `5684641`; `dli` initialized around `5705691`). `Doi` reads this association, and `O3` prefers it before falling back to the thread's project assignment. The shared host resolver `G3` uses `O3` first when choosing a host for a thread (around `5702864`). Thus the click path records the row's known `(childId,hostId)` association before constructing and opening its route; this is evidence for host-resolution context, not proof that a child turn is activated, resumed, or executed.

`BQe` then selects the hotkey-window route builder `Vh` or the ordinary route builder `Pf`, based on `bg()`, and wraps the result with `$Me(...,hostId)`; its caller sends that path to the hotkey opener or router callback. These are static bundle relationships. They do not establish that host resolution or navigation succeeded in the installed session. `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` SHA-256 is `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`; source-string offsets are build-specific.

## First-page readiness before opening a child

In the installed `src-BJv5Mk7n.js` bundle (SHA-256 `72e2b366d574206f76fc079c79b64c31ce378970a797475ded8709908d338f08`), `ensureConversationFirstPage(childId)` first rejects a conversation suppressed after archive. It looks up the local conversation record and, when absent, awaits `hydrateBackgroundThreads([childId])` before looking it up again. If a record exists but is not in `resumeState === "resumed"`, or it has no turns while `Ao(record)` says its history is incomplete, it awaits `loadOlderConversationHistoryPage(childId)`. It then returns true only if the conversation was not suppressed and still exists; the panel click handler navigates only on that true result. The method is around source-string offset `260005`; the history helpers are around `528414..529900`.

The history helper `Ob` is conditional: it only performs work when tail hydration is enabled or the conversation is archive-suppressed. For a non-suppressed child on a cloud host that is `needs_resume` (or lacks a resume state), has no resume already in flight, and is not in the helper's special `Jp` state, it requests `loadBackgroundThreadHistoryPage` and returns. Otherwise it may call `resumeConversationForUnavailableOwner` through `kb`, then requests `loadOlderConversationTurnsPage`; `kb` skips that resume request for already-resumed, suppressed, or cloud-`Jp` conversations. Therefore opening a child can cause history hydration and, in some states, a resume-owner request as part of preparing history. The static path does not establish that a child turn is started, that remote work succeeded, or that any particular branch ran in the installed session. `Ao` treats canonical history as complete only when its history is complete and has one island; for non-canonical history it uses the turns and item pagination completeness state. These are implementation conditions, not a guarantee that every child click makes a network request.

This is static analysis of the installed bundle; the renderer was not executed and no network or UI event was captured. `ensureConversationFirstPage` itself does not expose whether the data came from cache or a service. Source-string offsets are build-specific navigation aids.

## Subagent lifecycle hooks into Browser Use session routing

In the installed ChatGPT `26.1007.30912` main bundle `main-CqEoQDOU.js` (SHA-256 `09d19386e1f23cf4ccbc950fc40d85a1e8a064a08be588b2a4be6a89f6ada9e6`), function `lfe` (source-string offset about `721532`) registers Browser Session Registry callbacks on a manager. The attachment site around `745716` requires `b != null` and `browserSessionRegistry != null`. The preceding manager setup defines `b` as `threadExecution ?? fallback`; the fallback exists only when `executeDynamicToolCall` exists. So the first condition means thread-execution support is present, either directly or through that dynamic-tool fallback. It is not itself a Browser Use feature flag, and this is not an unconditional app-wide hook.

The Browser Use native-pipe availability is controlled separately. `Xte.setDesktopFeatureAvailability` (around offset `97933`) ignores an update only when both `inAppBrowserUse` and `mcpAppsBrowserUse` are null; otherwise it passes those values, falling back individually to the current desktop feature state `K()`, to `BrowserSessionRegistry.setBrowserUseNativePipeEnabled` (call around `3808986`, implementation around `1795810`). On a value change, the registry disposes existing session backends and, when in-app Browser Use is enabled, ensures backends for existing routes. The bundle's initial feature-state object has `inAppBrowserUse`, `mcpAppsBrowserUse`, and `inAppBrowserUseAllowed` set to false (around offset `68695`); the desktop feature state can later be updated, so these defaults do not establish the observed session's effective values. This pipe-availability path is distinct from the manager listener-attachment condition above. These offsets and defaults are static bundle observations, not a runtime feature-state capture.

When attached, `thread/started` parses the thread source and captures a child route when `parentThreadId` is present. The `[item/started,item/completed]` callback also captures a route for `subAgentActivity(kind=started)` using its `agentThreadId` and the notification's parent `threadId`; `completed` and `interrupted` activity kinds end that child's session activity. For `collabAgentToolCall`, each receiver is captured while its reported state is absent, `pendingInit`, or `running`; other reported states end activity. A turn-interrupt-started callback also ends activity. The disposer unregisters the listeners and ends any routes still tracked by this closure.

`BrowserSessionRegistry.captureChildSessionRoute(child,parent)` (around source-string offset `1801268`) returns false if a route for the child already exists or the parent has no route. Otherwise it captures a route with the child as both `browserConversationId` and `sessionId`, sets `disposeAfterSessionActivity: true`, and copies the parent's `ownerWebContentsId` and `windowId`. The shared `captureSessionRouteForWindow` stores that mapping and calls `ensureBackendForSession(sessionId)`. The registry's `isSubagentBrowserUseRoute(route)` predicate (around `1817367`) identifies a captured route as this kind when its `disposeAfterSessionActivity` flag is true. Thus the static code associates a child-scoped route/backend with the same owning WebContents/window as the parent and explicitly marks it for child-activity cleanup. It does not establish that browser state itself is inherited or that the child actually used Browser Use.

`endSessionActivity({conversationId})` (around offset `1806647`) resolves the stored browser route, asks that session's backend to release control, releases its MCP app session control, marks Browser Use inactive through a route host or delegate, and disposes the route when `disposeAfterSessionActivity` is set. This is lifecycle cleanup/routing behavior, not evidence of a subagent-specific browser capability being enabled.

These are static observations from the hashed installed bundle; no runtime notification, feature-gate state, Browser Use action, or child route was captured. The offsets are source-string navigation aids, not runtime addresses, and the code does not reveal whether this manager path was active in the observed desktop session.

### Renderer-to-main Browser Use availability update

The renderer bundle `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`) contains `w_c` (around source-string offset `9840216`). Its effect waits until the relevant availability queries and loading states have settled, then dispatches `electron-desktop-features-changed` (around `9843747`). The payload distinguishes `inAppBrowserUse: le.available`, `inAppBrowserUseAllowed: le.allowed`, and `mcpAppsBrowserUse: H`; the effect also records a `browser_use_availability_resolved` log call with safe fields for availability, platform, reason, and release (around `9845312`). `browserPane` is separately sourced from the `browser.in-app` capability and is not the same value as `inAppBrowserUse`. The log call is present in code; no emitted log record was captured.

In main bundle `main-CqEoQDOU.js` (SHA-256 `09d19386e1f23cf4ccbc950fc40d85a1e8a064a08be588b2a4be6a89f6ada9e6`), the `electron-desktop-features-changed` receiver around `3376389` ignores messages from a dormant primary window, constructs the desktop feature-state object from the received fields, and calls the supplied updater `S` with that state and bundled-plugin eligibility reasons around `3378330`. Startup supplies `setDesktopFeatureAvailability: lt` around `3810445`; the updater normalizes the state and reaches `Xte.setDesktopFeatureAvailability`, which passes the Browser Use availability values to `BrowserSessionRegistry.setBrowserUseNativePipeEnabled` (implementation around `1795810`, call around `3808986`). This statically connects renderer-resolved feature state to the main-process registry pipe gate. It refines the earlier observation of false initial defaults: those defaults alone do not describe the later effective state.

This is static propagation evidence only. No current feature-state payload, IPC event, emitted availability log, or effective native-pipe state was captured from the running desktop app. Bundle offsets are build-specific source-string positions.

### Inputs that determine renderer Browser Use availability

The imported renderer helper `fWe` maps to export `pJ` from `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`), whose implementation is `GJr` around offset `5355395`. It combines several independent requirements before returning `available`, `allowed`, `isLoading`, and `reason`:

- The `browser.in-app` capability must be present (`isCapable`).
- The host feature list must enable `browser_use`; the helper defaults that feature to enabled if it is absent, but the actual `GJr` call does not supply a host feature default. The feature list is still loaded through the host-aware query.
- Feature flag ID `410262010` (passed as `isBrowserAgentGateEnabled`) must be enabled.
- The host/account permission query must not explicitly set `requirements.allowBrowserAndComputerUse` to false. Pending auth/permission requirements keep the result loading. This is a shared browser-and-computer-use permission condition, not a child-specific permission check.
- Neither `runCodexInWsl` nor the host's WSL classification may indicate WSL; `KJr` then returns `wsl-disabled`.

`KJr` around offset `5356590` returns `available` only after those gates pass. Its reason ordering is explicit: pending requirements first; then permission denial; then platform loading; then browser-pane capability; then the feature flag; then the `browser_use` host feature; then WSL; otherwise available. In `GJr`, both returned booleans `available` and `allowed` are derived from that same final `reason === "available"` result, so the name `allowed` should not be read as an independent raw permission value. This traces the decision inputs in shipped code; it does not reveal their values in the current session or prove that a child tool call uses the browser.

A passive unified-log query for process `ChatGPT` and marker `browser_use_availability_resolved` over the preceding six hours returned no matching records. This is a bounded empty log result only; it does not establish that the renderer did not run or that the feature was unavailable. No live Electron/CDP capture was available in this REA session. Source offsets are build-specific.

## Cancellation-safe V2 child reload in the later alpha.9 snapshot

The installed desktop CLI is `rust-v0.162.0-alpha.17.2`; this section describes a later public source snapshot (`rust-v0.163.0-alpha.9`, commit [`4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589)) and must not be projected onto the installed build. The later source returns a residency read pin from `ensure_v2_agent_loaded`, and `AgentControl::send` keeps that pin alive through `send_input` or inter-agent communication submission. For a child that must be reloaded, the reload path now hands final registration to a detached Tokio task: it acquires the residency pin, clears saved evicted environments, commits the residency reservation, emits resumed-child analytics when applicable, notifies thread creation, and returns the pin. The caller awaits that task, but cancellation of the caller no longer cancels the registration task. Sources: [reload and detached registration](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L315-L323), [detached completion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L626-L660), [send retains the pin](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs#L109-L120).

The source history contains a matching regression test, `cancelled_v2_send_finishes_reload_registration` ([test source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L1015-L1138)): it evicts a child, starts a send that triggers reload, blocks registration at the residency gate, cancels the sender, then asserts that thread-created notification and residency state still complete and that the child can be evicted/reloaded again. This is evidence of a source-level regression test, not a test run in this investigation or proof of behavior in the installed desktop binary. The source change is present in alpha.9; the install's alpha.17.2 exact tag predates it.

## Child capabilities do not depend on copied history in alpha.9

The later `rust-v0.163.0-alpha.9` snapshot also changes how a spawned child receives startup capabilities. Commit [`845345b1dd4f8e2a5d5ce91363fff666e41b07fc`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/845345b1dd4f8e2a5d5ce91363fff666e41b07fc) replaces separate fresh-thread and fork-thread startup branches with shared `StartThreadOptions`, captured from the spawning turn before history is prepared ([spawn path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L686-L845)). The parent is resolved from the session source first, with the explicit parent option as fallback. If `disabled_plugin_ids` is omitted, the child inherits the parent's current list; an explicit list, including an empty list, overrides it.

Selected capability roots are a separate setting from the disabled-plugin list. At shared session startup, explicit roots win, including an explicit empty list. Otherwise, a non-resumed child with `SessionIsolation::Inherit` takes roots from its inherited environment snapshot even when its conversation history is fresh or forked. A resumed thread instead restores its saved roots from history; isolated startup does not inherit the parent's environment roots ([session startup](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L647-L671)). Regression-test source covers explicit-root precedence, empty roots, inherited versus isolated startup, history persistence, and fresh/forked plugin-choice overrides ([startup tests](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager_tests.rs#L1953-L2098), [plugin-choice tests](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L2271-L2328)). The app-server test also exercises executor skill listing/reading and plugin MCP registration for `fork_turns: "none"` and `"all"` ([test source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589/codex-rs/app-server/tests/suite/v2/executor_skills_spawn_tests.rs#L27-L242)). These are source tests; none were run here.

This narrows the earlier statement that fresh and forked children may receive different capabilities: history mode alone does not select the startup capability roots or omitted plugin-disable choices in this snapshot. The inheritance remains conditional on the inherited environment snapshot and isolation policy, and it does not mean the child receives the parent's entire tool registry. This is later public source, not runtime evidence for the installed `0.162.0-alpha.17.2` desktop CLI.

## Alpha.10 comparison boundary

The GitHub compare between `rust-v0.163.0-alpha.9` (`4753ce3d19df9ffa58353736c4ffde49ed071589`) and `rust-v0.163.0-alpha.10` (`cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f`) reports `diverged` (12 commits ahead, 1 behind). Treat it as a comparison of two source snapshots, not a linear alpha.9-to-alpha.10 history. Within the inspected core subagent control, V2 spawn, message, and thread-spawn paths, no matching core file changed. `app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs` adds three lines disabling auto-compaction inside the `experimentalPredictionMode` prediction-fork setup ([alpha.10 source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L5152-L5185)); that is not the ordinary child-spawn path.

The broader path-name scan also found external-agent migration changes and Guardian V2 parent-compaction changes. Commit [`cb7091b1803761ea882bf4017f519b9b83b83fd4`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/cb7091b1803761ea882bf4017f519b9b83b83fd4) preserves recognized URLs and email addresses while rewriting third-party agent configuration during import; its app-server test keeps the imported source attribution-only. This is configuration migration, not internal child creation. The Guardian change adjusts parent-compaction input/token bounds, not the spawn lifecycle. These were considered because their paths matched the broad scan; neither provides evidence of changed child scheduling.

One broader capability-related change deserves a precise boundary. Commit [`703d253ed676c26e4b9708a374b7344b5c1bcc53`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/703d253ed676c26e4b9708a374b7344b5c1bcc53) wires `capabilities/discoverV2` through the exec-server RPC: initialized remote sessions advertise support, `Environment::discover_capabilities_v2` checks that support, executor-side discovery is refreshed according to watcher registration and sandbox context, and requests are replayed after executor recovery. Its integration test checks metadata refresh, omission of skill instruction bodies, and sandbox separation ([RPC test](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/exec-server/tests/capabilities_v2.rs#L1-L214)). In the inspected alpha.10 source, this exact `discover_capabilities_v2` method remains confined to exec-server and its tests; no core/app-server caller was found.

That negative result does **not** mean core skips executor capability discovery. The model-step path resolves selected roots, calls `executor_capability_discovery_for_step`, places the resulting `ExecutorCapabilityDiscoverySnapshot` in step extension data, and passes it to selected-plugin projection and `turn::built_tools` ([step preparation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L3919-L4008)). The cache behind this path calls the distinct `Environment::discover_capability_roots` API ([cache](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/exec-server/src/capability_discovery_cache.rs#L128-L220), [environment API](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/exec-server/src/environment.rs#L1062-L1093)). `SkillsExtension::tools_for_step` reads that snapshot and selected roots to build executor skill tools; the executor provider projects discovered skill metadata/instructions into the catalog and reads a selected skill through the environment filesystem ([skill tool assembly](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/extension.rs#L282-L322), [executor provider](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/provider/executor.rs#L82-L220)). The app-server spawn test exercises a child listing and reading an executor skill and checks plugin MCP registration with both `fork_turns` modes, and with the plugin enabled/disabled ([spawn test](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/app-server/tests/suite/v2/executor_skills_spawn_tests.rs#L27-L242)). Thus selected-root V1 discovery does feed the child-visible skill/plugin path in this source snapshot; the separate V2 RPC's use in child tool assembly is not established. These are source links and test assertions; no tests were run and this does not establish the installed desktop client's runtime behavior.

The source also closes the “catalog versus model-visible tool” gap. `SkillsExtension::tools_for_step` returns `skills.list` and `skills.read` only when cloud skills are enabled or the step has a nonempty executor-root query ([tool construction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/tools/mod.rs#L59-L91)). `core::tools::spec_plan::built_tools` obtains each contributor's step tools, wraps and registers them subject to tool policy, then `build_model_visible_specs` keeps only directly exposed tools; `build_prompt` supplies that visible-spec list to the model ([registration](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs#L145-L180), [visibility filter](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs#L627-L646), [prompt assembly](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L1615-L1635)). Thus this source establishes the route by which child skill tools can reach the model, while tool policy, exposure/mode, and availability gates remain material conditions; the child spawn tests establish exercised calls in their test fixtures, not universal availability in every live child.

Plugin MCP registration is a parallel path with separate gates. The step resolver considers only contributors matching ready selected roots, then omits a plugin when the Plugins feature is off, the plugin configuration disallows it, or its ID is in the child's disabled list ([per-step plugin selection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/core/src/mcp.rs#L191-L225)). The MCP projection builds registrations from selected-plugin contributions, omits disabled servers, and installs the resulting registrations in the MCP server catalog ([plugin MCP projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/core/src/mcp.rs#L252-L289), [catalog construction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/core/src/mcp.rs#L396-L456)). So selected roots are shared input, but skill list/read availability and plugin MCP registration are not the same mechanism or controlled by identical gates.

The paths meet again when the executor skill catalog is projected. `SkillsExtension::tools_for_step` gives the skill tools both the executor query and the step's `SelectedPluginSnapshot`; before returning the executor catalog, the skill tool context applies `attribute_executor_plugins`. That function removes skills whose root is in `disabled_plugin_roots`, and attaches the selected plugin ID to skills under enabled plugin roots ([step wiring](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/extension.rs#L286-L317), [catalog filtering and attribution](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/tools/mod.rs#L231-L245), [projection function](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/provider/executor.rs#L68-L84)). Consequently, a disabled plugin affects both its MCP registration and the skill entries associated with that selected root; this is a source-level behavior in alpha.10, not a live-child observation.

The app-server spawn test asserts both sides of this behavior for the parent and child. With the plugin disabled, each agent's skill list is empty, its read result omits the skill body, and its MCP status omits the plugin server. With the plugin enabled, each reads the expected contents from the selected root, lists `spawn-plugin:deploy`, and sees the plugin MCP server; the test also asserts that the parent and child catalogs match. Its four cases cross `fork_turns: "none"`/`"all"` with enabled/disabled plugin selection ([test setup and matrix](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/app-server/tests/suite/v2/executor_skills_spawn_tests.rs#L27-L38), [assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/cd643519fb56011557d09b41053a3a4c0e013e1f/codex-rs/app-server/tests/suite/v2/executor_skills_spawn_tests.rs#L202-L255)). These are assertions in the source test; the test was not run for this report and does not establish what a live desktop child received.

These checks broaden the earlier path-filter result; they do not prove that no other alpha.10 change can indirectly affect child behavior. They also do not establish behavior in the installed desktop CLI (`0.162.0-alpha.17.2`).

## CLI TUI Agents Overview: parent-task rows with child status projection

The following is a source-only trace of the public `rust-v0.162.1` snapshot at commit [`092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d). It is a separate standalone CLI source snapshot; it does not identify the installed desktop bundle or establish that this code ran in the current Codex session.

The TUI's Agents Overview discovers top-level tasks through paginated `thread/list` requests. Discovery keeps separate cursors for the default and Exec/AppServer sources, asks for up to ten tasks at a time, and merges the pages by recency ([discovery and pagination](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview_discovery.rs#L110-L211)). The root-task predicate excludes ephemeral threads, any thread with a parent, and threads explicitly sourced from `SubAgent(ThreadSpawn)` ([pinned and overview filters](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview_discovery.rs#L21-L89)). This is therefore a task dashboard, not a flat, independently selectable child-agent list.

After discovery, the view reconstructs parent-child relationships, fills a missing parent ID from `ThreadSpawn` source metadata, and creates rows only for roots ([row projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview.rs#L329-L386)). Descendant status is recursively folded into the root's urgency group ([group aggregation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview.rs#L1229-L1237)); the details projection can show the selected child's name, needs-input state, reasoning, and latest message while retaining the root row as the selection target ([details projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview_details.rs#L130-L227)). The status mapping groups active work needing approval/input and system errors under “Needs You”, active work under “Working”, idle work under “Ready”, and unloaded/finished work under the inactive group ([status groups](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview_view.rs#L66-L98)).

Keyboard actions target the selected root thread ID: opening selects that task, fork and rename use that root, and stop is offered when the root row itself is active ([row actions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview_view.rs#L359-L366), [shortcuts](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview_view.rs#L671-L739)). Archive/delete confirmation text says the task and child agents' running work will be stopped; the action handler itself calls the app-server archive or delete operation with the root thread ID ([confirmation and handler](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview_actions.rs#L197-L285)). This UI copy plus root API call does not independently establish the server's cascade semantics or prove that every child was stopped.

The overview is daemon-wide only with the shared background server; embedded-server mode presents an unavailable state ([overview entry](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview.rs#L90-L164)). These findings describe the inspected TUI source paths only. No tests were run, no TUI action was executed, and no runtime child state was captured.

The surface can be opened through the configurable `tui.keymap.global.open_agents` action, which is described in the keymap UI as “Open the shared agent-session overview”; startup also has an explicit `SessionSelection::AgentsOverview` route that opens this view without starting a fresh chat first ([keymap action](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/keymap_setup/actions.rs#L85-L89), [key dispatch](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/input.rs#L695-L703), [startup selection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/startup.rs#L498-L515), [startup open](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/startup.rs#L995-L999)). The keymap action is configurable; these inspected lines do not establish a particular default key.

The command center offers status filters All, Needs you, Working, Ready, and Inactive, cycled by Tab/Shift-Tab. Search matches the root task's name, preview, and working directory; pinned tasks are ordered ahead of other visible tasks. Non-pinned rows can be grouped by project, status, or model, and the selected grouping is written to the user's `tui.agents_overview_grouping` config setting ([filters and keys](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agent_center/mod.rs#L11-L18), [filter input](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agent_center/input.rs#L83-L107), [search and grouping projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview_grouping.rs#L26-L69), [grouping persistence](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/092d3acd6bec3e3a14bdc7e7a2810ab628ab759d/codex-rs/tui/src/app/agents_overview_grouping.rs#L104-L119)). This is source-level UI/configuration behavior; no user config was read or changed.

## Current main snapshot: shared child spawn and fork-prefix handling

This section inspects public OpenAI Codex `main` at [`20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf).
