It is a source snapshot comparison against the earlier `rust-v0.162.1` notes, not a released-version claim or proof about the installed desktop binary. The inspected Git checkout was shallow; this comparison does not establish a linear release history.

The V2 native tool path delegates child preparation to `core/src/thread_spawn.rs`. That shared function rejects a blank message, prepares the child config and role, extends the inherited `AgentPath`, stamps `ThreadSpawn` source metadata with parent/depth/path/role, resolves fork-specific usage hints, then asks the backend to spawn with the captured parent turn, environment snapshot, plugin selection, and input ([shared spawn request and validation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/thread_spawn.rs#L30-L80), [source metadata and backend request](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/thread_spawn.rs#L81-L167)). The controller owns the actual lifecycle: it resolves the parent before inheriting settings, reserves capacity, builds `StartThreadOptions`, prepares copied history only for a fork, starts the child, persists the parent-child edge, submits the initial input, and then commits the reservation ([parent and capacity setup](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L683-L760), [shared startup options and fresh/fork split](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L783-L844), [registration, input submission, and reservation commit](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L845-L1012)). This describes code ownership and ordering, not a live trace.

The controller copies `disabled_plugin_ids` from the spawn options when supplied. Only when the option is `None` does it look up the parent thread's current setting; an explicit empty vector therefore remains an explicit clear. The source test matrix covers fresh and full-history children with omitted, replacement, and empty selections ([inheritance branch](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L743-L756), [test cases and assertion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L2270-L2328)). These tests were inspected, not run.

Full-history preparation now has a `preserve_fork_prefix` branch. It is active only for V2 when the config flag is true and the selected compaction checkpoint still has usable context baselines. In that branch, the interrupted snapshot is prepared, copied response items are retained with inherited-message metadata, and an explicit child-context hint is appended; legacy checkpoints without replacement history do not qualify. The config default in this snapshot is false ([eligibility and snapshot handling](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L1112-L1152), [retention and child hint](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L1221-L1295), [default](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs#L1370-L1404)). Nearby source tests cover restored child instructions after compaction and a legacy-compaction fallback with the flag both off and on ([test source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L3520-L3606), [legacy fallback cases](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L3671-L3718)). The checked-in default is off; this does not establish the effective config used by a release or desktop session.

No source tests were run, and no desktop or CLI runtime behavior was observed in this comparison. The feature's effective release/config availability, and whether the installed desktop uses this `main` implementation, remain unknown.

## Current main snapshot: child terminal outcome and parent delivery

This continues the public Codex `main` snapshot at [`20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf), not a claim about the installed desktop runtime. For V2 child sessions, `Session::send_event` publishes the terminal turn event, then checks whether it is a final `TurnComplete`/`TurnAborted` outcome from a `ThreadSpawn` source with an agent path. Ordinary `Interrupted` is not final; Guardian `TooManyDenials` is explicitly represented as an error outcome. The session passes an `AgentTurnOutcome` to `AgentControl::turn_finished` only for eligible terminal outcomes ([session terminal callback](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L2457-L2569), [controller API](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs#L292-L298)).

`completion.rs` separates a completion activity from the result message. A successful child completion with a recorded parent turn can emit a `Completed` activity to the initiating thread (which may differ from the structural parent); then the controller formats a terminal result and constructs `InterAgentCommunication` addressed to the structural parent with `trigger_turn=false` ([routing and activity](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs#L25-L120)). The activity has no result body. The formatted message carries the child's final text, an empty payload for `Completed(None)`, or bounded failure/shutdown text; ordinary `Interrupted`, `Running`, and initialization states do not produce this result message ([completion formatter and limits](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/session_prefix.rs#L9-L47)).

Queue acceptance is not the same as the parent model consuming the result. With `trigger_turn=false`, `send_inter_agent_communication` skips the execution-capacity check. If the parent session is loaded, the controller submits an ordered `Op::InterAgentCommunication`; if it is unloaded, it writes the message to the persistent mailbox and returns a submission ID ([delivery branches](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs#L163-L185), [loaded/unloaded delivery](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs#L254-L323)). Delivery metrics distinguish `queued` from `failed`, which records submission outcome rather than model acknowledgement. Separately, V2 `wait_agent` subscribes to current-turn activity and can wake on mailbox activity, user steering, or timeout; it does not return the child result or join a particular child ([wait outcome handling](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/wait.rs#L67-L131)).

When the loaded parent session handles the operation, it enqueues the communication and emits a receive record. A queue-only message does not by itself start a new turn; the handler asks the pending-work scheduler to run only for `trigger_turn=true` or when the session has outstanding durable sleep ([handler and scheduler condition](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/session/handlers.rs#L80-L97)). An unloaded parent's mailbox is drained into pending `TurnInput` items in delivery order when mailbox delivery is accepted ([mailbox drain](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs#L237-L275), [pending input delivery](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs#L400-L432)). The turn input is converted into an assistant `AgentMessage`, recorded in conversation history/rollout, and emitted as response items ([protocol conversion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs#L987-L1019), [history recording](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L4043-L4099)). This establishes history insertion and response-item emission, not that the parent model has sampled a subsequent prompt.

The app-server thread-read summary derives its `final_text` from `TurnSummary.last_agent_message`, populated from assistant `AgentMessage` items whose phase is `FinalAnswer` or absent; that field is not a generic view of all inter-agent messages ([turn summary projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/app-server/src/thread_state.rs#L201-L221), [thread-read final text](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_read_state.rs#L114-L130)). Thus a final-answer summary and a child-result message are different projections. This does not establish how every desktop surface renders its conversation history.

These are static source findings. No source tests were run and no desktop/CLI runtime event, mailbox consumption, parent model turn, or UI update was captured. The public checkout is shallow, so this section does not establish when the behavior entered a release or whether the installed client contains it.


### Parent transcript projection for inter-agent AgentMessage items

Followed the unresolved desktop-history boundary in the installed `2026-10-13` app bundle. The extracted `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` has SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`; its shared turn projection `d1` begins near byte offset `4984251`. For each `agentMessage` item admitted by the projection's filters and turn budget, `d1` creates a UI `assistant-message` block carrying the item text, ID, phase, delivery, completion state, and related display metadata. This branch does not special-case subagent authorship. A separate `subAgentActivity` item becomes `subagent-activity`, with `isMessage` derived from `kind === interacted`; `collabAgentToolCall` becomes `multi-agent-action`. Thus the renderer's activity/status projection and assistant-message transcript projection are distinct paths.

The extracted `local-conversation-thread-turn-entries-289ca6f16609.js` (SHA-256 `1a3b8fbb3442fa3fca5d6b545cfde2cb193bd314b31dad8e3feb70bbf498aec2`) calls that shared projection for a turn's items and turns `assistant-message` blocks into transcript entries (projection call near byte offset `14107`; entry conversion near `15100`). The extracted `local-conversation-turn-632904a8790c.js` (SHA-256 `f16c75de8c65e6849f3976cc8e0efec198e7b063ec81ce514ff85b348967b376`) dispatches the `assistant-message` block to the standard assistant-message component (`Ua`, branch near byte offset `21032`). Its separate activity branch routes non-message subagent activity through the inline activity group path (`Lc`, near byte offset `69465`). These are static component-construction paths in the inspected installed bundle.

Combined with the public `main` source trace above, this establishes that a delivered inter-agent communication is recorded as an assistant `AgentMessage`, and that the inspected desktop renderer projects `AgentMessage` items as ordinary assistant transcript blocks. It does not establish that every communication is delivered to a particular parent, that it is sampled by the parent's next model request, or that a specific UI frame was committed or painted. No live renderer/DOM trace was captured.

### Parent model-request construction after mailbox delivery

To close the gap between history insertion and model-request construction, I inspected the separate local public Codex checkout at commit [`67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787). This is a source snapshot from the checkout, not a claim about the installed desktop build or a live session.

For an already-loaded parent, `session::handlers::inter_agent_communication` enqueues the communication and requests the pending-work scheduler only when `trigger_turn` is true or the session has outstanding durable sleep ([handler](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/session/handlers.rs#L79-L95)). Therefore a queue-only result (`trigger_turn=false`) does not by itself start an idle parent's new turn. When a turn is started, the task drains queued mailbox items into its turn-local pending-input queue ([task startup drain](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/tasks/mod.rs#L300-L330)); while a turn is active, `get_pending_input` drains mailbox items only when the current mailbox-delivery phase accepts them ([active-turn gate and drain](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs#L328-L360)). Mail deferred by that phase remains distinct from input already admitted to the current sampling cycle.

Once a communication is admitted as `TurnInput::InterAgentCommunication`, `record_pending_input` routes it to `Session::record_inter_agent_communication`; that method calls `to_model_input_item`, prepares and records the resulting `AgentMessage` in conversation history/rollout, then emits the raw response item ([input recording](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/hook_runtime.rs#L716-L782), [history insertion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L3969-L4004), [communication conversion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs#L883-L924)). At turn start, the initial input is recorded before the sampling loop. For later sampling cycles, pending input is drained and recorded before request construction ([turn input ordering](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L400-L445)). The request input is then built from cloned conversation history with `for_prompt(input_modalities)` and passed to `run_sampling_request` ([request assembly and call](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L510-L548)). `for_prompt` normalizes history and may drop unsuitable or unsupported content ([history prompt projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/context_manager/history.rs#L526-L548)).

This closes the source-level path from an admitted mailbox item to inclusion in the assembled model-facing request, conditional on a sampling cycle occurring and the item surviving history normalization/truncation. It still does not prove that a particular live message was admitted, survived context management, was transmitted, or was consumed by the remote model. The checkout was not modified; no tests or runtime calls were made. The source commit is a separate snapshot from both the installed desktop artifacts and the other public source snapshots cited above.

### Parent model-request serialization and transport boundary

Continuing the same local public source snapshot at [`67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787), `run_sampling_request` receives the history projection and the model client builds a `ResponsesApiRequest` whose `input` is `Vec<ResponseItem>` ([sampling input](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L513-L531), [request assembly](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/core/src/client.rs#L885-L895), [request type](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/codex-api/src/common.rs#L279-L304)). `ResponseItem` has a serde-tagged `AgentMessage` variant with `author`, `recipient`, and `content`; its content variants serialize as `input_text` or `encrypted_content` ([response item](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/protocol/src/models.rs#L1012-L1045), [content variants](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/protocol/src/models.rs#L908-L912)). Thus an admitted plaintext child message is represented in the outgoing request as a typed agent-message item, not rewritten into an ordinary user-role `Message` at this boundary.

Both inspected Responses transports consume that request type. The HTTP path JSON-encodes the request and posts it to `/responses` ([HTTP request encoding](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/codex-api/src/endpoint/responses.rs#L70-L99)). The WebSocket path converts the request to `ResponseCreateWsRequest`, serializes it as JSON, and sends it as a text frame ([request conversion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/codex-api/src/common.rs#L306-L330), [WebSocket serialization](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/codex-api/src/endpoint/responses_websocket.rs#L924-L926), [text-frame send](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/67a709665ac7b50311b93e32612c9a8281684787/codex-rs/codex-api/src/endpoint/responses_websocket.rs#L895-L908)). This establishes the client-side serialization and send path when one of these Responses transports is selected. It does not show which transport a particular desktop turn selected, whether a server accepted a particular live request, or whether the model attended to or used the item. No request was sent during this source inspection; the checkout remains unmodified and no tests were run.

## Cold-hydration status mismatch: desktop bundle, issue report, and TUI comparator

This section separates three evidence sources: static inspection of the installed desktop bundle, a public issue's reproduction and diagnosis, and public Codex TUI source at commit [`20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf). They are not interchangeable, and the TUI implementation is not evidence that the desktop panel has been fixed.

### Installed desktop bundle (static analysis)

The previously inspected installed-bundle status projection distinguishes a known runtime state from a child that is not currently loaded. For a known active runtime, it can project active; for a known non-active runtime state other than `notLoaded`, it can project inactive; otherwise it falls back to the latest child-turn state. The panel then displays any state other than its completed/done case under Active. Consequently, the static fallback path can classify a `notLoaded` child as Active when the turn-history fallback does not establish completion. This is a code-path possibility in the inspected bundle, not a live observation that a particular cold-reloaded child was rendered Active. See [renderer row-status derivation](#renderer-row-status-derivation-in-the-subagents-panel) and [the installed bundle evidence](#local-static-evidence).

### Public issue report (reported reproduction and diagnosis)

[OpenAI Codex issue #37042](https://github.com/openai/codex/issues/37042) reports a Windows cold-reload case in which a child returned by descendant `thread/list` has runtime status `notLoaded`, while its latest durable turn is `completed`, yet the Subagents panel displays Active. The issue author attributes the display to a merge between the state-database child result and historical parent `subAgentActivity`: the latter records started/interacted/interrupted activity but has no durable completed activity kind, and the stale running reference wins. The report also says the renderer's latest-turn fallback is used for descendants absent from the list, not descendants returned by the list with `notLoaded` status. The issue notes that `thread_spawn_edges.status=open` is persisted edge state, not a runtime status. These are claims and analysis in an open user-reported issue, not an independently verified server-side root cause or an official fix; the issue also records a macOS report of the same symptom.

### Public TUI source comparator (static source)

In current public `main` at the pinned commit above, `tui/src/app/loaded_threads.rs` constructs a tree from `thread/loaded/list` results and maps `ThreadStatus::NotLoaded` to a closed state, rather than active ([loaded-thread tree and status mapping](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/tui/src/app/loaded_threads.rs#L86-L98)). The session lifecycle refresh path separately reads thread status, treats `Active` as running and `NotLoaded` as closed, and updates picker liveness ([liveness refresh](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/tui/src/app/session_lifecycle.rs#L283-L330)). This TUI path is scoped to its loaded-thread view and lifecycle; it is a comparator only. It neither proves desktop behavior nor shows that a persisted child edge is closed or that the child's last turn completed.

The key semantic distinction is that `notLoaded` describes runtime residency, `completed` describes a turn outcome, and an open spawn edge describes persisted topology. One must not substitute for another. The issue's proposed correction remains unverified here. No desktop renderer was run or captured, no issue patch was applied, and no source tests were run.

## V2 residency quota versus cold-restored agent metadata

I rechecked the quota/resume question against public Codex `main` at commit [`20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf), which matched `refs/heads/main` when queried on 2026-10-14. This is a moving public source snapshot, not evidence of the installed desktop binary's exact source revision.

The V2 spawn path selects `V2Residency` admission for child sessions and passes `None` as the separate `AgentRegistry::reserve_spawn_slot` limit ([V2 path selection and registry reservation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L700-L735)). `V2Residency` admits a pending slot only while `residents.len() + pending_slots < capacity`; when full, it tries to evict a resident, otherwise returns `AgentLimitReached` with `ResidencyCapacity` context ([quota and failure path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L177-L213)). This identifies the in-memory admission accounting; it is not a count of persisted open graph edges.

On V2 root resume, `restore_v2_agent_metadata` reads descendants whose persisted edge status is `Open`, loads stored thread metadata without history, and commits metadata reservations with no registry thread cap; its doc comment explicitly says it restores identities without reopening runtimes ([restore implementation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L191-L270)). The root resume caller invokes this before children can be loaded lazily ([resume initialization](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager.rs#L2233-L2242)). Separately, V2 `resume_agent_from_rollout` returns before the legacy recursive reopen loop ([V2 early return](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L1341-L1372)). Thus, in this source snapshot, open edges reconstruct discoverable identities but do not by themselves repopulate V2 resident runtimes or consume the V2 resident quota.

The source test `resume_agent_from_rollout_does_not_reopen_v2_descendants` sets up a parent, worker, nested reviewer, and sibling; after shutdown and root resume it asserts the descendants are not loaded, explicitly restores metadata, checks worker/sibling identities, then closes the worker and checks that the sibling remains known and unloaded ([test source](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L1509-L1635)). This strengthens the source-level coverage for lazy restoration, but the test was inspected only and was not run; it does not exercise the issue's reported exhausted-quota failure.

The current eviction policy has an additional distinction: `is_unloadable` allows `PendingInit` when there is no active turn, outstanding durable sleep, or trigger-turn mailbox item, but the capacity-driven eviction loop explicitly skips `PendingInit` residents as “unstarted workers” ([capacity eviction selection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L230-L260), [unloadability predicate](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L313-L327)). This is relevant to quota pressure but does not establish the behavior of older packaged versions cited in issue reports.

This narrows, but does not solve, [issue #39694](https://github.com/openai/codex/issues/39694). The reporter's observation that editing persisted edge rows to `Closed` correlated with relief after restart is not independently established as the cause by this current-main path: the path restores metadata from open edges, while capacity counts residents and pending reservations. The issue's affected binaries may predate this source behavior, and another path or state interaction may be involved. The source shows explicit edge closure in `close_agent`; the inspected V2 automatic capacity-eviction path is not itself proof that a persisted edge is closed ([explicit close path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/legacy.rs#L48-L105)). The SQL workaround remains a user-reported correlation, not a recommended repair or a verified root cause.

No source tests were run and no live runtime was exercised. The key unresolved evidence gap is a version-matched reproduction of the reported post-resume spawn failure, with resident/pending state and restored metadata observed separately.

### Issue #39694 timeline and reporter correction (2026-10-14)

The issue history adds important version and reproduction context, but remains user-reported evidence rather than a trace from the inspected `main` snapshot. The original report describes Codex Desktop 26.814.41957 / embedded CLI 0.148 on Ubuntu, where child spawns failed despite few visible active children; the author also observed stale `pending_init` entries. A follow-up on 0.149.0-alpha.4.3 reported successful controlled batches, but those batches had not been run before updating, so the report does not establish that the update fixed the problem. A later long-running reproduction instead associated the failure with queue-only messaging to a nearly completed child. The reporter's proposed timing window was that terminal completion becomes visible before `active_turn` clears, briefly preventing eviction; this remains their source-based diagnosis, not independently reproduced here. ([issue timeline](https://github.com/openai/codex/issues/39694))

The issue later reports recurrence on 0.153.1 and 0.160.0/0.160.1. In the October 7 follow-up, the author corrected an earlier interpretation: they had reached nine persisted open edges while the running session's configured capacity was five and could still spawn, so open-edge count alone did not cap live residents. They still reported that changing old edge rows to `Closed` relieved the post-restart failure in repeated attempts. This narrows the claim to a restart-correlated workaround; it does not identify which persisted or in-memory state caused the original admission failure. The current-main source audit above is compatible with open edges serving metadata discovery rather than the live residency counter, but it cannot adjudicate behavior in those older packaged versions.

The issue therefore supplies at least two potentially distinct failure modes: (1) a transient live-session eviction/admission race around terminal delivery, and (2) a post-restart failure that the reporter says correlated with stale open edges. Treating them as one root cause would exceed the available evidence. No issue patch, SQL repair, or runtime reproduction was performed in this audit.

### Version comparison: 0.153.1, 0.160.1, and current `main`

I compared the public `residency.rs` snapshots at `rust-v0.153.1`, `rust-v0.160.1`, and commit `20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf`. The issue reports a live-session reproduction on 0.153.1 and later recurrence on 0.160.0/0.160.1, so the 0.160.1 tag is the closest inspected public source match for the later report. A tag match is source evidence, not proof that a desktop package was built from that exact commit.

The 0.153.1 snapshot has a simple resident LRU and pending-slot count. On a full slot request it scans residents, checks terminal status, no active turn, and no pending mailbox item, then shuts down and removes an eligible runtime. It has no residency read/write gate around delivery and eviction, no exact-`Arc` revalidation before removal, and no independently spawned teardown task ([0.153.1 admission and eviction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.153.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L73-L149), [predicate](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.153.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L217-L223)).

By 0.160.1, the capacity count is still `residents + pending_slots`, and the predicate still accepts only `Completed`, `Errored`, or `Interrupted` with no active turn and no pending mailbox items. The eviction path has materially stronger lifecycle coordination: delivery obtains a per-thread read gate; eviction takes a write gate, rechecks the exact registered `Arc`, spawns a teardown task that retains the gate through shutdown and registry removal, then removes both the thread and residency entry under the manager write lock ([0.160.1 pin and revalidation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L72-L89), [eviction protocol](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L126-L205), [0.160.1 unloadability](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L251-L257)). This makes the reporter's terminal-visible-before-evictable timing window plausible for this generation: completion status alone is insufficient while `active_turn` remains set. It does not reproduce the reported failure or prove that this was its cause.


The cold-resume identity path is not a new current-main behavior. The 0.160.1 tag also has `restore_v2_agent_metadata`: it lists persisted `Open` descendants, reads their stored metadata without history, and reserves registry entries with `max_threads: None` ([0.160.1 identity restore](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L175-L254)). `None` still increments `AgentRegistry.total_count`; it only bypasses the conditional `max_threads` check ([0.160.1 registry reservation and release](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/registry.rs#L82-L120), [counter check and reservation drop](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/registry.rs#L311-L371)). Crucially, the same 0.160.1 V2 child-spawn path selects `None` for this registry limit when it has reserved V2 residency capacity ([V2 spawn admission](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L628-L656)). Thus restored metadata increases the registry's bookkeeping counter, but that counter alone does not reject this V2 spawn path; residency admission is separate. The root-resume initialization calls metadata restoration before lazy child loading ([0.160.1 resume caller](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager.rs#L2083-L2092)). The corresponding current-main function, caller, registry semantics, and V2 spawn selection follow the same structure ([current identity restore](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L179-L256), [current resume caller](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager.rs#L2138-L2147), [current registry counter](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/registry.rs#L83-L121), [current V2 spawn admission](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L681-L714)). In both snapshots, V2 `resume_agent_from_rollout` returns before the legacy recursive descendant-reopen loop ([0.160.1 early return](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L1215-L1285), [current early return](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L1304-L1375)). Thus persisted open edges restoring discoverable identities without eagerly reopening V2 runtimes is already present in the closest inspected source tag for the report. It does not establish that the reported package used this exact tag, nor explain why the reporter saw a post-restart admission error.

Across these snapshots, residency accounting remains in-memory; persisted `Open` edges do not appear in the admission expression. The issue reporter's Oct 7 correction also says the live session could exceed its five-child resident capacity in historical open edges, while a restart with nine open edges was followed by admission failures. Since the identity-only restore path exists in both 0.160.1 and current main, a simple “new current code versus old resume behavior” explanation is not supported. The precise restart mechanism remains unresolved pending proof of the affected package's source/build identity and a version-matched trace of registry initialization and subsequent spawn admission.

### 0.160.1 lazy-load triggers after metadata restoration

The 0.160.1 app-server source shows two explicit ways an unloaded, metadata-known V2 child can become resident. A client `thread/resume` for a recorded V2 child enters the parent-owned branch and calls `ThreadManager::ensure_multi_agent_v2_child_loaded`; that method reads the persisted parent ID, requires the parent runtime to be loaded, then delegates to the parent's `AgentControl.ensure_child_loaded` ([child resume dispatch](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L3784-L3817), [parent-owned reload method](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager.rs#L1227-L1255), [owner validation and reload](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs#L160-L175)). If the parent is not loaded or the child cannot be resolved as an owned V2 child, the resume path errors and tells the caller to resume the parent first.

The other inspected trigger is delivery through the loaded parent's agent control: `send` calls `ensure_v2_agent_loaded` for a known recipient before submitting either user input or inter-agent communication ([delivery path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/api.rs#L94-L158)). These are demand-driven loads of specific children; they do not imply that root resume reopens every persisted Open descendant. By contrast, app-server `thread/read` builds its response from persisted metadata and optional live state and contains no child-load call ([read response path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L862-L883), [read view](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/rust-v0.160.1/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L2824-L2860)); the resume error explicitly points to `thread/read` as an inspection option for an unloaded child.

This identifies source-level triggers, not which one occurred in the issue reporter's desktop session. It also does not show that Desktop automatically issues `thread/resume` for every child in its panel. No desktop client trace or runtime was available, and no tests were run.

### Installed Desktop child-panel navigation versus resume

The installed `open-local-conversation-subagents-panel` asset (SHA-256 `676ecbb8172e729e5a77dc4f799cbca2f38f0aaf986bf44da9424f92e9237491`) separates opening the panel from selecting a child. Opening with no selected child creates or reuses a durable panel route and returns without invoking child history hydration. Selecting a child first validates/hydrates the requested descendant route; after that, it may request `loadBackgroundThreadHistoryPage(childId)` when no complete local conversation is present and the latest parent-derived child activity does not already cover the visible child turn. The route opener does not directly call `resumeConversation` or send `thread/resume`.

The shared implementation in `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4c4be0e870245`, `loadBackgroundThreadHistoryPageInner` around byte offset 2,570,094) attempts to fill a history page through item/turn pagination and a supplied hydration callback. The method is history hydration; its name and call site alone do not establish that the V2 child runtime is loaded. Separately, the inspected panel detail renderer passes both `canInteract` and `shouldResume` as false, while the general `use-resume-conversation-if-needed` hook sends the manager's `resumeConversation` when enabled for a selected conversation. That hook's presence proves a possible client resume path for eligible conversation views, not that panel selection invokes it.

Therefore the static bundle supports this bounded distinction: panel discovery is not a resume request; selecting a child can hydrate its displayed history; a separately eligible interactive conversation view can invoke `thread/resume`. No instrumented desktop request trace was captured, so the exact requests made by a user's panel interaction remain unverified.

### Host propagation into direct background-agent tabs

The installed conversation-page bundle resolves the selected conversation's host before opening a direct background-agent tab. It calls the shared host selector with the conversation ID; the selector prefers an explicit conversation-to-host mapping, then `"local"` if that host is among the mapped candidates, otherwise the first mapped host, followed by the current thread-context host and finally `"local"` as fallback. The resolved value is passed as `hostId` to the background-agent tab opener. This does not imply that every conversation belongs to the local host; it describes the fallback and precedence in this client snapshot.

Combined with the preceding `shouldResume = !isDurableHost(hostId)` finding, and `isDurableHost(hostId) === (hostId === "durable")`, a conversation resolved to the literal host ID `"local"` receives the default `shouldResume: true`. This remains a static control-flow conclusion, conditional on the resolved host and absent an explicit override; it does not prove that a specific user interaction sent a resume request or that the server completed it.

I also inspected local Desktop logs for `thread/resume` request-routing events. These logs contain requests from multiple app sessions and contexts; the available fields do not associate a particular request with this reverse-engineered child tab or distinguish its opener from other eligible conversation views. They therefore do not close the attribution gap and are not treated as subagent runtime evidence. No raw log lines, IDs, or user content are included here.

## Current-main lifecycle follow-up: disconnected V2 children and provisional spawn

I compared the installed CLI's matching public source tag `rust-v0.162.0-alpha.17.2` (peeled commit `740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74`) with public Codex `main` at `6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a`, queried on 2026-10-14. This section records changes after that tag; it does not establish that the installed ChatGPT Desktop app was built from either source revision.

The Oct 8 change `2fdf047c9631c9ed01a31b62efb7891718a931a8` starts lifecycle listeners for persisted V2 children even when no initialized app-server clients remain. The empty-connection branch waits for any prior unload of the same thread ID, then accepts only non-ephemeral V2 children and installs the watch/listener state ([listener attachment](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L3765-L3810)). On idle eviction, the listener marks the ID pending, attempts V2 runtime eviction, preserves the listener if the child is busy or eviction fails, and after success removes the listener, cancels outstanding requests, removes watch state, emits `thread/closed` only when it actually evicted the current runtime, then clears the pending marker ([idle eviction and cleanup](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_lifecycle.rs#L398-L470)). This addresses disconnected children whose runtimes otherwise lacked the listener needed for idle release, and serializes replacement listener setup against unfinished cleanup. It does not imply that ephemeral children are evicted: the attach branch excludes them so their context remains available for follow-up.

The same change raises the default `thread_unload_delay_secs` from 60 to 1800 seconds (30 minutes); callers can still set an explicit value ([default resolution](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs#L4031-L4039)). Its regression test covers disconnect-before-child-creation, zero- and one-second unload windows, waiting for pending cleanup, idle eviction, keeping active children loaded, and an ephemeral follow-up that sees the earlier reply ([lifecycle test](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/app-server/src/message_processor_thread_lifecycle_tests.rs#L226-L230), [disconnect and cleanup assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/app-server/src/message_processor_thread_lifecycle_tests.rs#L361-L400)). These tests were inspected, not run here.

A separate Oct 13 fix, `4e7ef7d`, keeps a child provisional through final status/config awaits after first-input admission; registry and residency reservations are committed only after those awaits, and cancellation cleanup remains armed until then ([commit-pinned spawn ordering](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L957-L973)). Its cancellation test covers cancellation while the final status lookup is blocked and checks that the child is unregistered, terminated, unloaded, and shutdown can finish. This tightens the source-defined rollback boundary; it does not establish that the desktop uses this source revision. A later Oct 13 refactor (`73bbb65`) extracts shared V2 thread preparation/spawn request construction into `core/src/thread_spawn.rs`; inspected code preserves V2 configuration preparation, agent path/source derivation, fork usage hints, and backend `SpawnRequest` options, so I record this as ownership/refactoring evidence rather than a new user-visible behavior.

No upstream source tests were run and no live child was created or evicted during this comparison. Installed Desktop bundle hashes still match the previously inspected snapshot, and runtime UI capture remains unavailable while the macOS session is locked. Keep the current-main behavior separate from installed-app observations until build provenance or a live request trace connects them.

## Current-main V2 capability discovery metadata and child-step projection

At public Codex `main` commit [`6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a), thread spawning captures the parent's selected environments unless the caller supplied an explicit environment snapshot, and places the inheritable selections plus `inherited_environments` in `StartThreadOptions` ([spawn inheritance](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L736-L797)). During child startup, inherited capability roots are selected when the session inherits isolation and is not resuming; absent explicit environment requests, a `ThreadSpawn` child derives requests from the inherited selections ([root binding](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L651-L675), [environment request derivation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager.rs#L2104-L2159)). This establishes a source path by which a child can retain the parent's selected remote executor environment; explicit child environment choices and isolation rules remain relevant.

For each step, V2 discovery returns `None` unless `ExecutorCapabilityDiscoveryV2` is enabled. It then checks selected environments, skips local ones, queries environment capability metadata, and only calls `capabilities/discoverV2` for remote executors advertising `capability_discovery_v2`; the request includes that selection's cwd and sandbox context ([gated V2 acquisition](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/src/session/mcp.rs#L517-L575)). Step preparation passes resulting snapshots into `McpServerContributionContext` before plugin selection, stores them in extension data, and retains them on `StepContext` ([step preparation and MCP contributor projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L3920-L4040)). Context contributors also receive the snapshots in `WorldStateContributionInput` ([world-state contribution](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/src/session/world_state.rs#L335-L359)). This proves availability at those contribution boundaries, not that an external contributor consumes each discovered field or renders it as a particular model-visible tool.

The separately introduced V2 metadata enrichment in commit [`20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf) parses standalone skill dependencies/policy, qualifies plugin skill names, retains standalone discovery-root identity, resolves plugin display names, and materializes object-valued inline `mcpServers` with the manifest path as provenance; missing declared MCP/Apps configuration produces warnings ([metadata and manifest handling](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/20b4ff2f35f2193608385e4caa8da8ceee45a3cf/codex-rs/exec-server/src/discoverV2/capability_discoveries.rs#L260-L350)). This is discovered metadata/configuration, not proof that skill instructions were loaded.

The current source integration test `v2_inventory_reaches_mcp_projection_without_loading_skills` uses a mock remote executor and an MCP contributor recorder. Its assertions require the V2 snapshot to reach that contributor, verify a sentinel discovered plugin, and ensure the sentinel string does not appear in model request bodies ([test assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/tests/suite/executor_discovery_v2.rs#L184-L197), [inventory and request assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/core/tests/suite/executor_discovery_v2.rs#L356-L444)). The test does not spawn a child, so it is evidence for the shared acquisition/projection behavior, not a child-specific integration test. Together with the spawn inheritance path, source supports conditional applicability to a child selecting an inherited supported remote environment. It does not establish V2 skills becoming direct model-callable skill tools; the inspected legacy V1 selected-root discovery remains a separate path. The test was inspected, not run. No live executor, child, or Desktop request was used; installed-app build provenance remains unlinked to this public `main` snapshot.

## Current-main V2 inventory versus executor skill catalog

I followed the inventory beyond the step projection into the in-repository skills extension at the same public `main` commit [`6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a). `SkillsExtension::tools_for_step` copies both the V1 capability snapshot and V2 snapshots from step data into `SkillListQuery` ([step query construction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/extension.rs#L287-L324)). The query type explicitly documents `executor_capability_discovery_v2` as “Inert V2 inventories” ([query contract](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/provider.rs#L30-L44)). The world-state catalog builder also copies those inventories into the query ([world-state query construction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/world_state_catalogs.rs#L154-L180).

The executor provider's `list` implementation branches on the V1 `executor_capability_discovery` snapshot; absent that, it walks `executor_roots` and discovers skill files through each selected environment's filesystem. It does not read the V2 inventory field ([executor skill listing](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/ext/skills/src/provider/executor.rs#L87-L142)). A source test supplies a V2 snapshot containing an error sentinel, then compares direct discovery and V1-backed discovery and asserts the catalogs match ([inert V2 parity test](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/ext/skills/tests/executor_file_system_authority.rs#L612-L660)). This is direct evidence that the current skills provider treats the V2 field as inert for skill listing, not merely an absence of a test.

There is also a direct V2 child-spawn integration test: it enables `features.multi_agent_v2`, explicitly selects an executor capability root, then has both parent and child call `skills.list` and `skills.read`; the assertions check equal catalogs and the selected plugin's skill body ([test setup and feature](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/app-server/tests/suite/v2/executor_skills_spawn_tests.rs#L25-L115), [parent/child calls and assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/6786dccbe7261b75103260475bc0a04cb36c2c0a/codex-rs/app-server/tests/suite/v2/executor_skills_spawn_tests.rs#L137-L253)). That test demonstrates selected-root skill continuity across V2 spawning. Its configuration does not enable `ExecutorCapabilityDiscoveryV2` and it does not exercise `capabilities/discoverV2`; it therefore does not contradict the inert-inventory boundary above.

Updated source conclusion: V2 agent spawning can preserve selected executor roots so child steps can use the same root-based skill catalog. Separately, the remote V2 capability inventory is projected to MCP/world-state extension boundaries, but the current built-in skills provider does not turn that inventory into the `skills.list/read` catalog. These claims are bounded to this public source commit and its inspected tests; none were run, and this does not establish the installed Desktop binary's exact implementation.

## Public-source V2 child idle eviction and state-preserving reload

I traced Codex commit [`d2a6dac20b3b76d4b95d8a699ef7bc6ee3a852d6`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/d2a6dac20b3b76d4b95d8a699ef7bc6ee3a852d6), titled “Preserve agent state when unloading idle multi-agent v2 children.” This is source and test evidence for that commit, not proof that the installed Desktop build contains or executes it.

The app-server's V2 child idle listener routes eligible unloads through `try_evict_v2_thread`; a busy runtime or an error leaves the listener active for a later attempt. On successful eviction, the core first stops the exact runtime under a residency write guard, drains unread mailbox communications back into the runtime mailbox, captures selected environments, then removes that exact registered thread and its residency record. A replaced runtime is left alone ([eviction and exact-runtime removal](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/d2a6dac20b3b76d4b95d8a699ef7bc6ee3a852d6/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/residency.rs#L34-L100); [app-server idle-unload route](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/d2a6dac20b3b76d4b95d8a699ef7bc6ee3a852d6/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_lifecycle.rs#L405-L457)). This is runtime unloading with recovery state retained, not deletion of the persisted thread history or agent identity.

On a later V2 child load, the controller reads stored thread metadata and model context, restores saved environment selections, re-registers the runtime, and returns a residency pin. The send path holds that pin while it submits the new input, so a just-reloaded child cannot be evicted before receiving the work ([reload and pin acquisition](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/d2a6dac20b3b76d4b95d8a699ef7bc6ee3a852d6/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L322-L423), [re-registration](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/d2a6dac20b3b76d4b95d8a699ef7bc6ee3a852d6/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L612-L680), [pin lifetime through message submission](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/d2a6dac20b3b76d4b95d8a699ef7bc6ee3a852d6/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents/send_input.rs#L48-L115)).

The app-server test `v2_child_idle_eviction_preserves_mail_and_followup_context` configures a zero-second unload delay, spawns one child, sends queue-only mail, unsubscribes it, and waits for `thread/closed`; it checks the child is no longer loaded while the parent remains loaded. A later `followup_task` uses the same child ID. The mock request assertions require the resumed model input to contain the earlier assistant reply and each of the queued message and follow-up exactly once ([test setup and unsubscribe](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/d2a6dac20b3b76d4b95d8a699ef7bc6ee3a852d6/codex-rs/app-server/tests/suite/v2/thread_unsubscribe.rs#L528-L678), [reload-context and exactly-once assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/d2a6dac20b3b76d4b95d8a699ef7bc6ee3a852d6/codex-rs/app-server/tests/suite/v2/thread_unsubscribe.rs#L680-L710)). I inspected this test source but did not run it; it is a mocked integration test, not an observation of the user's Desktop session.

This is distinct from the previously documented disconnected-client listener path: here the test drives actual unsubscribe-triggered child eviction and subsequent tool-driven reload, with queued mail and conversation context preserved. It still does not establish the exact installed build behavior. No source tests or runtime experiments were run in this pass.

## Public-source child-result delivery metric

At Codex commit [`41acdad2466628487911f985ece0ca2880ac6c1e`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/41acdad2466628487911f985ece0ca2880ac6c1e), the V2 completion path increments `codex.multi_agent.result_delivery` after attempting to send a terminal child result to its parent. The `outcome` attribute is `queued` when the send API returns success and `failed` when it returns an error; a metric backend failure is ignored ([metric and delivery handling](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/41acdad2466628487911f985ece0ca2880ac6c1e/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs#L107-L150)). Since the underlying result communication is queue-only (`trigger_turn=false`) and the send success indicates accepted/queued work, `queued` does not mean the parent model read or acted on the result. This adds telemetry visibility, not a delivery acknowledgement or retry guarantee. Static source only: I did not run the metric backend or observe a live emission.

## Public-source resolved model and reasoning effort in V2 subagent activity

At Codex commit [`b0a6b8d86f455d3db9dd869fc1178a5fbd865e7f`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/b0a6b8d86f455d3db9dd869fc1178a5fbd865e7f), the V2 spawn path captures the child startup config and model metadata after initial-input admission, resolves an unset effort from the model default, then normalizes it through that model's supported effort mapping. The code comment says this is captured before the child can publish later-turn metadata; the accompanying test distinguishes the reported resolved effort from the child setting (for example, an explicit Ultra can be reported as XHigh while the child retains Ultra). This is a startup snapshot for activity/telemetry, not a second config mutation after the child starts ([spawn snapshot and resolution](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/b0a6b8d86f455d3db9dd869fc1178a5fbd865e7f/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L914-L930); [resolution test](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/b0a6b8d86f455d3db9dd869fc1178a5fbd865e7f/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control_tests.rs#L2087-L2141)).

The same change adds optional `model` and `reasoning_effort` to the core subagent activity item and event, populates them for the Started activity, and carries them through the typed app-server `ThreadItem::SubAgentActivity` and history builder. Completed activity and non-spawn interactions use `None`; old history can therefore omit these fields ([core item contract](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/b0a6b8d86f455d3db9dd869fc1178a5fbd865e7f/codex-rs/protocol/src/items.rs#L361-L369); [app-server item contract and mapping](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/b0a6b8d86f455d3db9dd869fc1178a5fbd865e7f/codex-rs/app-server-protocol/src/protocol/v2/item.rs#L390-L405), [history projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/b0a6b8d86f455d3db9dd869fc1178a5fbd865e7f/codex-rs/app-server-protocol/src/protocol/thread_history.rs#L1020-L1032)).

In the inspected public TUI source, `sub_agent_activity_display` and its history-cell renderer destructure the item with `..` and use kind/path/thread ID only; they do not display the new model or effort fields ([TUI projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/b0a6b8d86f455d3db9dd869fc1178a5fbd865e7f/codex-rs/tui/src/multi_agents.rs#L287-L319)). Thus the commit makes resolved startup metadata available to typed app-server clients and persisted item history, while the inspected TUI does not render it. A separate inspection of the installed Desktop bundle confirms that its selected-child details panel renders model settings from the child thread record, but does not establish that these activity-item fields populate that selector (see [installed Desktop panel evidence](#installed-desktop-panel-renders-selected-child-model-settings)). Source and test definitions inspected; no tests were run, and no live app-server event was captured.


## Installed Desktop panel renders selected-child model settings

A read-only extraction from the installed ChatGPT Desktop 26.1007.30912 `app.asar` (SHA-256 `75e397e2280dde93b70a85aa926f4282c728eacc84dfeb15ba7fb6ddd84628af`) shows that `local-conversation-subagents-panel-tab-2ab06de7bd1d.js` (SHA-256 `345278adc9f056901c6e0b8d64c98b09d6d3e12019c2ec20be8567b100196eef`) renders a selected subagent's model and reasoning effort in its details header. The component imports `kF` as `m` and calls its selector for the selected `{hostId, threadId}`, formats the model, and renders localized reasoning labels when the effort is recognized (asset byte offset 4313). The shared bundle export table maps `lci` to `kF` (offset 8107902), connecting the component import to the `modelSettings` selector. The message description explicitly identifies this as the selected subagent details header.

The same bundle's `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`) defines a `modelSettings` selector backed by a conversation's `latestModel` and `latestReasoningEffort` (offset 2632700); its thread projection initializes those fields from the thread record's `model` and `reasoningEffort` (offset 2670285). This establishes the inspected Desktop panel's static rendering path and the model-settings source used by the panel. It does not prove that the V2 `SubAgentActivity` item's `model`/`reasoning_effort` fields populate that thread record or selector: no such bridge was established in this pass. Nor does static component construction prove the panel mounted, rendered a particular child, or painted pixels in a live session. The installed app version and ASAR hash scope this result; this is not a claim about every Desktop release. No UI interaction or live notification capture was performed.

## Installed Desktop separates activity-item updates from thread model settings

A second pass through the same shared bundle clarifies how these values enter the UI store. Its `modelSettings` reader projects `latestModel` and `latestReasoningEffort`. Thread-list upsert/merge code initializes those values from the server thread record's `model` and `reasoningEffort`; the merge helper only applies later metadata while explicit `latestThreadSettings` are absent and the locally observed model/effort still match the read-start snapshot (function `v7t`, around character offset 2676670; thread-list merge `_7t`, around 2673485). This preserves a locally selected thread setting against stale background metadata. Hydration similarly captures the model-settings snapshot before `thread/read` and uses it when reconciling the response (around 2721069 and 2719193).

The store has a separate item-change path. When `setConversation` receives changed item IDs, it notifies `subagentActivity` readers based on the thread's activity projection; thread-field readers, including `modelSettings`, are compared and notified separately (around 2691324–2692583). This proves distinct store projections and notification checks, not that the underlying server notifications are causally independent: one conversation update can affect both projections.

There is, however, an identity-mediated route from a spawn/activity item to the thread-record model settings. The item updater calls `SubagentTopology.discoverForSpawn` for completed `collabAgentToolCall(spawnAgent)` items and `subAgentActivity(started)` items (around 2824786). `discoverForSpawn` takes the receiver/agent thread ID and, when exactly one child is present and its recorded parent matches the current parent, calls `refreshAndDiscoverSubtree`; otherwise it falls back to general discovery (around 3075313). That refresh captures the child's model-settings snapshot, requests `thread/read` for the child, then calls `upsertConversationFromThread` on the returned thread record (around 3075665). The thread upsert projects the record's `model` and `reasoningEffort` into the same latest model/effort state consumed by the panel selector. Separately, the `thread/started` notification handler directly upserts its full `params.thread` record as live state (around 2832240).

Thus the static bundle establishes an indirect route: activity supplies child identity, topology refresh reads that child's thread record, and the thread record can update the model-settings projection. It does not establish that the activity item's own `model` / `reasoning_effort` fields are copied into, or are the source of, the fetched thread record. No live notification was captured and no UI render was observed; these findings remain scoped to the exact installed bundle hashes above.

## Installed Desktop has separate Work and local-conversation subagent panels

The installed ChatGPT Desktop `app.asar` identified above has distinct entry points and identity models for Work threads and local conversations. The Work panel is registered for the `chatgpt-thread` route and selects rows by `threadId`; the local-conversation panel uses `conversationId` and a durable Subagents tab. The latter's route and conditional history hydration are detailed in [child-panel navigation versus resume](#installed-desktop-child-panel-navigation-versus-resume) and [the local panel model-settings path](#installed-desktop-panel-renders-selected-child-model-settings). This section records the Work-specific response path and the separation between these consumers.

The Work panel module does not issue the child-list request itself. Its `p` import resolves to `vin` in `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js`; that projection parses `codex_sub_agent_activity` and `codex_collab_agent_tool_call` metadata from conversation messages and reduces them into child rows, per-thread activation IDs, and activity groups. The reducer maps provider statuses (`pendingInit`/`running`/`completed`/`interrupted`/`shutdown`/`errored`/`notFound`) to UI waiting/working/done/failed states, and also uses activity events, completed `wait` calls, and the latest user-message boundary to infer row status. The detailed mapping and its limits are recorded in [Work panel status projection](#work-panel-status-is-projected-from-conversation-message-metadata). Thus the panel's child list is a client-side transcript-metadata projection; the exact upstream transport that supplied those conversation messages remains outside this finding. The panel consumes `projection.subagents ?? []` and uses `projection.activationIdByThreadId` for its separate completed-child response query. Panel asset SHA-256 is `406de5e3b812c5fb5a60ab34c01085e829d13c69fec6eb0d11c22e830d231442`; shared projection bundle hash and offsets are in the linked section.

The caller narrows the input boundary. In `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`), `dD` starts near byte offset 1,862,698 and derives `f` by passing the conversation object through `Gd(e)` (or `Tm(e)` for active-turn-only mode), then `hin(..., context_truncation_continuation)`. In conversation mode it calls `vin(f, {isTurnLive: a})` around offset 1,864,787 and later associates each projected activity item with the matching source message by ID. This establishes that the Work projection consumes the renderer's normalized message list derived from the current conversation object. It does not identify how that conversation object's mapping was populated or prove that `setConversation` in `app-shared` is its direct producer; the cross-store/transport edge remains unknown. A second caller in the same bundle narrows the renderer state source further: the derived selector `jgl` (near offset 11,235,733) reads `mapping` from the `NT` conversation mapping selector and `current_node` from `DT`, then passes `{conversation_origin, current_node, mapping}` to `dD`. `NT` itself resolves the conversation ID through `wT` and reads the corresponding `$w` mapping state (selector definition near offset 1,533,373). This identifies the immediate UI-state inputs but still does not connect that `$w` mapping's population to the separate `app-shared` thread-store notification reducer. The producer relationship remains unproven. No live event was captured.

The same bundle exposes the renderer-side population path for `$w`. The `Wk` snapshot applier (around offset 2,243,132) writes the conversation mapping and current node. The `pDn` history loader (around 2,258,772) can first apply a validated provisional snapshot from the renderer's local conversation-snapshot cache during hydration, then await its `load` callback and apply the returned server snapshot with `Wk`; its query caller `WDn` (around 2,267,425) supplies `Xk` (around 2,263,871), which fetches `/conversation/{conversation_id}` (around offset 2,265,025). The same loader uses the `/conversations/{conversation_id}` history response when paging mode is requested (around offset 2,264,499). Separately, the conversation WebSocket subscription handles `conversation-update` events: `Nsr` (around 2,979,603) parses message-bearing updates or marks the conversation for refetch, `wsr` (around 2,971,238) applies recognized messages to an already-loaded mapping or invokes `jsr`, and `jsr` (around 2,977,011) fetches a fresh snapshot, merges it with retained active-branch messages, and calls `Wk`. Thus static code identifies local-cache hydration, server-history fetch, and WebSocket update/refetch as renderer `$w` producers. It still does not establish a causal bridge from app-shared's app-server `item/started`/`item/completed` reducer to this conversation query or WebSocket stream; no live request/event or rendered panel was observed.

A separate ingestion path is visible in `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`). Its app-server notification reducer `Ktn` handles `item/started` and `item/completed` by locating the thread and turn, converting the item through `qtn`, and adding/updating the turn item (`Km`) (around offsets 2,821,830–2,823,800). `qtn` leaves `subAgentActivity` items unchanged; it enriches `collabAgentToolCall` with currently known receiver-thread records. The thread store's `setConversation` calls `threadItemIndex.update` and separately notifies subscribed `subagentActivity` readers when changed items alter that projection (around offsets 2,689,942 and 2,691,798); the item index exposes its activity projection through `activity()` (around 2,663,489). On completion, a `collabAgentToolCall` with `tool === "spawnAgent"` or a `subAgentActivity` with `kind === "started"` also triggers `subagentTopology.discoverForSpawn` (around offset 2,825,187), which can refresh/read the identified child thread. This establishes an app-server-to-thread-store item path and a child-discovery side path in the installed renderer. It does **not** establish that this store path is the transport that populates the Work panel's separate conversation-message input to `vin`; that exact cross-store/source relationship remains unknown. No live notification or UI event was captured.

## Installed Desktop conversation socket notifies the local thread catalog

The same ChatGPT Desktop 26.1007.30912 `app.asar` (SHA-256 `75e397e2280dde93b70a85aa926f4282c728eacc84dfeb15ba7fb6ddd84628af`) contains a second, narrower connection between the renderer conversation path and desktop thread infrastructure. In `webview/assets/app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`), the conversation WebSocket setup subscribes to `conversations` and `alder-conversations` through `/celsius/ws/user` (initializer `Hsr`, around byte offset 2,982,048). Accepted conversation events reach `Wz` (around 2,971,238), which reads the current host ID and calls the injected `Pd.localThreadCatalog.notifyThread({hostId, threadId: conversationId}, action)`; the reconnect path invalidates that host's catalog source (around 2,982,852). This is an identity bridge from a conversation event to a desktop catalog notification, not evidence that the socket payload is inserted into the app-server thread-item store.

The desktop implementation is in `/.vite/build/main-CqEoQDOU.js` extracted read-only from the same ASAR (SHA-256 `09d19386e1f23cf4ccbc950fc40d85a1e8a064a08be588b2a4be6a89f6ada9e6`). Its `notifyThread` method (around byte offset 2,175,646) maps `action === "remove"` to `thread/archived` and other actions to `thread/unarchived`, then forwards the notification to the host's catalog coordinator. The coordinator handles archive/delete by removing the catalog entry; an unarchive notification with a thread ID schedules a per-thread refresh, while `invalidateSource` schedules source reconciliation (around offsets 3,173,970 and 3,184,020). The refresh updates catalog records; this inspected path does not carry conversation message bodies into `Ktn`, the turn-item store, or Work's `vin` projection.

The source factories make the catalog boundary more explicit. For app-server-backed hosts, factory `l5` (around byte offset 3,199,166) pages through `listThreads` and reads individual records with `readThread(threadId, {includeTurns:false})`, mapping thread records into catalog entries. For the ChatGPT host, factory `bJe` uses `/conversations` and `/conversations/batch`; its `u5` mapper projects identifiers, titles, timestamps, project/origin/type and async status, not transcript turns. The coordinator's `refreshThread` calls the selected source's `readItem`; notification-driven reconciliation therefore refreshes catalog entries through those source contracts.

The renderer's catalog observer is not entirely isolated from conversation state. In `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js`, the catalog subscription buffers observations until initialization, then calls the host thread manager's `observeCatalogThreads` (around byte offset 8,968,536). In `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js`, `observeCatalogThreads` updates `threadsById`, recent-thread summaries, and—if a conversation is already loaded—selected metadata such as originator, environment, title, and eligible runtime status (around offset 2,702,387). `getCachedThreadMetadata` exposes such catalog entries as records with `turns: []`, empty preview, null path, and `status: {type:"notLoaded"}` (around offset 2,703,840).

The corresponding `refreshRecentConversations({mode:"catalog"})` branch (around offset 2,713,980) marks catalog history as the source, clears/rebuilds recent summaries through catalog observations, filters the existing recent-conversation ID list to active/pinned/unread/pending/active-runtime conversations, notifies subscribers, and returns. Unlike the routine/expanded branch immediately following it, this catalog branch does not call `loadThreadHydrationState`, `listRecentThreads`, or `hydrateThreads`. This supports a precise boundary: catalog synchronization can update renderer thread metadata and summaries, and can merge metadata into an already-loaded conversation, but this inspected catalog-refresh path does not load or replace transcript turns. Other explicit hydration paths (for example selecting/opening a thread, pinned-thread hydration, or background history loading) remain separate and can load thread history; no live request/event or UI interaction was captured here.

This narrows the earlier “bridge unknown” finding: there is a confirmed conversation-WebSocket-to-local-thread-catalog bridge using `(hostId, conversationId/threadId)`. Catalog source reads and the `mode:"catalog"` refresh update catalog/thread metadata and summaries, not transcript turns in the inspected path. The separate app-server `item/started`/`item/completed` reducer and Work's conversation-message projection remain distinct observed paths; a causal bridge from this catalog notification to either item ingestion or Work activity rows is still unproven. Evidence is static in the exact installed bundle; no live socket event, catalog mutation, endpoint response, or rendered panel was observed.

For completed Work children, the client uses a dedicated query: `/flora/subagent/thread/turns` with `conversationId`, `threadId`, and `limit: 1`. It scans returned items backward, prefers a nonempty `agentMessage` with `phase === "final_answer"`, and otherwise retains the first nonempty agent message found in that reverse scan (`chatgpt-subagent-final-response-query-b2b6816e81df.js`, SHA-256 `43b96d88ea4b71bdda82e04d2e137531c0ce726e17559de7bde9be018d9d5012`; query definition around offset 630). If no candidate message exists, the query throws a dedicated “not available yet” error. Its retry callback allows that error twice, with delay `min(250 * 2^failureCount + random[0,100), 1000)` ms; `retryOnMount` is `!isTurnLive`, `refetchOnWindowFocus` is false, and stale time is infinite. Thus `isTurnLive` controls the mount-retry option, not whether the query's retry callback permits those two retries. The query key includes activation, conversation, and thread identity. The Work panel and opener hashes are respectively `406de5e3b812c5fb5a60ab34c01085e829d13c69fec6eb0d11c22e830d231442` and `a973875c237d3e85ef6b164a4e6a8e1a5f00d6ed10ac7e4d869fed657384aace`.

This establishes two different static UI data paths: Work child completion text is fetched through the dedicated endpoint/query, while the local-conversation panel navigates to a child transcript and may hydrate its history. It does not establish a successful endpoint response, a mounted/rendered panel, or runtime resumption. The local opener asset SHA-256 is `676ecbb8172e729e5a77dc4f799cbca2f38f0aaf986bf44da9424f92e9237491`; the selected-asset manifest records the 12-file selection (47,682 bytes, zero parse failures, coverage complete within selection), input digest `1a53bed15b359e5a20dbcdfd1a63f756632680fb758211827328c3a18dcacecc`, parent Evidence ID `ev_c90cbf10dd5b7e1602962bbefab5a74fd627896067e37e239516ff38515f24a5`, and summary Evidence ID `ev_b585a9b8953cee0b3b4f730506e3b60b613e542b6aeed7d466b4ec6decc6e3cb`. Semantic graph coverage remains `unknown` with 2,316 unknowns, so it does not support complete cross-module resolution. No endpoint request or live UI observation was made.

## Installed Desktop Work projection is fed by the renderer conversation mapping

In the same ChatGPT Desktop 26.1007.30912 `app.asar`, `jgl` in `webview/assets/app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`) selects the current node and mapping from the renderer conversation state, then calls `dD`. In conversation mode, `dD` derives the normalized message list from that mapping/current node and passes it to `vin`; the Work panel's activity rows are therefore projected from the renderer conversation transcript state, rather than directly from the app-shared thread-item index. The relevant selector and projection offsets are approximately 11,235,733 (`jgl`), 1,862,698 (`dD`), and 1,864,787 (`vin` call).

The history query factory `WDn` (around offset 2,267,425) calls `pDn`.
