During hydration, `pDn` may first apply a validated local conversation snapshot provisionally, then awaits its supplied history loader and applies the server result through `Wk` (around offsets 2,258,772 and 2,243,132). Its normal loader calls `Xk`, which requests `/conversation/{conversation_id}`; when a limited-history response is used, it may request `/conversations/{conversation_id}` and fetch earlier pages from `/conversations/{conversation_id}/messages`. The pagination converter `RDn` turns the flat message page into a parent/child mapping rooted at `paginated-root:<conversation_id>`, with a current node and pagination metadata (around offset 2,263,871). `Wk` writes the resulting mapping/current node into the renderer `$w`/`Gw` state. If a conversation is already loaded, it uses merge helpers (`NEn` or `Mln`) to reconcile the fetched snapshot with existing branch, streaming, and local state; this is not always a blind replacement.

The WebSocket path supplies another producer for the same renderer mapping. `Nsr` parses supported conversation updates into message operations or refetch requests; `wsr` applies recognized messages when the conversation mapping is loaded, otherwise invokes `jsr`; `jsr` calls `Xk`, merges the fresh snapshot with retained mapping entries, and applies it through `Wk` (around offsets 2,979,603, 2,971,238, and 2,977,011). This closes the static data path from renderer history/socket handling to the transcript projection consumed by Work's `vin`.

The boundary to the app-shared `item/started`/`item/completed` reducer remains unproven. The inspected catalog notification path does not carry message bodies into either store, and no live request, WebSocket event, store mutation, or rendered panel was captured. Findings are static and specific to the installed bundle hash above; semantic cross-module graph coverage is incomplete.

## Work response-query activation IDs are transcript message IDs

Follow-up inspection of the same `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`) resolves how `vin` builds `activationIdByThreadId`. The reducer `yin` begins around byte offset 1,787,726; its normalized input parser `Sin` is around 1,790,533, and the tool classifier/status mapper `Tin`/`Ein` is around 1,790,826. It stores the source `messageId` as the activation value when it sees a nonterminal `subAgentActivity` (`started` or `interacted`), a `spawnAgent`/`sendInput`/`resumeAgent` tool call for a receiver, or a per-agent provider state of `pendingInit`/`running`. Because transcript contributions are traversed in order and later qualifying events overwrite the map entry, the selected activation is the latest qualifying message ID for that child in the supplied transcript.

Terminal `subAgentActivity` kinds (`completed`/`interrupted`) do not replace that value. Nor do completed `wait` calls: they may mark currently waiting/working rows done while leaving the activation key unchanged. A child with no qualifying event receives the synthetic key `initial:<threadId>`. The Work final-response query uses this activation value together with the parent conversation ID and child thread ID as its cache/query identity, but the inspected request passes only `conversationId`, `threadId`, and `limit: 1` to `safeGet`—the activation value is not sent as an endpoint parameter (query contract and asset hash are recorded in [Work panel status projection](#work-panel-status-is-projected-from-conversation-message-metadata)). Thus “activation ID” in this client projection is a transcript-derived cache discriminator, not evidence of a server-issued execution ID or a distinct child thread. It can change when a later spawn/send/resume or active-state message appears in the transcript.

This is static client logic only. It does not establish that every qualifying message corresponds to a newly started backend run, that the query returns a response for each key, or that the UI displayed a particular cached result. Scope is the installed Desktop bundle hash listed above.

## Invalid Work activity metadata is ignored per record

The same `app-initial` resource shows the parser boundary around `vin`. `Cin` (byte offset 1,790,908) checks only whether either metadata key is non-null; if present, `vin` proceeds even before schema validity is known. `Sin` (offset 1,790,615) then runs `safeParse` independently for `codex_sub_agent_activity` and `codex_collab_agent_tool_call`, exposing each parsed value only when that full record succeeds. The schemas are initialized by `Ain` (offset 1,791,372): activity records require string `agentPath`, `agentThreadId`, and `id`, plus a recognized `kind`; collaboration records require `agentsStates`, `id`, `receiverThreadIds`, `senderThreadId`, recognized status and tool values. Their literal `type` tags are optional. Per-agent state requires a recognized status, while its message is optional and nullable.

Consequently, a non-null but malformed record is silently represented as `null` in `Sin` and does not create/update a child row or activity group. The other metadata record on that message, and valid records on other messages, are parsed independently and can still contribute. If the transcript contains metadata keys but every associated record is invalid, `vin` returns a non-null projection with no rows/groups rather than taking its “no metadata” early return. This is a static parse-path conclusion; no malformed live payload was injected and no warning/telemetry behavior was evaluated.

## Work activity blocks group contiguous metadata contributions by turn

In the same `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` asset (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`), `xin` (around byte offset 1,790,359) assigns each normalized transcript message a turn key. It uses the message's turn ID when present, carries the most recent turn ID forward within a user-message interval, and otherwise uses a fallback key based on the user-message count. A user message increments that count and resets the carried turn ID before resolving the current message's key.

`vin` (around offset 1,786,793) parses all messages with `Sin`, then scans those contributions in transcript order. A contribution is groupable only when it has a successfully parsed `codex_sub_agent_activity` or `codex_collab_agent_tool_call`. Consecutive groupable messages remain in one run only while their turn keys match. A non-groupable message—including a message whose activity metadata was present but failed schema parsing—or a turn-key change flushes the current run. Each flushed run is passed to `bin`, which reduces its child rows; when the resulting row list is nonempty, `vin` stores one `chatgpt-subagent-activity` item in `activityItemByMessage`, keyed by the source transcript message at the run's final index. Thus the inline block is attached to the last message in a contiguous run, not emitted once per metadata-bearing message. Runs that produce no rows are omitted.

The item's `completed` flag is set when the run is not in the current live parent turn, or when every projected row has a status other than `waiting`/`working`. This is an aggregate flag for the inline group. `dD` (around offset 1,864,698) looks up the group by source-message identity and emits it as an assistant transcript item adjacent to that final source message. Separately, `vin` returns the overall `subagents` rows used by the Work panel; those rows and the inline group share a reducer but are distinct projections.

These are static renderer semantics for the installed Desktop asset. They do not prove which exact serialized events arrived in a live transcript, what React ultimately rendered, or whether a group corresponds to one backend execution. In particular, turn IDs may be absent and then the renderer uses its fallback user-message boundary. No live event or UI render was captured.

## Work inline activity items enter the generic reasoning-group projection

The same `app-initial` asset continues the path after `dD` emits the `chatgpt-subagent-activity` transcript item. The conversation renderer `jan` (around byte offset 1,811,901) passes the projected items through `Man` and then, in the ordinary non-parent-projection path, through `qin`. `qin` delegates to `Jin` (around offset 1,796,984). Its eligibility predicate `lan` (around offset 1,804,167) explicitly includes `chatgpt-subagent-activity` among items eligible for reasoning grouping.

`Jin` builds `chatgpt-reasoning-group` items and places eligible source items in their `items` array; group boundaries depend on neighboring item eligibility and source-message grouping conditions. Therefore the inline activity item is carried as a nested item of the renderer's generic reasoning-group projection, rather than remaining a standalone top-level projected item at this stage. The streaming-parent branch uses `Nan` instead of `qin`, so this finding is limited to the ordinary path and should not be generalized to every projection mode.

This identifies a client-side projection boundary, not the React component or final pixel rendering of that nested item. The inspection did not capture a live conversation, selected group, expanded state, or rendered UI. Evidence is static in `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`) from ChatGPT Desktop 26.1007.30912.

## Installed conversation renderer has a dedicated inline subagent activity component

The same `app.asar` contains `webview/assets/chatgpt-conversation-turn-content-fb0cd9e95f87.js` (SHA-256 `e8353444de8909488419ca71bbbadf347b89ca67f36fbd30d0185c5253487571`). Its turn-content renderer `xN` (around byte offset 427,312) passes `chatgpt-reasoning-group.items` to `wN` (around 428,805). For a Work conversation, `wN` delegates to `kN` (around 439,470) when the double-texting presentation and streaming-parent region are both absent and the reasoning recap is not `keep_inline`. That Work-specific branch converts the first activity of each `chatgpt-subagent-activity` item into an activity-unit record (`subagent-activity` with thread ID, display name, and a display status), registers one `mw` React element for the source item in `standaloneActivityContentByItemId`, and passes the resulting unit list and inline-content fragment to `pv`. In the ordinary `wN` rendering loop, `chatgpt-subagent-activity` is excluded from generic activity-unit conversion and is passed to `fN` for direct item rendering; `fN` has a branch that creates `mw` for that item.

`mw` (around offset 118,640) maps each activity to a compact item whose label is the display name and whose click handler selects that activity's thread ID in the conversation's subagents panel. It renders `tv` with the accessible label “Subagent activity,” aggregate status, live status announcements, and test ID `chatgpt-subagent-activity`. This confirms a dedicated React rendering path and the intended click-through behavior in the installed renderer. It still does not prove that this branch mounted for any particular conversation, that a user actually saw it, or how the imported `tv` component looked at runtime; no live UI was captured.

The app archive hash is `75e397e2280dde93b70a85aa926f4282c728eacc84dfeb15ba7fb6ddd84628af`, matching the inspected ChatGPT Desktop 26.1007.30912 installation. This is static bundle evidence only.

## Installed inline activity component summarizes names and lifecycle status

Following the `tv` import in `chatgpt-conversation-turn-content-fb0cd9e95f87.js` resolves it to export `b` from `webview/assets/sites-end-resource-78b98c374310.js` (SHA-256 `4897e9b701726b0874a5036157ea32eb85932a04669ab9e205b820623f8be4a3`). The implementation is `tk` (around byte offset 356,084); its item renderer `nk` starts around 359,275. `tk` returns no section for an empty item list. For one to three activities, its translated status sentence includes each available agent label. For larger groups it names the first two, reports `N-2 more`, and renders up to four compact seed/avatar chips. A named item with a selection callback is a keyboard-operable inline button; the remaining compact items use tooltip-wrapped buttons when selectable. The overflow phrase is a button that calls the renderer's overflow callback, which invokes the subagents panel selection route with a null selected thread ID.

The status passed by `mw` is the `np` import, which resolves through the `app-initial` export map to `_in` in `webview/assets/app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`). `_in` (around byte offset 1,786,502) returns `interrupted` if any activity kind is interrupted; otherwise, when no activity status is `waiting` or `working`, it returns `failed` if any row failed and `completed` otherwise. While work remains, an `interacted` row still working yields `updated`; all other live combinations yield `active`. `tk` uses that value to select lifecycle wording and puts the summary in an `aria-live="polite"` span under the “Subagent activity” section label.

These findings identify the component's static content and interaction rules, not a captured DOM, mounted React tree, screenshot, or runtime click. The translation catalog also contains a `progress` wording, but the inspected `_in` call path does not return that status. Asset hashes were checked against files extracted from the installed archive whose SHA-256 is recorded above.

## Work activity renderer creates one panel unit per inline activity item

Follow-up inspection distinguishes the Work-specific `kN` path from the ordinary `wN` item loop. `xN` forwards the reasoning group's items to `wN`; when `isWorkConversation` is true, no double-texting presentation or streaming-parent region is present, and the recap is not `keep_inline`, `wN` delegates the Work layout to `kN`. In `kN` (around byte offset 439,470), each `chatgpt-subagent-activity` item reads only `activities[0]`. If it exists, the code appends one `subagent-activity` unit using that activity's thread ID and display name, with display status set to `interrupted` for that activity kind, otherwise `updated` when the source item is completed, otherwise `active`. It also stores one `mw` element keyed by that unit ID; `mw` itself maps the complete `item.activities` array into the inline summary component. Thus a multi-activity source item can show multiple compact names in its inline content while contributing only its first activity as a unit in this `kN` activity list.

The ordinary `wN` loop has a different behavior: it excludes `chatgpt-subagent-activity` from generic activity-unit conversion, closes any accumulated generic activity slice, and renders the item through `fN`, whose dedicated branch creates `mw`. The Work `kN` path groups its `h` rows with `vv`, then passes the units and inline-content fragment to `pv` together with `hasInlineSubagentActivity`, `hasFinalAssistantStarted`, and `isTurnCancelled` (call near byte offset `444,671`). For that call, the `hasFinalAssistantStarted` value is `hasFinalAssistantStarted || completed`, while `isTurnCancelled` receives `wasStopped`. In the inspected `sites-end-resource-78b98c374310.js` asset (SHA-256 `4897e9b701726b0874a5036157ea32eb85932a04669ab9e205b820623f8be4a3`), the renderer import `pv` resolves through export `v` to component `hk` (export map near `413,050`; component near `363,286`). `hk` treats either nonempty units or inline content as renderable activity. It builds the unit rows with `jO`, appends the inline-content fragment after those rows, and inserts a separator when both are present. Within `jO`, standalone units reach `UO`; `UO` looks up `standaloneActivityContentByItemId` by the unit item's exact ID and returns the registered `mw` element. This ties the inline summary element to the matching activity unit in the static component tree.

`hk` wraps the assembled content in `vk`, the collapsible activity renderer. Its `mk` rule permits collapse when `allowCollapseBeforeFinal` is true or when the passed final-assistant flag is true and the turn is not cancelled, provided renderable activity exists. The inspected `kN` call passes `hasFinalAssistantStarted || completed` as that flag and `wasStopped` as `isTurnCancelled`; it omits `allowCollapseBeforeFinal`, `forceExpanded`, `disableCollapse`, `preventAutoCollapse`, and `persistedCollapsed`. With those props omitted, collapse is not allowed before the final assistant starts (or the turn completes); when allowed, `hk` initially computes a collapsed state unless its local state has changed. This establishes the static composition and collapse rules, not a mounted DOM, pixel layout, or captured user-visible result.

## Inline Work activity summary selection updates the parent-keyed Subagents panel

The summary's `mw` component imports `n` from `open-chatgpt-subagents-panel-2ef970567216.js` as `_y` (renderer asset `chatgpt-conversation-turn-content-fb0cd9e95f87.js`, SHA-256 `e8353444de8909488419ca71bbbadf347b89ca67f36fbd30d0185c5253487571`, `mw` near byte offset `118,640`). A named activity click calls `_y(scope, {conversationId: parentId, selectedThreadId: childId})`; the overflow callback calls the same opener with `selectedThreadId: null`. Thus this summary's click path selects a child in the parent conversation's Subagents panel rather than directly opening a child conversation tab.

The opener asset (SHA-256 `a973875c237d3e85ef6b164a4e6a8e1a5f00d6ed10ac7e4d869fed657384aace`, `x` near byte offset `8,921`) opens a panel tab with ID `chatgpt-subagents:<parentId>`, and stores `selectedThreadId` in its props. Its `onSelect` prop writes a newly selected child ID back through the same opener. The matching `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`) resolves the opener's imported `openTab` to `Gj` (near byte offset `2,465,001`), which dispatches to the right-panel controller for this route; the controller's `w` function derives the tab ID from `getId`, and `revealAndFocus: true` activates the selected tab. If a tab with that ID already exists, its update path retains its panel instance identity and replaces its props before activation, so repeated selections for the same parent update/reuse that tab rather than creating another one. `chatgpt-subagents-panel-e608a4ff5e56.js` (SHA-256 `406de5e3b812c5fb5a60ab34c01085e829d13c69fec6eb0d11c22e830d231442`) searches the current projected `subagents` rows for the selected ID and renders its detail; its back action clears the selection. The opener dynamically imports this `ChatGptSubagentsPanelTab`; this is distinct from the separately named `LocalConversationSubagentsPanelTab` used by local conversation routes. This establishes client tab-controller semantics from static code, not persistence across app restarts.

This is distinct from the local conversation page's child-avatar route, where `canInteract` selects between a direct `background-agent` tab and the Subagents panel. The inline Work summary itself passes only parent and child IDs to the panel opener; this call path does not branch on `canInteract`. These are static client routing semantics. No live click, route transition, mounted UI, or server request was captured.

Following the selected row into the Work detail branch shows a second distinction: when the projected child status is `done`, the detail component reads the final-response query using parent ID, child ID, and activation ID; that query targets `/flora/subagent/thread/turns`. Other statuses render the row's available status message or preview path instead. The inspected selected-detail route does not invoke the interactive local-thread renderer or a resume hook. This is a static UI/query path only; selecting a child here has not been observed to issue a network request or resume the child.

This is static evidence in the installed Desktop 26.1007.30912 chunk (SHA-256 `e8353444de8909488419ca71bbbadf347b89ca67f36fbd30d0185c5253487571`). No live Work conversation, rendered DOM, or panel interaction was captured. The `kN` first-activity behavior should not be generalized to the ordinary `wN` path.

## The Work Subagents panel is omitted from the inspected durable tab-route snapshot

The `open-chatgpt-subagents-panel-2ef970567216.js` opener (SHA-256 `a973875c237d3e85ef6b164a4e6a8e1a5f00d6ed10ac7e4d869fed657384aace`) registers its tab type through imported `wgn` (`Mj` in `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js`) with `getId`, `isAvailable`, `renderThumbnail`, and `open` fields. This registration object has no `durableRoute`, `serialize`, or `restore` field. The generic `Mj` helper at byte offset 2,437,642 copies the supplied configuration and adds the component and tab kind; it does not synthesize a durable route.

The matching `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`) shows the consequence in the inspected persistence path. `Nj` (around byte offset 2,438,263) returns the tab's `durableRoute` when it is null; `ORn` (2,438,449) only returns a persistable route when that result is non-null. The stored-tab snapshot builder `n5a` (6,354,172) filters every tab through `ORn`, builds `routes` from the surviving descriptors, and includes only workspace-home tabs or tabs with those descriptors in `tabIds`; `activeTabId` is retained only if it is in that set. The route restorer `ARn` (2,438,665) also requires the registered tab type to have a `durableRoute` and delegates to its `restore` method. On this path, the `chatgpt-subagents:<parentId>` panel tab is therefore absent from the durable route snapshot and has no route-specific restore handler.

This narrows the earlier tab-reuse finding: a same-parent tab is reused while its controller state is live, but the inspected durable panel-route mechanism does not restore this panel after restart. This is static evidence from the matching installed bundle, not an observed restart. It does not rule out another persistence mechanism outside the inspected tab-route path.

### Follow-up: trace the filtered snapshot through checkpoint save and restore

The route filter is used by the higher-level checkpoint lifecycle, not only by an isolated serializer. In `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (same SHA-256 above), `t5a` combines the right and bottom controller snapshots from `n5a`; `n5a` includes only tabs for which `ORn` produced a durable route descriptor. `Geo` watches the conversation-scoped `YP` checkpoint and, when tab state changes, calls `t5a` and merges the result through `yeo` before updating `YP`. Thus a visible Subagents panel without a `durableRoute` is absent from the checkpoint's `routes` and controller `tabIds` at the point the checkpoint is refreshed.

The checkpoint also feeds workspace draft transitions. `Qto` stores the checkpoint in `savedDraftTabs$` when a persistent workspace session/account is available, otherwise it writes through `Seo.setDraft`; `Zto` reads those saved draft records back. `Reo` carries a draft checkpoint onto the conversation ID when a client thread becomes a conversation, while `Leo` reads the conversation-scoped `YP` checkpoint during startup and passes it to `zeo`. `zeo` delegates route entries to `Beo`/`Veo`, then restores the selected tab topology through `Heo`. These call sites confirm that the same route-filtered checkpoint is used on save and restore paths. They do not establish the storage engine or prove that every app restart uses one specific branch; the finding remains limited to this inspected checkpoint and durable-route mechanism.

This follow-up is static analysis of the installed bundle. No restart was performed, and no local storage/database contents or live restore event were inspected.

## Local-conversation Subagents panel registers a durable route and restorer

The local-conversation panel is a separate tab type from the Work panel above. In the same `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` bundle (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`), the registration around byte offset 6,397,620 sets `kind` to `subagents`, declares `durableRoute.version: 1`, and provides a `restore` callback. The tab ID helper at 6,397,434 returns `subagents:<conversationId>`. The route schema accepts nullable `selectedConversationId`; serialization returns the current descriptor unchanged. The opener in `open-local-conversation-subagents-panel-5abfac9190b9.js` (SHA-256 `676ecbb8172e729e5a77dc4f799cbca2f38f0aaf986bf44da9424f92e9237491`) constructs this descriptor with `payloadVersion: 1` and the selected child ID.

The registered restorer validates payload version and params, requires target `right`, a `local-thread` scope, and the parent-derived tab ID, then delegates to `restoreSubagentsTabRoute` in `restoration-ef6d1498039d.js` (SHA-256 `0e0a17d64389503e0a5f543bfa2ff8647613a2cbaf3adbf4746794c605a81d17`). That helper obtains the host ID from the scope's conversation mapping and calls the local panel opener with the original tab ID and `openOverviewWhenSelectionUnavailable: true`. When the restore reason is `persistence`, it deliberately passes `selectedConversationId: null`; for other restore reasons it passes the descriptor's selected child. It reports success only if the opener returns the original descriptor tab ID.

Therefore the inspected durable checkpoint mechanism can reconstruct the **local-conversation** Subagents panel, while the separate **Work** panel opener lacks a durable route and is filtered out of that same route snapshot. For the local panel, the persistence restore preserves the parent panel route but not the selected child detail. This is static evidence from the matching installed bundle; no restart or live restore event was observed, and the finding does not establish that every launch takes this path.

## Local-conversation Subagents overview merges membership sources and hydrates selected children

The panel component `local-conversation-subagents-panel-tab-2ab06de7bd1d.js` (SHA-256 `345278adc9f056901c6e0b8d64c98b09d6d3e12019c2ec20be8567b100196eef`; component `F` near byte offset 615) selects rows from `kJ` (`Dl` export) with the parent conversation and host. The selector in `app-primary-6f60e9ac4103.js` (SHA-256 `7fe9c00b03cb1de3ebc3a4bf298fc44b13a7fe67ca9cc335a9e2ecda969a1321`) reads the parent conversation turns, indexed subagent activity, cached conversations, thread summaries, and the `OJ` descendant snapshot. Its row builder `Sat` (byte offset 1,108,149) merges parent-turn references, cached child conversations, source-linked descendant threads, and active thread summaries; deduplicates candidates; then computes membership/state from child and parent turns, activity/tool-call references, discovery completeness, and runtime status. `Cat` sorts the returned rows by recency. The panel renders all non-`done` rows in Active and `done` rows in Done, with separate visible row limits.

The `OJ` store is initialized by `AJ` (byte offset 1,120,639). When the selector subscribes to this store for the parent key, its mount handler calls `discoverSubagentDescendantSnapshot(parent,{reconcile:true})`, publishes the returned descendants, subscribes to descendant thread-item activity, and listens for `thread/started`, archived, deleted, unarchived, and parent `resumeState` changes. Relevant notifications trigger another discovery pass. This is the panel's descendant-membership refresh path; it does not imply that each child's full transcript is loaded on overview restore.

Selecting a child takes a distinct path. The opener `open-local-conversation-subagents-panel-5abfac9190b9.js` (SHA-256 `676ecbb8172e729e5a77dc4f799cbca2f38f0aaf986bf44da9424f92e9237491`; function `x` near byte offset 354) first calls `verify-descendant` (`verify-descendant-d02f27822163.js`, SHA-256 `dc7baa2e1adc3b9b469f2e766c3b88ea25cdecd098534a9e39ee7c2b88596554`). That helper checks parent/child identity and ancestry, rejects cycles or a conflicting host, may hydrate missing ancestors without turns, and finally calls `ensureConversationFirstPage` for the selected child. The opener additionally loads the selected child's history page when no usable local child conversation or qualifying current-parent activity turn already supplies the needed content. Persistence restoration sets `selectedConversationId` to null, so this selected-child path is not part of the overview-only restore itself.

The panel's selected detail is rendered through `local-conversation-thread`; its thread view has a separate history query in `thread-e7a2ada76342.js` (SHA-256 `dc860dbd8eaed9b159d2e5d11b11777c20de422c759f26af795dbda4b9faa4b9`, query definition `D` near byte offset 350) calling `loadBackgroundThreadHistoryPage` when history is needed. This is distinct from the Work panel's `/flora/subagent/thread/turns` final-response query. The evidence establishes static client data paths, not which requests ran for a particular conversation or whether the panel rendered successfully. No live restore, click, request, or transcript hydration was observed.

### Follow-up: `turn_finished` emits the result-delivery counter through the metrics helper

In the installed arm64 CLI identified above, `turn_finished`'s boxed future poll at `0x1049d1a70` reaches the post-delivery branch at `0x1049d31cc..0x1049d3270`. It obtains `MetricsClient::active_inner` at `0x1049d323c`, builds an `outcome` attribute, and selects the string `queued` or `failed` from the delivery result. The PC-relative string references resolve inside `__TEXT,__cstring`: `0x10b34fadb` begins `outcome`, `0x10b34fee8` begins `failed`, `0x10b351af8` begins `queued`, and `0x10b351afe` begins `codex.multi_agent.result_delivery`. The poll passes the metric-name pointer and the selected attribute data to the anonymous helper at `0x107bc77f4` (`BL` at `0x1049d3270`).

The named `codex_otel::metrics::client::MetricsClient::counter` entry at `0x107bc67c8` obtains the same active inner object and calls that exact helper at `0x107bc6824`, with the corresponding metric arguments forwarded. This cross-call structure supports identifying the `turn_finished` branch as the installed binary's result-delivery counter path, rather than merely noting that the binary contains the metric string. The matching source at [`completion.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/completion.rs#L112-L141) places `metrics.counter("codex.multi_agent.result_delivery", 1, ... outcome: queued/failed)` after awaiting `send_inter_agent_communication`, and returns after logging if delivery failed. In the matching [`control.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control.rs#L253-L323), successful unloaded delivery means the message was enqueued to the recipient mailbox; successful loaded delivery means the `Op::InterAgentCommunication` request path returned successfully. Thus `queued` is a submission outcome, not an acknowledgement that the parent model consumed the result. Source correlation identifies the semantic label; it does not turn static inspection into proof that the branch ran in a live desktop session.

This finding is static analysis of the Mach-O with SHA-256 `0d5ac7aae20c4eda6242428d5e218c55ba96d8763fda2a271088ff37104b9d28`. `__TEXT,__cstring` has VM address `0x10b078558` and file offset `185042264`; string addresses above were calculated from that section mapping and checked against the bytes at those file offsets. No runtime metric export or subagent completion was captured, and the anonymous helper's Rust type remains unknown.

### Follow-up: parent mailbox delivery is gated by a turn phase

At pinned source commit [`740e5af`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74), the parent-side intake path separates message receipt from adding that message to the current model request. [`session/handlers.rs::inter_agent_communication`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/handlers.rs#L80-L95) enqueues each message in the session input queue, emits the receive activity, and asks the pending-work scheduler to start an idle turn only when `trigger_turn` is true or the parent has outstanding durable sleep. [`input_queue.rs::enqueue_mailbox_communication`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs#L205-L219) first imports older runtime-mailbox entries and then appends the new submission, preserving FIFO order. [`get_pending_input`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/input_queue.rs#L400-L430) drains both turn-local input and queued mailbox messages only when the active turn's mailbox phase is `CurrentTurn`; otherwise it returns without draining the mailbox.

The phase starts at `CurrentTurn`. [`stream_events_utils.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/stream_events_utils.rs#L112-L135) switches it to `NextTurn` after recording nonempty assistant final-answer text (an untagged assistant message is treated as final; finalized item facts can carry the same decision). At that boundary, late mailbox messages stay queued so they do not extend already-recorded visible answer text. If pending input already contains explicit same-turn work, the defer helper does not close the phase. [`session/inject.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/inject.rs#L15-L90) reopens the phase while accepting injected user input; a tool call after a prior answer can reopen it as well, and [`session/turn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L548-L562) reopens mailbox intake after a model response that needs a follow-up. The state comments and [`session/tests.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/tests.rs#L12757-L13050) describe and cover those cases.

This refines the earlier `queued` metric boundary: a successful result submission can be accepted by the parent session while current-turn consumption remains deferred. A queued message is not necessarily part of the next model request immediately; turn phase and follow-up work determine when it is drained. The pinned source includes tests for queue-only and `trigger_turn` mail after the answer boundary, for user-steer reopening, and for tool-call reopening. Those tests were inspected as source evidence but were not run in this investigation. This is static analysis of the exact source snapshot; it does not establish a live parent turn's queue contents or model request.

The installed CLI's Mach-O symbol table independently retains `LocalAgentControl::take_mailbox` at `0x1049dda5c`, `LocalAgentControl::watch_mailbox` at `0x1049ddda0`, `Mailboxes::enqueue` at `0x1049fa6bc`, `Mailboxes::remove` at `0x1049fa2c8`, and `Session::maybe_start_turn_for_pending_work` at `0x104a05de4`. This corroborates that the corresponding named mailbox and wake-scheduler components are present in the inspected binary. The phase-gating details above are still derived from version-matched Rust source; these symbols alone do not identify the optimized `TurnInputQueue` state transitions or prove that any method executed in a particular run.

### Follow-up: parent mailbox communication becomes a typed model input

At pinned source commit [`740e5af`](https://github.com/openai/codex/commit/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74), the path from accepted parent input to the sampling boundary preserves inter-agent provenance. [`SubmittedTurnInput` conversion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn_input.rs#L899-L904) keeps `InterAgentCommunication` as its own `TurnInput` variant. [`record_pending_input`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/hook_runtime.rs#L716-L772) routes that variant to `Session::record_inter_agent_communication` and then materializes the rollout.

The recorder calls [`InterAgentCommunication::to_model_input_item`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/protocol/src/protocol.rs#L859-L892), which produces the dedicated `ResponseItem::AgentMessage` variant. Plaintext communication becomes `InputText` content while retaining author, recipient, optional ID, and internal metadata passthrough. The encrypted-content branch adds a text header containing `NEW_TASK` or `MESSAGE`, recipient/task name, sender, and payload marker, followed by the encrypted content. The API helper `to_response_input_item` is a different conversion and is not the path used here.

[`record_inter_agent_communication`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L3917-L3974) prepares and records this response item under the communication boundary, reserves and attaches `user_input_order`, waits for earlier pending code-mode message recordings, and persists both `InterAgentCommunicationMetadata { trigger_turn }` and the `ResponseItem` to the rollout. Thus `trigger_turn` remains distinct scheduling metadata rather than being collapsed into the plaintext item. This source path also calls `send_raw_response_items` for event delivery; that call is not itself evidence that the model consumed the item.

At sampling time, [`turn.rs`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L526-L544) builds `sampling_request_input` from `clone_history().for_prompt(...)` and passes that `Vec<ResponseItem>` to `run_sampling_request`; [`build_prompt`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L1572-L1600) stores the vector directly in `Prompt.input`. The history projection [`for_prompt`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/context_manager/history.rs#L597-L614) removes history envelopes but keeps their response items. Its normalization maintains tool call/output pairing and strips unsupported image/audio content; `AgentMessage` is accepted as an API message and has no rewrite to an ordinary user-role message in this path ([normalization and API-message classification](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/context_manager/history.rs#L954-L972)). History truncation or other request-affecting policy can still affect whether a particular earlier item remains in a later request.

The source tests [`record_inter_agent_communication_sets_turn_id_in_rollout_and_resume`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/tests.rs#L2744-L2820) assert the recorded `AgentMessage`, input order, `trigger_turn` rollout metadata, and restoration of the item. These tests were inspected but not run. This closes the source-level path to the prompt-construction boundary; it does not prove that any specific live communication survived truncation and was sent in a particular request, nor does it establish model-side consumption.

The matching installed arm64 CLI (`0.162.0-alpha.17.2`, SHA-256 `0d5ac7aae20c4eda6242428d5e218c55ba96d8763fda2a271088ff37104b9d28`, Mach-O UUID `2CE1483B-FF35-344F-9958-9D48FFE5D5ED`) retains the named symbol `InterAgentCommunication::to_model_input_item` at `0x107ca4304`. Static disassembly at that address corroborates that the implementation is present in this binary. The symbol and disassembly do not establish runtime invocation or, without validating the struct layout, justify assigning exact semantics to every field offset. Source tag and installed version match, but no signed build manifest proves this binary was built from this exact commit.

### Follow-up: the typed agent message reaches the serialized Responses request

The prior section established the source path to `Prompt.input`; this follow-up traces the request builder and transport encoder at the same pinned commit. [`ResponseItem`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/protocol/src/models.rs#L1012-L1049) is a Serde internally tagged enum (`type`, snake_case), so the `AgentMessage` variant serializes with `type: "agent_message"` and its author, recipient, content, optional ID, and optional passthrough metadata. Its content components are separately tagged (`input_text` / `encrypted_content`) ([definition](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/protocol/src/models.rs#L906-L911)).

[`build_responses_request`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/client.rs#L891-L953) clones the formatted prompt input, may filter unsupported `ConfigurationUpdate` items, and prepends instructions/tools when appropriate. For non-OpenAI providers it clears internal chat metadata passthrough on each item; the match does not replace or remove `AgentMessage`. Before HTTP submission, request preparation can clear unsupported item IDs and content-item kinds ([`prepare_response_items_for_request`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/client.rs#L1016-L1025)). The optional 15 MiB soft bounding path is specifically gated on executed-tool-call metadata and sheds that metadata; it does not rewrite agent-message content ([`bounded_input`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/client_tool_metadata.rs#L10-L40)).

`ResponsesApiRequest` is `Serialize` and has `input: Vec<ResponseItem>` ([definition](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/codex-api/src/common.rs#L278-L304)). The HTTP `/responses` `stream_request` encodes that request with `EncodedJsonBody::encode(&request)` before sending it ([endpoint](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/codex-api/src/endpoint/responses.rs#L70-L96)). For WebSocket transport, `ResponseCreateWsRequest` borrows the same request input and `serialize_websocket_request` serializes the request enum to JSON ([request mapping](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/codex-api/src/common.rs#L306-L326), [serialization](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/codex-api/src/endpoint/responses_websocket.rs#L911-L914)). The turn client passes the constructed request to the HTTP stream or WebSocket path after request preparation ([HTTP caller](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/client.rs#L1747-L1783), [WebSocket caller](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/client.rs#L2017-L2070)).

This supports a source-level conclusion stronger than prompt construction alone: absent earlier truncation, a preserved `AgentMessage` is carried as a typed item into the core's serialized Responses request for either transport. It still does not prove that a particular live message survived all earlier history policy, that a provider accepted or interpreted this item, or that a model consumed it. No outgoing request was captured in this analysis; source tests were not run. Request contributors and downstream transport/provider behavior remain outside this static path claim.

### Follow-up: request contributors cannot rewrite the input vector through their API

The extension API's `ModelRequestInput` exposes request kind, thread ID, mutable `client_metadata`, and model identifier; it has no reference to `ResponsesApiRequest.input` or `Prompt.input`. `ModelRequestContributor::request` returns a `ModelResponseInterceptor`. In the core `prepare` implementation, the contributor receives a temporary metadata slot; accepted additions are filtered and merged into request metadata, while the returned interceptors are collected separately. At the HTTP generation call site, `prepare` receives `&mut request.client_metadata`, then the request is passed to `ApiResponsesClient::stream_request`; the interceptors are attached to the returned response stream by `map_response_stream` / `intercept_stream`. Thus this extension point can add allowed client metadata and wrap response events, but the inspected API does not permit it to mutate or replace the outgoing typed input item. The interceptor documentation notes that trusted host implementations may synthesize model-visible content, which concerns response-stream events and should not be confused with rewriting the request input.

The transport evidence narrows the remaining boundary rather than closing it: HTTP encodes the request as JSON and calls the configured session transport for `POST /responses`; WebSocket constructs a request borrowing the same `input` and serializes the request enum to JSON. This establishes the client-side serialization and dispatch path only. Provider gateway/server interpretation, request rewriting beyond the inspected client, and model consumption remain unobserved. Source links: [`ModelRequestInput` and contributor contract](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/ext/extension-api/src/model_request.rs#L19-L42), [`prepare` metadata handling](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/model_request.rs#L10-L46), [HTTP request encoding](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/codex-api/src/endpoint/responses.rs#L70-L96), and [WebSocket input mapping and serialization](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/codex-api/src/common.rs#L306-L326).

### Follow-up: Bedrock catalogs enable the client-side collaboration tools

The provider capability boundary is not equivalent to “first-party OpenAI only.” The pinned source's Bedrock model catalogs are built from bundled OpenAI model metadata, and the runtime catalog tests explicitly expect `MultiAgentVersion::V2` for most listed models and V1 for the two listed GPT-5.6 Luna variants. The integration test `bedrock_catalog_selects_v2_by_default` covers both Bedrock Mantle and Runtime provider IDs. It leaves the `MultiAgentV2` feature flag disabled and the custom model catalog absent, submits an ordinary turn to a mocked Responses endpoint, then asserts the resolved multi-agent version is V2 and the emitted request includes the `collaboration.spawn_agent` and `followup_task` tools.

This establishes that the Codex client can configure collaboration for those catalog entries and serialize the collaboration tools on its shared Responses API path. The test does not call a real Bedrock service, ask a model to invoke a tool, create a child thread, or verify `AgentMessage` acceptance by Bedrock. It is provider-catalog and client-request evidence only. Sources: [Bedrock Mantle/Runtime integration test](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/tests/suite/bedrock_multi_agent_tests.rs#L15-L61), [runtime catalog capability assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/model-provider/src/amazon_bedrock/runtime_catalog_tests.rs#L56-L147), and [Bedrock API-provider construction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/model-provider/src/amazon_bedrock/mod.rs#L184-L188).

### Follow-up: V2 selection precedence explains the disabled-feature Bedrock test

The catalog test's V2 result is consistent with the config resolver. `Config::multi_agent_version_override` returns V2 when `Feature::MultiAgentV2` is enabled, returns Disabled when agents are globally disabled, and otherwise returns no override. `multi_agent_version_for_model` then chooses that override first, the selected model's catalog metadata second, and the feature-derived fallback last. With V2 disabled and agents enabled, a Bedrock catalog entry marked V2 therefore resolves to V2 even if the legacy `Collab` fallback would select V1 or Disabled. At session startup the runtime metadata/history can also pin a version; when the session first resolves a model-specific version, it stores the selected result for that thread. Sources: [`Config` precedence](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/config/mod.rs#L1602-L1628), [session initialization and history resolution](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L496-L512), [per-model resolution](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L4185-L4196).

The local V2 spawn/reload path checks the resolved parent/thread multi-agent version, including V2 ownership checks for reloaded children. This ties the selected client version to local control-flow eligibility; it still does not establish that a remote Bedrock model invokes the exposed tool or that its service accepts agent-message input. Source: [V2 child validation and loading](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L300-L405).

### Follow-up: provider adapters inspected here do not rewrite AgentMessage

The provider abstraction's documented default resolves `ModelProviderInfo` to the API provider configuration; its provider-specific hooks cover capabilities, authentication, error mapping, and endpoint configuration. Bedrock's override resolves its runtime base URL and converts provider metadata to the shared `codex_api::Provider`. In the shared core path, `normalize_image_details` explicitly leaves `ResponseItem::AgentMessage` unchanged, and request preparation only clears unsupported item IDs and content-item kinds. A source search across `codex-rs/model-provider/src` found catalog metadata references to multi-agent versions but no provider-specific `AgentMessage` transformation. This supports the bounded claim that the inspected client adapter path does not rewrite the item; it cannot establish what a gateway or remote provider does after dispatch, nor rule out transformations outside these inspected source paths. Sources: [provider abstraction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/model-provider/src/provider.rs#L103-L127), [Bedrock provider construction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/model-provider/src/amazon_bedrock/mod.rs#L184-L188), [shared item normalization](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/client_common.rs#L69-L107), and [request item preparation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/client.rs#L1016-L1025).


### Follow-up: V2 spawn tool namespace is preserved through runtime dispatch

The turn's tool-plan builder registers the V2 spawn handler only when collaboration tools are enabled and the resolved version is V2. It takes the configured V2 namespace, then wraps the handler when namespace/schema/description overrides are present. The wrapper advertises a namespace-grouped function spec, returns the namespaced `ToolName`, and delegates `handle`, payload-kind matching, exposure, and parallel-call behavior to the underlying handler. The bundled V2 spawn handler itself reports the plain name `spawn_agent` and accepts function payloads. This separates the stable handler implementation from its configured public tool identity.

When a Responses `FunctionCall` is converted into a `ToolCall`, the protocol namespace, function name, call ID, and argument payload are retained. `ToolRouter` looks up the runtime using the resulting full tool name; dispatch builds `ToolInvocation` with that name, ID, payload, source, and current session/turn context. The registry then selects the runtime by tool name. A name with no registered runtime returns `FunctionCallError::RespondToModel`; a payload kind mismatch returns `Fatal`. Thus a configured namespace is part of executable routing identity, not merely display decoration. This analysis follows the static registration and dispatch path; it does not establish how every provider serializes namespaced calls or how an error is represented in a particular live model turn.

Sources: [`add_collaboration_tools`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs#L1374-L1423), [`multi_agent_v2_handler` and override wrapper](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/multi_agent_tool.rs#L21-L125), [`ToolRouter` response conversion and dispatch](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/router.rs#L245-L297), [`ToolInvocation` construction](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/router.rs#L347-L379), [`ToolRegistry` selection and payload validation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/registry.rs#L533-L607), and [V2 spawn handler identity](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs#L43-L62). Source tests were not run.

### Follow-up: V2 spawn results become parent conversation items

The V2 spawn handler's `SpawnAgentResult` serializes a `task_name` and optional nickname, then converts that result into a successful function-call output tied to the original call ID. `AnyToolResult::into_response` preserves that call ID and payload while wrapping the converted output as a response-item envelope. The direct tool-call path returns this envelope; the sampling path collects in-flight tool futures in `FuturesOrdered` and, after the response stream ends, drains them into `record_annotated_conversation_items`. This establishes the client-side path from a successful spawn result into the parent's recorded conversation history, including ordered collection for that batch.

The recorded item can become a candidate for a later prompt through the shared history path, but this code alone does not establish that another model request necessarily occurs: turn control flow, cancellation, compaction, or subsequent input may affect that. The previously observed local CLI V2 probe did record spawn and wait calls, but the source mapping here does not independently identify that probe's exact serialized result bytes. Sources: [V2 spawn result conversion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/handlers/multi_agents_v2/spawn.rs#L313-L333), [`AnyToolResult::into_response`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/registry.rs#L197-L220), [direct tool-call response path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/tools/parallel.rs#L68-L112), [in-flight result collection and history recording](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L2467-L2493), and [sampling-loop drain](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L3158-L3174). Source tests were not run.

### Follow-up: tool outputs normally trigger another sampling step

The previous section left the later-request transition conditional. The pinned `handle_output_item_done` path resolves the normal function-call case by immediately recording the assistant's call item, scheduling its handler future, and setting `OutputItemResult.needs_follow_up = true`. The sampling loop accumulates that flag; once the response stream ends, it drains in-flight handler futures and records their response envelopes into conversation history. Back in `run_turn`, `needs_follow_up` is the OR of the model's explicit `end_turn=false` signal and pending input; when follow-up is required and no rollover/error path exits, the outer loop continues. The next sampling input is then rebuilt from `sess.clone_history().for_prompt(...)`, which includes the recorded tool outputs subject to normal history normalization/truncation.

Thus for a successfully dispatched V2 spawn call on the ordinary continuation path, the client-side source chain reaches another sampling step with the call/result pair in the prompt candidate history. This does not prove the provider actually receives the item in a particular live request: context rollover/compaction can intervene, cancellation and errors can terminate, history normalization can alter retained items, and transport/provider behavior lies beyond this local control-flow proof. Sources: [function call persisted and queued with follow-up flag](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/stream_events_utils.rs#L315-L358), [follow-up aggregation and outer-loop continuation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L540-L670), [next sampling input from current history](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L1647-L1668), [history prompt projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/context_manager/history.rs#L597-L614), and [tool-result drain before sampling completion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L2467-L2493). Source tests were not run.

### Follow-up: distinguish model-visible tool output from UI lifecycle items

The ordinary in-flight tool-result path calls `record_annotated_conversation_items`; the shared recorder persists each result to history/rollout and emits a `RawResponseItem` event, which app-server forwards as `rawResponseItem/completed`. This is a raw model-response event, not by itself a rendered transcript card. The separate `TurnInput::FunctionCallOutput` ingress path in `hook_runtime.rs` explicitly creates and emits `ItemStarted`/`ItemCompleted` lifecycle events, but that is not the ordinary in-flight handler drain used after `spawn_agent`.

The pinned protocol also makes the presentation boundary visible: `TurnItem::FunctionCallOutput` maps to no legacy event in `as_legacy_events`. V2 app-server can represent a lifecycle item as a typed `ThreadItem::FunctionCallOutput`; in the inspected TUI transcript projection, generic history cells skip that item, and replay only adds visible history for the special delegated-tool-output format. Thus the source supports this bounded conclusion: ordinary subagent tool results are retained and broadcast on the raw-response channel, while the inspected TUI does not render every function output as a normal transcript cell. Parent-side `SubAgentActivity` status cards are separate activity items. This does not establish the UI behavior of every desktop-client surface or arbitrary tool output.

Sources: [tool-result drain](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn.rs#L2467-L2493), [shared history persistence and raw event emission](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/session/mod.rs#L3500-L3643), [app-server raw-response notification](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/app-server/src/bespoke_event_handling.rs#L1164-L1176), [separate function-output lifecycle ingress](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/core/src/hook_runtime.rs#L744-L764), [legacy event mapping](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/protocol/src/legacy_events.rs#L546-L557), [TUI history-cell projection](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/tui/src/thread_transcript/other_items.rs#L128-L143), and [TUI replay's delegated-output special case](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/740e5af33c71225640e0c1c1555c514c2c93ab74/codex-rs/tui/src/chatwidget/replay.rs#L434-L452). No runtime desktop capture or source tests were performed.

### Follow-up: desktop subagent activity rows carry status and an open action

The installed desktop bundle's shared resource chunk `sites-end-resource-78b98c374310.js` (SHA-256 `4897e9b701726b0874a5036157ea32eb85932a04669ab9e205b820623f8be4a3`) exports `yE` as export `I`. The local conversation chunk `local-conversation-turn-632904a8790c.js` (SHA-256 `f16c75de8c65e6849f3976cc8e0efec198e7b063ec81ce514ff85b348967b376`) imports that export as `Ey` and calls it from the subagent presentation component. `yE` joins activity items to background-agent metadata by normalized child thread ID and constructs row objects containing `activityStatus`, `canOpen`, `conversationId`, `displayName`, and `status`. When the background-agent record belongs to the current parent turn, its status is used; otherwise the activity display status supplies the fallback. The row component maps each row to its display label and installs an `open(conversationId, displayName)` click action when `canOpen` is true (local-conversation-turn chunk near byte offset `57,360`; shared resource function near `247,948`).

The row payload passed to this inline activity component contains no child answer or transcript text. This supports the narrow claim that this activity-row path presents identity/status and can open the child conversation; it does not establish whether another desktop surface displays the child's final answer. Bundle inspection only: no live conversation, event stream, or UI interaction was captured.

### Follow-up: desktop activity state is derived from indexed parent history and refreshed by thread events

In `app-primary-6f60e9ac4103.js` (SHA-256 `7fe9c00b03cb1de3ebc3a4bf298fc44b13a7fe67ca9cc335a9e2ecda969a1321`), the background-agent projection scans parent-turn items. Its `wat` helper creates memberships from `subAgentActivity` items (excluding `interacted`) and `collabAgentToolCall` entries whose tool is `spawnAgent`; the latter may begin without a display name. The `Sat` aggregator combines those memberships with cached child conversations, thread summaries, source-linked descendant threads, and indexed subagent items. `Dat` builds the latest parent-turn reference from activity/tool-call records. `kat` then derives row status from the latest reference, child visible/ordinary turn state, thread runtime status, and whether descendant discovery is complete. These are derived client presentation records, not the child's answer transcript.

The same bundle contains a mounted scoped store atom that watches indexed parent/descendant thread items for new `subAgentActivity(kind: started)` and `spawnAgent` references, subscribes to host notifications `thread/started`, `thread/archived`, `thread/deleted`, and `thread/unarchived`, and watches the parent thread's `resumeState`. Relevant notifications enqueue a reconciled `discoverSubagentDescendantSnapshot(parentId, {reconcile: true})`; discovered descendants are added to the watched set. The snapshot refresh loop serializes overlapping requests, ignores stale generations, and disposes subscriptions when the atom is unmounted. The derived aggregator also reads per-thread cached conversations and summaries and touches thread/item cache entries for discovered children.

This supports a client-side event-driven refresh path for descendant membership and activity state. It does not show the backend's implementation of `discoverSubagentDescendantSnapshot`, guarantee notification delivery, or prove a particular live row visibly updated. Relevant offsets in this asset: `wat` 1,110,299; `Sat` 1,108,149; `Dat` 1,112,302; `kat` 1,114,902; descendant discovery and subscriptions approximately 1,120,000–1,123,800. Static bundle inspection only; no live session or runtime event trace.

### Follow-up: descendant discovery is a bounded client-side reconciliation workflow

The `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` bundle (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`) exposes `discoverSubagentDescendantSnapshot` as a facade over a `SubagentTopology` coordinator. Its snapshot getter returns currently known descendants with `isComplete: false`; discovery has separate in-flight maps for whole-subtree and direct-child scans. With reconciliation enabled, the coordinator captures runtime status before the asynchronous scan, reconciles discovered threads against that snapshot, and reuses overlapping in-flight work.

The scan sends background `thread/list` requests in pages of 200, restricted to `archived: false`, `sourceKinds: [subAgentThreadSpawn]`, `useStateDbOnly: true`, sorted newest first. It selects `ancestorThreadId` when the installed app-server version supports the filter; otherwise it starts from `parentThreadId` and walks discovered children. For thread-list responses, it checks each returned child's parent identity before accepting it in the fallback walk. A repeated cursor stops the scan and leaves `isComplete: false`; the loop also stops once a page contains a thread older than the root's known creation time.

To find relationships absent from the thread list, the client collects observed `spawnAgent` receiver IDs and `subAgentActivity(kind: started)` agent IDs. The cloud-history fallback pages `thread/items/list` (500 items per request); for a thread with paginated history and no cached conversation, it scans `thread/turns/list` (5 turns per request). Candidate children are read with `thread/read` without turns, and accepted only when the returned ID matches and the child's parent matches the thread being scanned. At most two candidate reads run concurrently. A failed read, identity mismatch, failed history read, or incomplete paginated-topology read lowers completeness; valid descendants remain available as partial results. The cloud-history scan is attempted when thread-list filters are unsupported and can also make completeness unknown on failure.

This is the installed client's discovery/reconciliation algorithm, not a server-side implementation of a dedicated descendant-snapshot RPC. It shows how the client combines thread metadata and persisted history, applies identity checks and a concurrency bound, and represents partial knowledge. It does not prove server completeness, delivery of any underlying request, or live UI behavior. Static bundle evidence only; no app-server calls were issued.

### Follow-up: reconciliation preserves concurrent runtime updates and distinguishes complete scans

In `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js`, `ThreadStore.reconcileSubagentDescendantSnapshot` uses the pre-scan runtime-status map to detect state that changed while discovery was running. For a returned thread, a newer cached thread record can win over the fetched record by `updatedAt`; a runtime-status update that differs from the captured pre-scan value wins over the thread's status. The method updates thread records, summaries, runtime-status evidence, and any already-loaded conversation state, then replaces the summary collection.

The `isComplete` result gates cleanup of known-but-not-returned descendant summaries. When complete, descendants absent from the result are treated as idle unless their reconciled runtime evidence is `active` or `systemError`; those active/error records are retained. When incomplete, this absent-descendant cleanup pass is skipped, so a partial scan does not make unseen descendants appear idle. The returned collection also appends cached or summarized descendants that are still known active but were absent from the discovery result, when their thread record and status are available.

The adjacent status helpers use a thread's `parentThreadId`, falling back to `source.subAgent.thread_spawn.parent_thread_id`, to walk ancestry and decide descendant membership. For status selection, `needs_resume` and `notLoaded` thread state can defer to summary evidence; otherwise loaded thread runtime status takes precedence over summary status. These rules explain how discovery results affect the client cache and prevent a slow scan from blindly overwriting some concurrent status changes. They do not establish that runtime status is server-authoritative or perfectly current. Static bundle evidence only; no discovery was executed.

### Follow-up: activity-row labels are a projection with explicit suppression rules

In `app-primary-6f60e9ac4103.js` (SHA-256 `7fe9c00b03cb1de3ebc3a4bf298fc44b13a7fe67ca9cc335a9e2ecda969a1321`), `kat` projects a membership and latest parent reference into the row status. Its activity-state mapping is `pendingInit -> waiting`, `running -> active`, `completed -> done`, and `interrupted` / `errored` / `shutdown` / `notFound -> hidden`. A row with a `closeAgent` latest tool is suppressed. Hidden activity is suppressed unless thread runtime status is active; `systemError` is suppressed; and a child's last turn status `failed` or `interrupted` is suppressed unless runtime status is active.

For a row that remains visible, a known runtime status maps only `active` to the visible status `active`; other non-null runtime statuses map to `done` (with `systemError` already filtered above). If runtime status is absent, discovery completeness is checked first: complete means `done`; otherwise child-turn state maps to `active` or `done`, then activity state can provide `waiting` / `done`, with `active` as the fallback. The row's `statusSummary` is populated only for projected `active` rows. This is a UI projection, not a faithful enum of provider/core terminal outcomes; some error/interruption states hide the row instead of displaying an error label. Function offsets: `kat` 1,114,902; `Rat` turn-state projection 1,117,504; `zat` activity-state mapping 1,117,783. Static code only; no live row was observed.

### Follow-up: installed desktop transcript selectively promotes tool outputs

The inspected ChatGPT.app bundle is version/build `26.1007.30912` / `20156`; its `app.asar` SHA-256 is `75e397e2280dde93b70a85aa926f4282c728eacc84dfeb15ba7fb6ddd84628af`. In `webview/assets/app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`), the transcript projection helper `u1` converts a `functionCallOutput` to a `userMessage` only when its output is a string, the size predicate allows it, and one of two recognized Codex app/delegation predicates matches. The `codex_app` cases include selected thread-management/delegation operations and automation output; other function-call outputs are returned unchanged by this helper. The projection later filters message-like entries using that conversion, so this is evidence for a selective projection path, not a claim about every surface or every transcript renderer.

This complements the preceding TUI finding without conflating the clients: the installed desktop bundle contains a path that promotes certain recognized tool outputs into message-like transcript items, while generic outputs do not pass through that conversion. It does not prove that every generic output is invisible elsewhere, nor that a specific event was subscribed to or rendered in a live session.

Separately, a bounded exact-string scan of 14,770 packaged JavaScript assets found `rawResponseItem/completed` in only `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` and `page-bootstrap-display.worker-a251533f04e5.js`. The worker copy contains a false-default event map and a subsequent expression that enumerates false entries except a small allowlist; the resulting expression is discarded in a comma-expression in the minified code path inspected. The shared bundle exports a corresponding boolean helper. A cross-chunk consumer is documented in the follow-up below: it gates renderer notification application, but does not establish a transport-level subscription filter or global unsubscription.

The projection helper is at byte offset `4,983,031`; the transcript conversion call is near `4,984,671`, and the message-like predicate is near `5,005,702` in the extracted shared asset. The display worker SHA-256 is `7ed2f925cc86e40042b8e15e7c3ee6ed450a6fdc381f53752910d648f00df69d`. Extraction used the repository-installed `@electron/asar` package. This is static bundle analysis only: no desktop conversation, event stream, or rendered transcript was captured.

### Follow-up: raw-response notification is rejected before renderer application

The shared bundle's `DEt` helper checks whether a method exists in its `AEt` map and returns true only for entries whose value is `true`; `rawResponseItem/completed` is explicitly `false`. In `app-initial-6350d04672d0.js` (SHA-256 `604b5d2de4fbb41f3d0dc5b3662129c558e1c7b8aac8cc87225286f58dcfaf8f`), the shared export `l5t` is imported as `Pze`. The incoming notification callback first records `renderer.notification_receipt` and calls the receipt helper, handles a few explicit special cases, then executes `if(!Pze(e.method)) return` before creating `renderer.notification_apply` and before calling the host implementation's `onNotification(method, params, ...)`. Thus, if a `rawResponseItem/completed` notification reaches this callback, the inspected renderer path records its receipt but returns before forwarding it to the app-server client's normal notification handler.

This resolves the earlier missing-consumer question and narrows the desktop boundary: the inspected renderer has a method-application gate that drops this event before normal notification handling. It still does not show whether the transport subscribed to or received the event in a live session, nor whether another worker or UI path consumes it independently. The app-initial consumer is at byte offset `9,620,708`; its `l5t as Pze` import is near `125,591`. In the shared bundle, `EEt` checks map membership, `DEt` checks the boolean value near `1,930,647`, and map initialization starts near `1,931,117`. The `app.asar` identity and package version are recorded above. This remains static bundle evidence; no runtime notification was captured.

### Follow-up: parent turn history is projected into distinct subagent UI blocks

In the same installed bundle, the local conversation thread hydrates a missing turn with `listThreadTurns`, using `itemsView: "full"` and request source `thread_hydration` (in `local-conversation-thread-c8c5e92ea89e.js`, SHA-256 `ee192ab3f19d493d64580f7690c94b05c589fe59c1757984e1c5a076976a47eb`, around byte offset `297,393`). The thread component imports shared export `u6` as `Fo`; this is the shared turn-to-transcript projector defined as `d1` in `app-shared-8bd9d0399038.js` (SHA-256 `607ad0866dd25ed3764711b4467a9b60708353a599485b5249ff4cbe0e870245`).

The shared projection maps parent-history `collabAgentToolCall` entries to `multi-agent-action` blocks when background subagents are enabled, excluding `wait`; it maps `subAgentActivity` entries to `subagent-activity` blocks under that same feature condition. The transcript predicate likewise accepts those two history item types only when background subagents are enabled. In the local thread's block builder (around offsets `218,300` and `221,747`), a `bem-item` with `presentation: "subagent"` is matched to its `subAgentActivity` by item ID; the builder then creates a synthetic turn containing only activity items for that `agentThreadId`, projects those items, and packages them as a subagent presentation block. For ordinary `codex` presentation segments, the builder selects the segment's item IDs, excludes activity already represented by a child presentation, and projects the remaining items. This shows how parent-turn history is shaped into separate activity cards and ordinary collaboration entries in the transcript model.

The evidence supports projection and block construction, not a captured live render or proof that all UI surfaces display these blocks identically. The app's hydration path may be invoked only when the requested turn is not already loaded; the bundle inspection does not establish which history source populated a particular live conversation. Bundle extraction and app identity are described above; no runtime desktop interaction or test was performed.

The static renderer path can be followed one step further: the `voice-presentation` renderer switches on `presentation.type === "subagent"` and creates component `Sk` (around byte offset `250,588` in that same local thread asset). `Sk` passes the activity items and turn ID to `Ey`, then renders the returned rows and status through a React component. This confirms that the projected subagent presentation is wired to a concrete row-rendering component in this bundle. It remains static wiring evidence; it does not prove the component rendered successfully or establish appearance in a live session.

### Follow-up: disconnected persisted V2 children receive a lifecycle listener

In the later public source snapshot `rust-v0.163.0-alpha.11` (commit [`5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3`](https://github.com/openai/codex/tree/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3)), `try_attach_thread_listener` has a distinct path for an empty initialized-connection list. It waits while the same thread ID is still owned by pending unload cleanup, re-fetches the thread, and starts a lifecycle listener only when the thread is V2 and non-ephemeral. The source comment states the reason for that eligibility boundary: persisted V2 children can reload after idle eviction. Listener startup errors are logged and the operation returns best-effort. This closes a lifecycle gap for a child created after the requesting app-server connection has disconnected; it does not attach a client conversation subscription.

The adjacent app-server lifecycle test `disconnected_v2_child_lifecycle` exercises the disconnected spawn path, forces attachment to wait while a pending unload owns the child ID, then verifies the running child is not closed just because the listener has no subscribers. Its non-ephemeral branch checks eventual registry cleanup and runtime release for both parent and child; its ephemeral branch instead exercises a real parent `followup_task` after the idle deadline and verifies the child's earlier assistant reply remains in the next model input. These are source-test assertions, not results from a test run in this investigation. The behavior is evidence from the later public tag only; the inspected installed ChatGPT Desktop embeds `0.162.0-alpha.17.2`, so this does not establish that the installed build contains this change.
