Sources: [`try_attach_thread_listener`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L3765-L3810) and [`disconnected_v2_child_lifecycle`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/app-server/src/message_processor_thread_lifecycle_tests.rs#L228-L230).


### Follow-up: persisted sender context is captured before turn admission locking

The later `rust-v0.163.0-alpha.11` implementation keeps the unloaded-sender store fallback described in the earlier alpha.9 follow-up, but changes where and how the evidence is prepared. In `turn_input.rs`, `capture_sender_user_messages` now accepts either `ResponseItem` or `AnnotatedResponseItem`, assigns a missing response-item ID, and calls the capture routine before `steer_input` or `start_if_idle` commits the input. The module comment explicitly says sender evidence is read without the active-turn lock and bound to the accepted turn. `pending_turn_input` then renders the prepared fragment into the accepted turn's `CodexHarnessMetadata`; annotated response items with `retain` set also preserve `client_authored` metadata. This separates potentially slow store access from the active-turn admission lock while retaining the delivery identity. Sources: [`capture_sender_user_messages` and `pending_turn_input`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn_input.rs#L915-L994), [`steer_input` call path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn_input.rs#L346-L390), [`start_if_idle` call path](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3/codex-rs/core/src/session/turn_input.rs#L580-L630).

The resulting `GuardianSenderMessages` is documented as a bounded fragment rendered once at admission and never added to the worker prompt. It marks persisted-history provenance and warns that newer unpersisted messages may be missing; its text also says assistant context is untrusted and does not transfer permission. The inspected guardian-context source test asserts that sender context appears in reviewer input and that the delegated worker request excludes the source user's and assistant's quoted instructions. That test exercises an already-loaded source thread; in the inspected tag I did not find a test assertion specifically covering the unloaded-store fallback or the new lock-placement behavior. These are source and test assertions only; no test was run here. Sources: [`GuardianSenderMessages`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/context/guardian_sender_messages.rs#L1-L80), [`reviewer-only test assertions`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/tests/suite/guardian_sender_messages_tests.rs#L153-L189). This later public source snapshot does not establish that the inspected installed Desktop build embeds the same behavior.

### Follow-up: sender evidence is required reviewer context and invalidates stale review authorization

In the same later snapshot, `SenderUserMessagesSection` reads only the latest delivery-bound snapshot from the receiver's `RetainedContext` and contributes its rendered text as a shared Guardian section. Composition gives it its own `sender_user_messages` section ID and places it at the root-conversation context position. The budget test treats that content as required user evidence: optional old tool output is dropped first, while a one-token budget that cannot hold the required sender evidence returns `EvidenceLimitExceeded` instead of silently omitting it. The source test therefore supports a fail-closed budget boundary for this evidence; it does not measure production tokenization or guarantee a particular model request succeeds. Sources: [shared sender section](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/guardian-context/src/sender_user_messages.rs#L13-L26), [section composition](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/guardian-context/src/composition.rs#L226-L231), and [required-evidence budget assertions](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/guardian-context/src/composition_tests.rs#L157-L198).

When that metadata is first recorded into receiver history, `ContextManager` increments `user_message_revision`; the retained store returns the latest delivery and deduplicates by receiver message ID. Guardian captures this revision for a review attempt and checks it again during reviewer execution. If it changed, the review outcome is `StaleAuthorization`; a real cancellation or root-history reset takes precedence and returns `Aborted`. Thus a newly accepted sender delivery cannot leave an in-flight review silently authorized against the prior sender-evidence snapshot. This is the source-level invalidation rule; no concurrent live review was exercised here. Sources: [history capture and revision bump](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/context_manager/history.rs#L583-L596), [latest delivery and deduplication](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/history/src/retained_context.rs#L240-L268), and [review invalidation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/guardian/review_request.rs#L45-L82). No source tests were run in this investigation; the later source tag does not establish that the inspected installed Desktop build includes this behavior.

### Follow-up: stale approval reviews retry on fresh evidence, then fail closed

The caller classifies `StaleAuthorization` as retryable. Each attempt re-reads the receiver's current root authorization/review revisions and user-message revision, then invokes the reviewer with a newly captured `ReviewAuthorization`; the source comment explicitly says a retried verdict must not use the first snapshot. Retries share the original deadline and configured attempt budget, with exponential backoff starting around 200 ms plus jitter. If cancellation wins during the wait, retry returns `Cancelled`; if the deadline is exhausted, it returns `Timeout`. The source retry test asserts that three stale outcomes consume exactly three attempts and that retrying does not extend the deadline. This establishes the retry policy and assertions, not a test run or a live concurrent review. Sources: [per-attempt evidence snapshot](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/guardian/review_request.rs#L200-L282), [retry classification and shared budget](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/retry.rs#L20-L73), [backoff and retryable errors](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/retry.rs#L75-L173), and [stale retry tests](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/retry_tests.rs#L166-L223).

An allow verdict that fails its final authorization recheck is changed to `StaleAuthorization` before publication and returns with `SessionDisposition::Rollback`. The pool marks that trunk for retirement and, when possible, forks only its last committed checkpoint for the next review; the invalidated attempt does not commit its captured conversation checkpoint. If retry attempts finish stale or run out of time, completion reports a failed-closed denial with the rationale that authorization changed before approval could be applied. Stale outcomes are excluded from failed-decision feedback so an obsolete verdict is not recorded as user-facing reviewer guidance. These are source-level control-flow boundaries; no approval was executed. Sources: [post-allow invalidation and checkpoint commit ordering](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/guardian/review_session_setup.rs#L294-L358), [pool rollback to committed snapshot](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/pool.rs#L205-L224) and [rollback retirement](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/pool.rs#L302-L328), [failed-closed completion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/completion.rs#L127-L158), and [stale feedback exclusion](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/ext/guardian-reviewer/src/feedback.rs#L74-L83). This later public source snapshot does not establish that the installed Desktop build implements the same behavior.

### Follow-up: close_agent closes one persisted edge, then shuts down loaded descendants

In `rust-v0.163.0-alpha.11`, `close_agent` marks the requested target's persisted spawn edge `Closed` before invoking `shutdown_agent_tree`. The latter snapshots descendants from `list_live_thread_spawn_edges`, which enumerates currently loaded, non-internal threads from each session's `ThreadSpawn` source; it then calls `shutdown_live_agent` on the requested target and each captured descendant. That shutdown removes the runtime thread, registry membership, V2 residency, and mailbox entry, but does not itself mark the descendant's persisted edge closed. Consequently, source supports explicit closure of the requested edge and shutdown of descendants visible in the loaded in-memory tree; it does not support the broader claim that every historical or unloaded descendant edge is recursively closed. The descendant list is captured before shutdown, and this is not evidence of a persistent graph-wide cascade. Sources: [`close_agent` and shutdown tree](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/legacy.rs#L7-L119), [`loaded-thread edge enumeration`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/thread_manager.rs#L1718-L1736), and [`descendant traversal`](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/runtime_context.rs#L70-L137). Static source analysis only; no close/reload runtime experiment was performed.

### Follow-up: closed V2 descendants are excluded from runtime restoration, but remain in thread-tree queries

In `rust-v0.163.0-alpha.11`, resuming a root V2 thread invokes `restore_v2_agent_metadata`, which asks the graph store for descendants with `ThreadSpawnEdgeStatus::Open`. The persistent store's status-filtered recursive traversal applies `Open` to each edge, so a closed edge also cuts off descendants below it for this restoration query. This restores identity metadata only for open descendants; it does not itself reopen their runtimes. The app-server's direct resume path recognizes a V2 `ThreadSpawn` session as owner-controlled and delegates loading to `ensure_multi_agent_v2_child_loaded`; that path requires the child to already have runtime registry metadata. Thus, after `close_agent` has shut down a child and its edge is closed, resuming the root does not restore that child into the V2 agent registry, and direct resume of that child cannot load it through the normal owner-controlled path. The app-server reports the child as unavailable for direct resume and directs the caller to resume the parent first or use `thread/read`; however, the open-edge restoration filter means resuming the parent alone does not re-register that closed child in this snapshot. This is static path analysis, not a runtime reproduction.

That does not mean the closed thread disappears from every query. The `thread/list` ancestor relation uses the state store's recursive spawn-edge query without an `Open` status filter, and the administrative subtree-ID helper likewise queries descendants without a status filter. Therefore a closed descendant may remain discoverable in thread-tree listing or be included in subtree-scoped administrative work, subject to the query's ordinary archived and other filters, while being excluded from V2 runtime metadata restoration. “Visible in thread history/tree queries” and “resumable as an owned V2 agent” are separate properties. Sources: [root V2 metadata restoration](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L190-L273), [owner-controlled direct resume](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/thread_processor.rs#L3987-L4035), [child loader registry precondition](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/core/src/agent/control/spawn.rs#L310-L365), [status-filtered graph traversal](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/state/src/runtime/threads.rs#L293-L329), and [thread-list ancestor relation](https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/5425d3e625daa58eea3ad1de27c087eb3b2ffbd3/codex-rs/state/src/runtime/threads.rs#L1240-L1295). No runtime resume experiment was performed; this alpha.11 result does not establish behavior in the installed Desktop build.
